## 0 · The year in one paragraph

🔗 **New to this client? Read §0A first** — it explains the company, every document and what each
one is for, from zero.

A single-shareholder Florida S corporation running general freight trucking, **no W-2 payroll in
2025** ⚠️ *(2024 paid the officer 25,000 — §4 decision 1 is open)*, no QuickBooks and no bookkeeper —
the books arrive once a year as the firm's own spreadsheet template, filled in by the client. 2025 revenue is up about 23% on 2024 while the
client's own profit line collapses to near nothing, and **the collapse is at least partly an
artifact**: one expense line's own label says it includes loan principal. ⚠️ **How much of the fall it
explains is not established** — depreciation also dropped and a home-office allocation was newly
booked. The year also carries six equipment additions, four disposals — 🆕 **a fifth was ruled on
2026-09-25** *(decision 33)* — including **a truck destroyed and settled by insurance**, personal cars moving through the
business account, and a home-office allocation computed the way a sole proprietor would.

## 0A · 🔑 ORIENTATION — the company and the document set, for someone who has never seen a trucking return

_(Added 2026-09-14 at Lilian's request: **"una explicación de cero para una persona que no tiene idea
de quién es este cliente… no sé nada de compras y ventas de camiones… qué documentos se utilizan y
para qué se utilizan."**)_

ⓘ **A Spanish-language HTML companion to this section was delivered to Lilian on 2026-09-14** —
§0A ①-③ plus her fifteen questions answered, with note fields. ⛔ **Delivered as a FILE, not published
as an artifact**: this is phase-1 review material *(CLAUDE.md — phase 1's review output is never an
artifact)*, and it carries organizer-sourced facts, which the unsettled [`double-mcp`](../../../.claude/skills/double-mcp/)
§2.2 limit also keeps off a hosted page. **And it is not in the repo either** — ⛔ *not* because it carries figures *(this folder is the one place that holds them)*, but because [the folder's README](../README.md) sanctions **one** rendered file per return, the reviewer briefing, and this is not that.

> 🛑 **NOTHING IN THIS SECTION IS A SOURCE. Every row is a POINTER, and where it differs from the
> section it points at, THE SECTION GOVERNS.** ⚠️ **This was written once without that rule and the
> review found five places where simplifying had dropped a hedge** — including two conclusions other
> reviews had already withdrawn. **A summary written for a novice is the easiest place in this file to
> re-introduce an error.**

### ① The business in five facts

| | |
|---|---|
| **What it does** | **General freight trucking, local** — activity code **484110** *(§3A)*. It owns tractors and trailers and hauls other people's goods. ⚠️ **How it is PAID is not established** — §3E ⑤ found the two largest payers arriving under **payroll-style ACH captions**, and records that *"customer"* is the natural reading, **not a quotation** |
| **Who owns it** | **ONE shareholder, 100%.** A Florida **S corporation** since 2011, so the company pays no federal income tax itself: the profit reaches the owner's 1040 on a **Schedule K-1** |
| **Who works there** | **No W-2 payroll in 2025**, per the organizer — **and the drivers are paid as contractors.** 🔴 **This is NOT settled background: 2024 DID pay the officer 25,000** *(§3A)*, and *"2025 at zero is a change that has to be explained."* **§4 decision 1 is OPEN and it is the return's largest exposure.** ⚠️ **The 1099 side is ANSWERED on WHO and OPEN on whether anything was TRANSMITTED** *(§3AB, 2026-09-25)* — five of the six drivers have a 2025 form on file and the sixth is the one he flagged, so **his two organizer answers were never in conflict**; ⛔ **no Form 1096 or acknowledgement has been seen**, and payees who are not drivers are a separate open population *(§3E ④)* |
| **Where the office is** | **The company's operating address is the owner's home** *(§3H ⑤)* — which is what makes the home-office question real *(§3D)*. ⛔ **"There is no premises anywhere" is NOT a claim this file has tested**, and §3H ⑤ records a **third lender writing to the company in Chicago, IL**, with *"which states"* still open |
| **How the books are kept** | 🔴 **No QuickBooks, no bookkeeper.** Once a year the client fills in **the firm's own spreadsheet template**. **That single fact drives most of this paper** |

### ② The three questions any 1120-S has to answer — and where this client stands

**A company return is not one thing; it is three, and they come from different places.**

| | The question | Where it lives | This client |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **What came in and what went out this year?** | **page 1** — the income statement | 🟠 **The SOURCE exists and is not verified against anything** *(§4 decision 7)*. ⛔ **But page 1 is NOT ready:** §2 lists two unavoidable entries, and §3B marks the 102,834 equipment line **"CANNOT GO ANYWHERE until split"**, the profit **"not the taxable figure"** and depreciation as coming from Form 4562, not from the client's cell. 🔑 **Julia's own decision 8 requires that split** |
| **2** | **What does the company OWN and OWE on 31 December?** | **Schedule L** — the balance sheet | ⛔ **Broken.** `#REF!` errors, **zero fixed assets** on a company running a fleet, and **no equity section at all** |
| **3** | **What did the OWNER put in and take out?** | **Schedules M-2 and K-1** | ⛔ **Nothing.** There is no equity section for it to come from |

### ③ The documents — what each one IS, and what a tax preparer does with it

**Grouped by the job it does, not by who sent it.** ⚠️ *Where a row states a general accounting or
legal proposition it is stated from knowledge, not read off a form — the same marking §3C, §3D and
§3F carry.*

| Document | What it physically is | What it is FOR on the return |
|---|---|---|
| **The tax organizer** | A questionnaire in Double the client answers online | The **yes/no facts** — states, employees, 1099s, accounting method. ⚠️ **Its silence is not evidence**: it is conditional, so a wrong answer hides the follow-up questions. ⓘ *This client's is **75% complete and never submitted** (§1 row 4)* |
| **The books** *(`Balance Sheet & P&L Templates.xlsx`)* | The firm's spreadsheet, filled in by the client | **The income statement** — ⓘ *and more: the same workbook carries the **2025 asset list** (§3C), an **older 2023 asset list** (all of §3G) and the broken balance-sheet tab* |
| **The home-office worksheet** | A separate spreadsheet the client fills in | **The allocation behind the home-office figure** *(§3D)* — one of the three positions Julia must settle |
| **Bank statements** *(twelve monthly PDFs)* | The checking account, month by month | ⚠️ **NOT the income statement.** They give the **cash balance** at each year end, and they are **the main window** on what the owner took out. ⛔ **Not the only one and not a complete one:** §3E ④ says **not one counterparty is ESTABLISHED as owner-facing**, §3E ⑥ says a bare wire or a check *"matches no string and is invisible to this method"*, and §3J makes the card summary a second window |
| **Credit-card year-end summary** | The issuer's annual recap, itemised by category and merchant | **What the card BOUGHT** — so business spending can be separated from personal. ⛔ **It does NOT give a balance**, which is what Schedule L needs *(§3J ⓪)* |
| 🔑 **Interest letter / *"tax breakout letter"*** | A one-page January letter from a lender | **THE MOST MISUNDERSTOOD DOCUMENT HERE.** A truck payment is **part interest, part principal**. **Interest is deductible; principal is NOT** — it repays a debt, and the truck is deducted separately through depreciation. **This letter splits them, and states the loan balance at 31 December.** ⚠️ **Not the only route** — §3H ② names the contracts' own amortization schedules. ✅ 🆕 **CORRECTED 2026-09-15 — BOTH CLAIMS THIS CELL USED TO MAKE WERE INVERTED.** ✅ **The three 2025 letters we hold ARE BMO's, all three** *(§4 decision 21 — Lilian opened them)*. ✅ **The two AP Equipment notes need no letter: they are COMPUTED from the notes themselves** *(§3U ⑨)*. ✅ **The Audi is a LEASE — no principal, no interest, no letter, ever** *(§3U ⑥)*. 🔴 **Still open: IPFS only.** ⛔ 🆕 **CORRECTED 2026-09-27 — three of the four this cell listed are CLOSED and the fourth was never needed: Mitsubishi by its own year-end email (decision 26), the Fifth Third line of credit by its December statement (decision 31), the SBA by its own statements (decision 23), and Financial Pacific by the caption search in our own statements (§3AD ②, decision 50).** ⛔ **Ask none of them for anything.** _(As written: Still open: IPFS, Mitsubishi, the Fifth Third line of credit, the SBA, and a Financial Pacific balance letter.)_ *(§3U ⑪ — the canonical list)*. ⛔ **AND FINANCIAL PACIFIC IS NO LONGER "the exception and the model": §3U ⑤ finds nine payments where its schedule assumes twelve, so its 3,198.13 and 18,837.47 may NOT be keyed until a balance letter arrives.** _(As written:)_ ⛔ **Only three 2025 ones exist here** — none for BMO ×3, both AP Equipment notes, the Audi lease, IPFS, the credit line or the SBA. ✅ **Financial Pacific is the exception and the model:** its own 2024 letters plus its amortization schedule settle both its contracts *(§3H ③ter, ③ter-bis)* |
| 🔑 **Secured promissory note / finance contract** | The loan agreement signed at purchase | **Proves the terms** — amount financed, rate, number of payments — and **the VIN of the exact vehicle**. ⚠️ **It proves the date of the NOTE, not of the purchase:** §3H ③bis records that *"a truck bought in 2023 and **refinanced** in 2025 produces exactly this document"* |
| 🔑 **Retail purchase agreement / dealer invoice** | The sales document from the truck dealer | **Proves what the asset COST and when it was PLACED IN SERVICE** — the two inputs depreciation is built from. ⚠️ **A loan amount is NOT a cost**: cash or a trade-in may have gone to the dealer on top |
| 🔑 **Sale agreement** *(for something sold)* | The sales document, the other way round | **Proves the PROCEEDS.** Gain or loss = proceeds − *(cost − depreciation already taken)*. ⛔ **No document, no COMPUTABLE gain — the disposal is still reportable**, and §6 keeps Form 4797 open regardless |
| 🔑 **Insurance settlement letter** *(for something destroyed)* | The insurer's letter stating what it paid | **The proceeds figure for a total loss** — the sale agreement's equivalent when there is no buyer *(§4 decision 9)* |
| **The prior-year filed return** | Last year's finished 1120-S | 🔑 **The answer key.** The **opening balance sheet**, the **depreciation schedule every asset continues on**, and **the firm's own conventions** |

### ④ Why the truck documents matter more here than anywhere else

**A truck is not an expense. It is an asset**, deducted a piece at a time over several years
*(depreciation)*. To do that the return needs, **for every single vehicle**: what it cost · **when it
was placed in service** · how much has been deducted so far · and, when it leaves, what it sold for.

🔴 **And on this client none of that can be matched by NAME.** §3G ① establishes **two** naming
systems the client uses *(a description list and a unit-number list)* and **a third on the returns**
*(`#7107` / `#1800` / `HU4577`)*. ✅ 🆕 **ANSWERED 2026-09-25 — §3AC ④: `1838` is the `2022 Frght Cascadia`, asset #7 on the 2024 return, and §6's row for it is TICKED.** _(As written:)_ ⚠️ **A possible fourth — `1838` on an interest letter — is flagged
by §3H ① as *"a fourth naming system, OR A TYPO. Ask,"* and is still open in §6.**

🔑 **The finance documents supply the VIN, which is why §3H ③ says the mapping should be asked for
against VINs rather than descriptions.** ⛔ **But a VIN is not a key all parties share — §3I ⑦
establishes that NEITHER FILED RETURN CARRIES ONE.** *(An earlier draft said it was; withdrawn.)*

### ⑤ What 2024 did — a POINTER TABLE, not a set of instructions

🛑 **Read the header rule above before this table. "What 2024 did" is NOT the same as "what 2025
should do"** — and for three of these rows the section it points at says the opposite.

| | What 2024 did | Does it carry to 2025? |
|---|---|---|
| The expense base | Almost everything to **page 1 line 20** with an itemised statement, not the named lines *(§3A, §3A-bis)* | ✅ **Yes — §4 decision 4** |
| Meals | **Half added back** on Schedule M-1 *(§3A-bis)* | ⚠️ **Decision 6, and it is CLAUDE's, not Julia's** — and §3A-bis ④-bis carries a live objection: **entertainment is disallowed in full, not half**, and DOT meals sit at a different rate. **Ask before repeating it on a year 3.4× larger** |
| The home office | 🔴 **EXCLUDED ENTIRELY**, proved by two exact checks *(§3A-bis ②)* | 🔴 **Julia's — decision 2** |
| New equipment | **§179 elected at ZERO**, and an **election OUT of bonus depreciation** attached *(§3A, §3A-bis ④)* | 🔴 **Decision 3 is open** · ☑️ **the election-out must be RE-ATTACHED — it is annual** |
| The owner's money | **K-1 113,157 · M-2 line 7 51,988 · paid-in capital (61,169)**, and `113,157 − 51,988 = 61,169` exactly *(§3I ③)* | ⛔ **NO.** §3A forbids stating **the mechanism** as what the preparer did — *"the identity is exact; the mechanism is inferred."* 🛑 **And §3I ③ calls the 2024 presentation a candidate DEFECT to put to the signer, not a house method** — while **§5C-v gate 4 FAILS, so 2025 is prepared GROSS** |
| Cash on the balance sheet | **One bank account** *(§3I ②)* | ✅ **YES — but by RULING, not by analogy.** §3I ② is titled *"why it is NOT yet a convention to copy"* and its objection stands: **copying a presentation cannot cure an ownership question.** 🔑 **Lilian OVERRODE it on 2026-09-14** — *only the accounts the 2024 return reported are the company's* — so cash is **5,788.50 unconditionally** *(§3K, §4 decision 11)*. ⚠️ **The objection travels to Julia WITH the decision** *(§3K ⑤, decision 11a)* |
| The debt | **One aggregate number**, no breakdown *(§3I ④)* | ⚠️ **Only the PRESENTATION.** §3I ④: *"what the analogy saves is a Schedule L statement, not the collection work"* |
| Disposals | **No capitalised asset left the 2024 schedule** — gross depreciable assets rose by exactly the two additions *(§3I ⑥)* | ⛔ **Nothing to copy — but state it as §3I ⑥ does.** *"No 4797 was ever filed"* is **NOT established** *(pages 15 and 18 produce no text)*, *"2023 disposed of nothing"* needs **the 2022 schedule nobody has read**, and **an uncapitalised disposal is invisible to this check while still needing a 4797** |

🔑 **That last row is still why §4 decision 9 had to come from Julia rather than from the prior
year** — there is **no VISIBLE prior-year method** for a disposal.

## 2 · Adjusting entries made before preparing

**None yet, and two are unavoidable before any figure is final:**

1. 🔴 **Split the equipment line.** `Heavy haul financed equipment paymnets incld interest and
   principal` — **102,834** — against the finance schedules. **Principal is not deductible**; the
   interest belongs with the existing interest line. Until this is done the client's expense total
   and profit are both wrong, and **the profit is UNDERSTATED, not overstated.**
2. 🔴 **Rebuild the balance sheet.** The client's sheet carries `#REF!` in net income, total equity
   and the balance check, shows **every fixed-asset line as zero** against a fleet of a dozen units,
   and has **no equity section at all**.

## 3 · The forms, line by line

### 🔑 3A · FIRST — the 2024 return as the answer key

**The method: reproduce last year from last year's books before touching this year.** Every row
here is read off the filed 2024 return and is a **convention to repeat**, not a figure to reuse.

| Page 1 line 1c gross receipts | 1,044,320 | |

| Lines 9–12, 16–19 (repairs, rents, taxes, advertising…) | **all blank in the extracted text** ⚠️ *(column alignment is unreliable — §1)* | 🔑 **THE CONVENTION THAT MATTERS: almost the entire expense base goes to line 20** |

| Form 4562 line 14 bonus | blank in the extracted text | ✅ **EXPLAINED — §3A-bis ④: the firm ELECTED OUT**, on an `Elections` page. Not an omission, a deliberate position, and **the election is annual** |

| Schedule L total equity at 12/31/2024 | 1,000 − 61,169 + 0 = **(60,169)** | 🔴 **Negative equity, and the AAA at zero** |

ZERO** and **shareholder equity opens NEGATIVE at (60,169)**. Any 2025 distribution therefore has

**his Form 7203 with his 1040, not on this return** — stands between it and a **§1368(b)(2) capital
gain**.

### 🔑 3A-bis · THE ANALOGY TABLE — what 2024 DID with each line, and what 2025 does with it

🔴 **This is the most valuable thing in this paper, and it comes from ONE page of the filed return**
that the first pass never opened: **page 16, the `Line 20 (1120S) — Other Deductions` statement**,
itemised caption by caption. With it, every book line maps to what the return actually did.

> ⚠️ **Four of the sixteen captions came through the extraction as `[GLYPH]`** — their values are
> unreadable. **They are recovered by arithmetic, not by reading**, and the arithmetic is exact: see
> the reconciliation below.

#### ① The reconciliation — books → return, and it ties to the dollar

**Source of the 2024 book figures below: the client's own `Income Statement (2024)` sheet** in the
same workbook *(§1 source 1)* — **not** the return. The 19 lines on that sheet total 973,895.08 to the
cent. ⚠️ **The 2024 return was re-opened on 2026-09-13 to read page 16**, after the first pass's
redacted text had been deleted.

```
2024 book expenses (19 lines)                            973,895.08
  less Interest            (own line, page 1 line 13)    -12,110.08
  less Depreciation        (books; return uses 4562)     -54,000.00
  = base carried to the line 20 statement                907,785.00
  less meals disallowed    (50% — M-1 add-back)           -5,909.00
  🔴 less Office lease/house,utility                      -7,951.00
  = 893,925  ←  EXACTLY the return's line 20              ✅
```

#### ①-bis 🔑 THE INDEPENDENT CHECK — and it is the one that earns the conclusion

⚠️ **The reconciliation above proves only that the 7,951 is not inside LINE 20.** It says nothing
about lines 9–12 or 16–19, and ⛔ **reading those as blank is exactly what §1 forbids.** The check
that closes them uses two operands neither reconciliation touches:

```
gross receipts                 1,044,320
 − officer compensation           25,000   (page 1 line 7)
 − interest                       12,110   (page 1 line 13)
 − depreciation                   35,861   (page 1 line 14)
 − other deductions              893,925   (page 1 line 20)
 = 77,424  =  page 1 line 22, exactly           ✅
```

🔑 **Page 1 closes to the dollar with no room for another deduction line.** Had the 7,951 been taken
anywhere on page 1, line 22 would read **69,473**. **That — not a blank cell — is what establishes
that it was not deducted.**

🔑 **And a third, free corroboration, from the M-1:** the client's own 2024 book income is
`1,044,320 − 973,895.08 − 25,000` = **45,424.92**, while Schedule M-1 line 1 as filed is **71,515** —
a gap of **26,090.08**, against the depreciation difference *(54,000 − 35,861 = 18,139)*
**plus the home office (7,951) = 26,090** — ⚠️ **a residual of 8 cents, which is the interest
rounding** *(the books carry 12,110.08, the return 12,110)*. **Not exact, and close enough to be
evidence rather than coincidence.** ✅ **So 2024's M-1 line 1 was built from a TAX-ADJUSTED book column,
not from the client's books**, and it carries neither reconciling item on its face.
🔑 **That also answers §3A's open flag** about the missing depreciation add-back: the adjustment was
made **before** the M-1, inside the book-income figure, rather than on it.

**And the four unreadable captions — their TOTAL is pinned, their individual values are not:** the twelve readable captions sum to
**847,972** *(⚠️ with meals at the NET 5,908, not the gross 11,817 — re-adding the table's own column
gives 853,881)*, the statement totals **893,925**, so the masked rows carry **45,953** — and the four
book lines not yet accounted for *(Agents/Crossdock 19,800 · IFTA/HUT/Permits 10,058 · Heavy haul
equipment 12,100 · TRT Wash 3,995)* sum to **45,953 exactly**.

✅ **What that DOES establish, and it is enough:** no subset of those four plus 7,951 also sums to
45,953, so **the home office cannot be hiding inside the masked group.** That question is closed.
⛔ **What it does NOT establish is which masked row holds which value** — any four numbers totalling
45,953 satisfy the arithmetic. **Three of the four are matched to their book lines because the
caption NAMES were readable**, which is a reading, not arithmetic.
⚠️ **And ①-bis, not this, is the independent confirmation** — ① and ② are algebraically the same
equation stated twice.

#### ② 🔴 THE HOME OFFICE — the answer, and it is not what the books do

🔑 🆕 **FIRST, BECAUSE LILIAN ASKED THE RIGHT QUESTION ON 2026-09-27 — *"si fue excluido, ¿cómo sabes
entonces que fueron 7,951?"* — THE TWO FACTS COME FROM TWO DIFFERENT DOCUMENTS, and that is the whole
answer:**

| The fact | Which document | How it says so |
|---|---|---|
| **The AMOUNT was 7,951** | 🔑 **the CLIENT's own 2024 Profit & Loss** — his book line `Office lease/house,utility` | **He wrote it. It is his bookkeeping, not the return** |
| **It was EXCLUDED** | 🔑 **the FILED 2024 RETURN** | **Page 1 closes to the dollar WITHOUT it: 1,044,320 − 25,000 − 12,110 − 35,861 − 893,925 = 77,424, exactly line 22.** ⛔ **Had the 7,951 been deducted, line 22 would read 69,473** |

✅ **So nothing is being read off a return that does not carry it.** **His books say he spent it; the return
says the firm did not deduct it; and the arithmetic is what makes "did not deduct it" a measurement rather
than an impression.** ⚠️ **What the return CANNOT say is where the 7,951 was debited instead** — it fell out
of the expense base before the M-1, **so a distribution, a receivable from the shareholder and officer
compensation all look identical from the outside.** _(The rest of this subsection is as written.)_

**2024 did NOT deduct it.** The book line `Office lease/house,utility` — **7,951** — **is not in the
line 20 statement** *(reconciliation ①, exact)* **and was not taken on any other page-1 deduction
line** *(check ①-bis: page 1 closes to the dollar without it; with it, line 22 would be 69,473)*.
⚠️ **Page 1 line 11, Rents, also reads blank in the extracted text** — that is consistent with the
above but is **not** the evidence, since §1 bars reading an absence off this extraction.
**It was removed from the expense base**, and both checks are exact only because it was.

| | 2024 | 2025 |
|---|---|---|
| Book line `Office lease/house,utility` | **7,951** | **27,248** — **3.4× higher** |
| Separate home-office worksheet from the client | **none found** — no 2024 organizer or worksheet is in §1's source list, and none was searched for | **yes** — `Home Office Deduction … .xlsx`, ⅓ of the whole home |
| **What the return did with it** | 🔴 **EXCLUDED — not deducted** | **The analogy says: exclude it** |

🔑 **So the analogy answer is: take the 27,248 out of the expense base, exactly as 2024 took the
7,951 out.**

🔴 **BUT "exclude it" IS ONLY HALF AN ANSWER, AND THE OTHER HALF IS NOT IN THIS PAPER YET.** The
company actually paid that money; a debit that stops being an expense has to land somewhere —
**a distribution**, **a receivable from the shareholder**, or **officer compensation**. ⛔ **Nothing
establishes which, and the choice is not cosmetic:** the AAA opens at **zero** and equity at
**(60,169)** *(§3A)*, so calling it a distribution walks straight into the §1368(b)(2) exposure, and
**27,248 of §6's open "how much was distributed in 2025" may be exactly this.**
⚠️ **2024's treatment of its own 7,951 is not visible either** — it fell out of the expense base
before the M-1, and where it was debited is not readable from the return. **Ask her.** ⚠️ **But say what changed, because it is not a like-for-like repeat:** 2024's figure was
small and had no worksheet behind it; 2025's is **three and a half times larger** and the client has
now built a **Form 8829-style allocation of the entire home, including his mortgage interest and
property taxes**. **He is clearly expecting it to be deducted.** ⛔ **So this is still Julia's call,
not an automatic repeat** — but the firm's own prior-year position is *out*, and that is the
strongest evidence in the file.

#### ③ The caption map — the 2025 statement writes itself

**The client's book captions are NOT what went on the return.** This is the renaming convention:

⚠️ **The `2024` column mixes three bases — read the row, not the column:** a **statement caption
value** *(most rows)*, an **arithmetic-recovered book value** *(the four marked `recovered`)*, a **book
value that never reached the return** *(the office lease)*, and two **page-1 figures** *(interest,
depreciation)*.

| The client's book line | → 2024 statement caption | 2024 | **2025 book figure** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fuel | **8 Fuel** | 245,200 | 348,129 |
| **Driver Salary** | 🔑 **11 Outside Services** *(renamed — they are contractors, not wages)* | 337,534 | 447,364 |
| Auto Repairs & Maintenance | **2 Automobile and truck expenses** | 107,689 | 54,032 |
| Insurance | **3 Insurance** | 67,231 | 79,963 |
| Tolls | **6 Tolls** | 25,402 | 46,801 |
| Lumper fees | **9 Lumper fees** | 23,650 | 10,420 |

#### ④ Depreciation — the convention is explicit, and it is an ELECTION with a statement

**Page 20 of the 2024 return is an `Elections` page:**

> *"Pursuant to IRC Section 168(k)(2)(D)(iii), the Taxpayer elects out of first-year special
> depreciation for all **3-Year** depreciable property placed in service during the current tax
> year."* — **and the same election for 5-Year property.**

🔑 **So there was no bonus because the firm ELECTED OUT, deliberately, with an attached statement.**
⚠️ **The election out of bonus is made annually, by property class, on a timely filed return — so if
2025 repeats it the statement must be attached again, and if it is not attached bonus applies by
default.** ⛔ **Stated from knowledge and NOT read off the 2025 instructions or the Code — verify
before relying on it**, and note the quoted cite is the software's pre-TCJA wording. **The 2025 bonus
rate also has to be looked up**, because "bonus applies by default" is a different number this year.
**This is the kind of attachment that blocks e-file when it is missing** *(delivery rule 6)*.

🛑 **And rule 6 says DRAFT it, so here it is — English, plain ASCII, ready to paste**, with the
classes left to be confirmed against 2025's own additions *(2024's statement named 3-year and 5-year
only, which was enough because all eight assets were 5-year)*:

```
ELECTION OUT OF FIRST-YEAR SPECIAL DEPRECIATION ALLOWANCE

Pursuant to IRC Section 168(k)(7), the Taxpayer elects out of the additional
first-year depreciation deduction for all classes of property placed in
service during the tax year ended December 31, 2025.

Taxpayer: ZAKOM INCORPORATED
```

⚠️ **The section number above is 168(k)(7) as the current election-out provision and is NOT
verified** — read it off the 2025 Form 4562 instructions before the statement is attached.

**And the asset convention is uniform** — page 19's `Summary of Unadjusted Basis of Qualified
Property`, 8 assets totalling **536,259**, **every one of them 5-year property at 100.00% business
use**. ✅ **Two free corroborations that the extraction read this page correctly:** the first six
assets sum to **383,709**, which is §3A's opening depreciable-assets figure to the dollar, and the
two 2024 additions sum to **152,550**, which is Form 4562 line 2 to the dollar.

| Asset | In service | Life | Cost | Business use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AUDI | 2/1/2021 | 5.0 | 50,020 | **100%** |
| FRT'18 (HU4577) | 5/15/2020 | 5.0 | 46,000 | 100% |

🔑 **The AUDI is on the schedule at 100% business use**, and page 17 *(Form 4562 Part V Section B)*

itself: the treatment is on it, whoever decided it.**
⚠️ **2025 has a NEW Audi on a lease and a Porsche the client calls personal**, so the ruling does not

#### ④-bis ⚠️ Two questions the meals split inherits without examining

**The arithmetic is clean — 2024's `5,909 + 5,908 = 11,817` and 2025's `6,857 + 6,857 = 13,714` both
tie** *(2024 rounded the odd figure up on the disallowed side; 2025's is even, so both halves are
exact)*. **The RATE is what nobody has examined:**

1. **The book caption is `Dining & entertainment`, and 2024's M-1 label was `Travel and
   entertainment`.** ⛔ **Entertainment is 100% disallowed, not 50%.** A 50/50 split assumes the line
   holds no entertainment at all. **Ask him before repeating it on a year 3.4× larger.**
2. **This is a general freight trucking company**, and meals of individuals subject to **DOT
   hours-of-service** limits sit at a different percentage from the general 50%. ⛔ **Whether any of
   the 13,714 qualifies is a question** — and one to settle off the 2025 instructions, not from
   memory.

#### ⑤ What the analogy CANNOT settle

| | Why |
|---|---|
| **The 102,834 equipment line** | 2024's book equivalent was **12,100**, and it reached the statement in one of the four masked rows — ⛔ **whose caption is uncertain (`13 TRL Rent`, or an unnamed row) and whose value was never read.** So **what 2024 did with it is NOT established**, and there is no prior-year treatment to copy |
| **Distributions** | 2024's K-1 figure exceeded what the M-2 could absorb, and the excess **equals the negative paid-in capital to the dollar** — ⚠️ **the identity is exact, the mechanism is inferred** *(§3A, which forbids stating it as what the preparer did)*. **The AAA now opens at zero**, so 2025 has no cushion at all; ⛔ **but there is no "only works once" rule — the real constraint is the shareholder's stock basis** *(§6)* |
| **The disposals and the casualty** | **No Form 4797 appears in the extracted text of 2024**, and pages 15 and 18 barely extracted — ⚠️ **so there may be no precedent, or there may be one nobody has read.** Confirm off the PDF *(§3C says the same)* |
| **Officer compensation** | 2024 paid 25,000; whether 2025 repeats it is a decision, not a convention |
| **The 2025 states** | 2024's return as extracted shows no state return; **pages 15 and 18 barely extracted**, so that is not evidence either way |

It is the company paying its drivers — operating cost, not owner-facing.** ⚠️ **A reading, not a match** *(106,565.70 apart, 194 payments against 364 rows, nothing traced one to one)*. _(As written:)_ **nobody knows what it is.** The pattern reads like the offsetting leg of an ACH origination *(i.e. the company's own payments to drivers and vendors)* — ⛔ **but that is a guess, and a million dollars is not a line to guess at.** **Ask the bank or the client.** ⚠️ Recorded here rather than left out: [§4A-M](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/) requires the scan to be reported **even when it finds nothing**, and this is the opposite of nothing |

### ⑤ Money IN — who actually pays this company

🔑 **The mirror scan quantifies the two big payers, which the first draft could not:**

| Payer | Credits | Gross IN | Debits back to them |
|---|---|---|---|
| **OWL LAND OF ILLINOIS** | 46 | **574,516.65** | **none** |
| 🔴 **USA FLORIDA FREIGHT** | 35 | **434,132.39** | 🔑 **none — so it is NOT a loan being repaid** |
| Everything else | 87 | **328,505.11** | — |
| **Total in** | 168 | **1,337,154.15** | |

🔴 **So the P&L is wrong about who pays this company.** OWL Land is **43%** of the money in, not the
business. And:

- 🔴 **`USA FLORIDA FREI` — 35 deposits, 434,132.39**, captioned `PAYROLL` and `DIRECT DEP`, many
  between 13,000 and 26,300, running all year. ⛔ **THE ASSET SHEET CALLS THIS A LOAN** — *"Business
  loan from USA Florida Freight LLC of $22,000 borrowed and paid off in 2025"*. 🔑 **The scan found
  NOT ONE debit back to that name all year**, so nothing was repaid to it — **a 22,000 loan borrowed
  and repaid is not what this account shows.** ⚠️ **What the bank actually says is that 434,132.39
  arrived from an entity of that name under payroll-style ACH captions. It does not say "customer"** —
  that is the natural reading, not a quotation. **Ask the client what the relationship is; 434,132
  against a reported revenue of 1,289,315 is a third of the top line.**
- ⚠️ **`Received RTP … from Robinhood Securities` — 13,035** on 27 October. **A brokerage transfer into
  the operating account.** ⛔ **What it IS has not been established** — a contribution, a company
  brokerage account nobody has mentioned, or something else. **One caption is not a characterisation.**
- **`HUNTINGTON BP MERCH PMT`** — 15,000 (18 Nov) and 13,216 (23 Dec).
- **Plain `DEPOSIT` with no counterparty** — including **19,500 on 21 August**. 🔑 **The asset sheet
  says the 2019 Audi was "traded in for $19,500"** — ⚠️ **the amounts match, but a trade-in credit is
  normally applied against the new vehicle, not deposited.** Ask.

### ⑥ What this DOES and DOES NOT give the capital account

✅ **What it settles:** opening and closing cash, both verified; that the twelve statements are one
complete, consecutive account; that **every debit and credit LINE was captured** *(the parsed sums
equal the statements' own printed totals)*; and **a list of every movement matching the counterparty
strings that were searched.** ⛔ **Not "every movement" — a distribution posted as a bare wire, or
as one of the check lines, matches no string and is invisible to this method.**

⛔ **What it CANNOT settle, and why the capital account is still not computable:**

1. **The other four accounts** — gross traffic both ways, **47,884.46 out and 51,231.00 back**, with no statements *(③)*. ⛔ ~~**The scan says sweep, not withdrawal** — but their balances may still be company cash.~~ 🆕 **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-14 — §3K ③(c): the accounts are not the company's, so it is not a sweep and the two sides do not net. Their balances are NOT company cash.**
2. **The Amex** — **123,786.73 of debits, and the scan found no Amex credits at all** *(no refunds, no returned payments)*. ✅ **The card's own charges are now known — §3J** — ⛔ **but they do not reconcile to the payments** *(§3J ④)*.
3. 🔴 **The `ZAKOM 7807` offsets — 997,566.98 net, unexplained** *(④)*.
4. **A bank statement never says WHY.** A transfer out is a distribution, a loan repayment, or a
   payment to a supplier who banks at Fifth Third. ⛔ **Only the client can say which.**

🔑 **The honest summary, with its arithmetic shown** *(an earlier draft said "roughly 185,000" with
no derivation, and it double-counted ③ against ④)*:

| Candidate for the capital account | Amount | Why it is only a candidate |
|---|---|---|
| Amex payments | **123,786.73** | ✅ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-14 — §3J.** The 2025 Year-End Summary itemises **111,231.98** of charges. ⛔ **The row stays in the range**: the payments still do not reconcile to the charges *(§3J ④)*, so it is not established that all 39 went to this card |
| Broward County | **14,140.18** | 🔑 **Purpose still unestablished** — §3G ② corroborates the home reading without settling it, and **leaves the fleet-registration alternative live**. The row stays in the range |
| Other-account transfers | ⛔ ~~**(3,346.54) net**~~ → **47,884.46 GROSS out** | 🆕 **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-14 — §3K ③(c).** ~~The scan says sweep~~ — the ruling removes the sweep premise, so the **gross** figure is the one that enters, and 51,231.00 came back from an outside party |
| **Bounded range** | ⛔ **WITHDRAWN 2026-09-14 — §3K ③(d)** | **The ≈ 0 floor assumed the other-account transfers netted to nothing and the Amex and Broward were fully business. The account-inventory ruling removes the first and two NEW rows enter — the 2025 payments to the Fifth Third card and to Macy's, NEITHER of which has a dollar total anywhere in this repo.** 🛑 **The range is not replaced by a smaller one; it is not computable until those two are scanned** |

⛔ **The `ZAKOM 7807` offsets are deliberately excluded from that range** — 997,566.98 unexplained is
not a capital-account candidate until somebody says what it is, and putting it in a range would
imply a judgement nobody has made.

### ⑦ 🔴 2024 READ TOO — the same one account, and TWO counterparties ③'s four do not include

_(Added 2026-09-13, answering "were more accounts uploaded for 2024, and how many does he have?")_

**Eight of the twelve 2024 statements were opened** — May to December; **January to April were
refused by the redactor's guard** *(row 8)*. Every one is the **same** `5/3 BUS ELITE CKG` account
`…7807`, one per month. 🔑 **So 2024 uploaded no second account either: it is one account in both
years, and the firm has never been given a statement for anything else.**

The transfers name their counterparty by the bank's **own type code**, and 2024 carries two that
2025's four do not:

| 2024 counterparty | When | Movements | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CK** (checking) | May · Jun · Nov · Dec | 4 | a second checking account, running in both years |
| **SV** (savings) | Dec | 1 | |
| 🔴 **CL** (credit line) | Nov · Dec | 3 | **the business line of credit — and it STARTS in November 2024**, which is exactly what the client's own `Asset List` says *("Started in Nov … Business line of credit")* and what the statements' service-charge waiver answers *("Business Loan or Line of Credit? Yes")* |
| 🔴 **CC** (credit card) | May | 1 | **a FIFTH THIRD credit card — not the Amex, and not one of ③'s four** |

🔑 **Why CL and CC are read as a line and a card rather than guessed at:** the bank writes the type
itself, and the two extract in the **16-digit** masked form (`XXXXXXXXXXXX####`) while CK and SV
extract in the **10-digit** form (`XXXXXX####`). The card also has a payment history of its own —
`WEB INITIATED PAYMENT AT FIFTH THIRD BANK WEB PAY` is debited **once every month from June 2024
through December 2025**.

⚠️ **Counterparties are counted by distinct masked last-four, so every count here is a FLOOR** — two
accounts sharing a last four would read as one. ⛔ **And Jan–Apr 2024 are unread**, so nothing is
reported absent for those four months.

Every row below is a **debit** in the statements' `Withdrawals / Debits` section. The count pattern

Nobody has re-tested 2024.** _(As read:)_ **2/month** May–Jun, **1/month** from July | ⛔ ~~1/month all year~~ → ✅ **NINE payments of 1,011.71, March–November only** | 🔴 **The thirteen debits that produced the "1/month all year" reading match the string `FPL`, which on this account is FLORIDA POWER & LIGHT. There is NO debit in these statements whose description contains `FINANCIAL PACIFIC` at all.** ✅ **The genuine payments were found by AMOUNT** *(§3U ⑤)* |

## 3F · 🔴 THE ASSETS — 2024's tax schedule against the client's 2025 sheet

> ✅ 🆕 **2026-09-25 — DECISION 33 RULES ON THIS SECTION'S AUDI QUESTION.** Lilian read the client's own
> note as the fact it states: **the vehicle was disposed of, and it comes off the depreciation schedule.**
> ⛔ **What this section still holds open is UNTOUCHED and is the IDENTIFICATION** — that the schedule's
> entry and the note's vehicle are the same one remains an assumption, and it is what gates removing the
> row and computing the Form 4797 disposal.

**The 2024 Form 4562 schedule is the opening position** *(§3A-bis ④)*. Matching it to the client's
2025 sheet is how disposals are found — **and the match does not work, because the two use different
names.**

🔗 **§3G narrows this and does NOT close it.** The client's **description** list does line up with the
schedule by date and cost *(approximately — §3G ①)*. **His UNIT NUMBERS, which is how his DISPOSAL
table names the trucks, still do not** — so everything below stands unchanged.

| 2024 tax schedule | Cost | On the client's 2025 sheet? |
|---|---|---|
| AUDI, 2/1/2021 | 50,020 | 🔴 **NOT in any 2025 table** — but a note says *"2019 Audi A6 traded in for 19,500"* |
| FRT'18 (HU4577), 5/15/2020 | 46,000 | ~*"2018 FRGHT Cascadia, 2020-05-01, paid off"* |
| 2018 Freightliner #7107, 6/16/2021 | 78,145 | ~*"2018 Freightliner Cascadia, 2021-06-01, 78,000"* |
| 2018 Freightliner #1800, 7/20/2021 | 100,245 | ~*"2018 Freightliner Cascadia, 2021-08-01, 100,000"* |
| 2019 Wabash Trailer, 5/3/2022 | 50,000 | ✅ same |
| 2023 Utility Trailer, 11/11/2022 | 59,299 | ⚠️ client says **56,000** — a 3,299 difference |
| 2022 Frght Cascadia, 9/25/2024 | 85,500 | ✅ same |
| 2025 Benson, 9/25/2024 | 67,050 | ✅ same |

🔴 **THE AUDI IS THE FINDING.** The 2024 schedule carries `AUDI, 2/1/2021, 50,020`, at **100% business
use with 15,000 business miles** *(Form 4562 Part V)*, and **Julia ruled in August 2025 that it is
100% Zakom's** — ⚠️ 🆕 **the RETURN is first-hand; the ATTRIBUTION is cited only to the 2026-09-13 Gmail/Drive sweep,
with no message named — flagged 2026-09-25 when Lilian asked where it came from** *(§3AC ⑥)*. The client's 2025 sheet records only a free-text note — *"2019 Audi A6 traded in for
19,500"* — **not an entry in his "2025 Sold" table.**

⚠️ **The premise, stated because the whole finding rests on it: that the schedule's `AUDI` and the
note's `2019 Audi A6` are the SAME vehicle is an assumption.** A 2019 model placed in service in
February 2021 is entirely ordinary, and no second Audi appears anywhere — **but nobody has confirmed
it.**

⛔ **If it is the same car, a depreciated business asset was disposed of and the client did not
present it as a disposal.** ⚠️ **That it belongs on Form 4797 is stated from knowledge and not read
off the 2025 forms** — the same marking every other legal claim in this paper carries.

⚠️ **And the client's disposal table cannot be matched to the tax schedule at all.** He lists units
**1834, 1835, 1836**; the 2024 schedule names **#7107, #1800, HU4577**. **Nobody can say which tax
asset each sale disposed of** — and without that there is no basis, no gain or loss, and no Form
4797. ⛔ **The client has to map his unit numbers to the schedule.**

⚠️ **The 2015 Utility Trailer** is sold in 2025 per the client and **appears nowhere on the 2024 tax
schedule** — so it is either fully depreciated, never capitalised, or a naming mismatch too.

🔑 🆕 **ITS PROVENANCE, STATED IN FULL BECAUSE LILIAN ASKED WHERE IT CAME FROM (2026-09-27).** **Both facts
about it — that it exists and that it was sold — come from ONE source: the client's own workbook**
`Balance Sheet & P&L Templates (2) (1).xlsx`, **uploaded to the 2025 organizer**, in **two different
sheets of it**:

| What | Which sheet | What it says |
|---|---|---|
| **It was SOLD** | `Asset List 2025`, the **Disposals** block | `2015 Utility TRL` · **2025-06-01** · proceeds **4,000** |
| **It EXISTED before** | the older `Asset List` sheet **carried forward inside the same file** *(§3G ①)* | `2015 Utility Trailer`, marked **"paid off"** — ⛔ **no cost, no in-service date** |

⛔ **THERE IS NO FIRM DOCUMENT AND NO LENDER DOCUMENT BEHIND EITHER FACT, and it is on NEITHER filed
return** *(§3I ①)*. ⇒ **On the most likely reading — never capitalised — there is no basis to recover against the 4,000, so
the whole 4,000 is gain.** ⚠️ 🆕 **STATED AS A READING, NOT A FACT, on review: the three readings
twelve lines above are NOT closed, and under the *naming mismatch* one the asset IS on the schedule
under another name and DOES carry basis.** ⛔ **A Form 4797 is required either way, which is why this
does not gate anything** — **but the 4,000 must not be keyed as pure gain until the unit-number map
arrives** *(§3AH ① row 1)* — **and a disposal of something never capitalised is invisible to the schedule check while
still needing a Form 4797** *(§6)*.

🛑 🆕 **AND THE TRAP THAT MUST BE SAID OUT LOUD: THERE ARE **TWO** UTILITY TRAILERS, AND THEY ARE NOT THE
SAME ASSET.** ⚠️ **This is §3X's two-Benson-trailers problem again, on a different pair.**

| | `2015 Utility TRL` | `2023 Utility Trailer` |
|---|---|---|
| On the 2024 depreciation schedule? | ⛔ **NO — nowhere** | ✅ **YES — asset #6 of eight**, in service **11/11/2022**, cost **59,299**, 5-year at 100% |
| Financed by? | ⛔ **nobody — "paid off"** | ✅ **Financial Pacific `001-1733069-302`** *(§3Z ③)* |
| In 2025 it… | **was SOLD for 4,000** | **was still being paid — twelve payments** *(§3AD ②)* |

🔑 **So "the utility trailer" is ambiguous on this client and must never be used unqualified.**
⚠️ **§4 decision 50's lease ruling is about the `2023` one** — the asset that IS capitalised — **and says
nothing about the `2015` one, which has no contract at all.**

## 3G · 🔑 THE OLDER `Asset List` SHEET — what it unlocks, and what it does NOT

**The workbook has an OLDER `Asset List` sheet** *(the 2023 one, carried forward inside the same
file)* that nobody had opened until late on 2026-09-13. It **narrows** §3F's mapping problem and
raises three other questions.

⛔ **It does NOT unblock Form 4797.** What it maps is the client's **description** list to the tax
schedule. **His UNIT NUMBERS — 1834, 1835, 1836, which is how his DISPOSAL table names the trucks —
are still unmapped**, and that is the map Form 4797 needs. **§3F's ask stands unchanged.**

### ① The description list lines up with the tax schedule by DATE and COST — approximately

| The client's description | Tax schedule | Date gap | Cost gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2018 Freightliner Cascadia, in service **2021-06-01**, purchase **78,000** | **#7107**, 6/16/2021, **78,145** | 15 days | 145 |
| 2018 Freightliner Cascadia, in service **2021-08-01**, purchase **100,000** | **#1800**, 7/20/2021, **100,245** | 12 days, **crosses the month** | 245 |
| 2018 FRGHT Cascadia, in service **2020-05-01**, *"paid off"* | **HU4577**, 5/15/2020, **46,000** | 14 days | ⛔ **no cost given at all** |
| 2015 Utility Trailer, *"paid off"* | **not on the schedule at all** | — | ⚠️ fully depreciated, or never capitalised |

⚠️ **Not one row matches exactly on either axis, so these are CONSISTENT WITH, not CONFIRMED** — and
the third row has only an approximate date behind it. **This file applies the stricter standard forty
lines earlier**: §3F flags a 3,299 cost gap as a discrepancy and marks these same rows `~`. **A
plausible reading — the client's dates are month-rounded and his costs are invoice price against a
capitalised cost including tax and fees — is an EXPLANATION, not a verification.** ⛔ **The
identification goes to the client in the same ask as the unit-number map.**

🔑 **The client's unit numbers live in a DIFFERENT table** — his *financed equipment* table, which
names **`2018 FRGHT unit 1835`** and **`unit 1836`**. **So he has two naming systems** *(a description
list and a unit-number list)* **and the return has a third** *(#7107 / #1800 / HU4577)*.

🔴 **AND THE POSSIBLE CONSEQUENCE IS LARGE — as a question, not a finding.** The client's 2025 sheet
disposes of units **1834, 1835 and 1836** — **three 2018 Freightliners** — and the 2024 tax schedule
carries **exactly three** 2018 Freightliners, cost **224,390** between them *(78,145 + 100,245 +
46,000 ✅)*.

⛔ **The count matches; identity does not follow — and this paper holds two counterexamples:**

- **A FOURTH 2018 Freightliner joined the fleet in 2025** — `2018 FRGHT Cascadia 146`, 23,000, cash
  *(§3C)*. 🔑 **“Exactly three” is a fact about the 2024 SCHEDULE, not about the client's fleet.**
- **The 2015 Utility Trailer was disposed of in 2025 and appears nowhere on the 2024 schedule**
  *(§3F)* — **direct proof that this client disposes of rolling stock that was never capitalised.**
  Once the two populations are known to differ, a 3-to-3 count cannot establish identity: one
  disposed unit could be uncapitalised while a scheduled Freightliner stays in service. ⛔ **That is
  precisely the error that puts a wrong basis on Form 4797.**

⚠️ **The unit numbers are themselves only partly evidenced:** the financed-equipment table names
**1835** and **1836**; **1834 is known only from the client's own disposal-table label** — his third
naming system.

⚠️ **And the SIZE of the event is not computed.** 224,390 is **cost**. A Form 4797 gain or loss is
proceeds less **adjusted basis**, and this paper holds **no per-asset accumulated depreciation** —
only the 410,452 aggregate *(§7)*. **No claim is made about how big it is.**

### ② 🟠 THE BROWARD PROPERTY TAX — CORROBORATED, NOT RE-ESTABLISHED

**The 2026-09-13 review objected that nothing in the file placed the home in Broward County. This
sheet answers that one objection and not the others** — so the assertion does **not** come back. A
**question to the client** does.

**What the sheet adds:** it names a property in **PLANTATION, FLORIDA** ⚠️ *(that Plantation lies in
Broward County is stated from knowledge and has not been read off a source)* and reports the **2023
property tax at 14,218**, against 2025 Broward payments of **14,140.18**.

**What it does NOT settle — four live alternatives, not quibbles:**

1. ⛔ **That the named property is the home the office sits in.** The sheet does not label it a
   residence and nothing ties it to the `Home Office Deduction` worksheet *(§3D)*. **This client buys
   other real estate** — ③ below records a 100,000 line of credit used for *"auction property under
   Mema Colors LLC"*. 🔑 **The file holds a far stronger test that has NOT been run:** §1 records that
   **the company address on the filed 2024 return is the shareholder's home.** **Checking the sheet's
   property against that address would be decisive.** ⛔ Until it is, the link rests on a bare city
   name.
2. ⛔ **That these two payments are a property tax at all.** A county tax collector also issues
   **commercial vehicle registrations**, and this is a **three-tractor fleet** carrying its own
   `IFTA/HUT/Permits/IRP` line *(§3B)*. **Florida apportioned registration is a full explanation for
   the 13,417.00, not a residual one.**
3. ⛔ **That the shareholder's home is in Florida at all.** §3A records his address on the 2024 K-1 as
   **Chicago, IL**, and §6 still carries *"which states"* as an open item on the strength of it.
   **The file records two different places; this section cannot assert one of them.**
4. ⚠️ **The amounts corroborate weakly, and the one figure FOR THE YEAR IN QUESTION points the other
   way.** 14,218 (2023) against 14,140.18 (2025) is a 0.55% gap between **two different years**, while
   the **2025 worksheet's own property-tax figure is 15,700** — 1,559.82 higher. **There is no stated
   reason to prefer the 2023 figure over the 2025 one.** 🛑 [`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/)
   §3A check 4 ②: *a figure that matches is not a figure that is caused — do not build an explanation
   for the match.*

⚠️ **And the TIMING has not been examined.** Both payments fall in **April and May**, and **no third
Broward payment appears anywhere in the twelve statements.** ⚠️ *(Knowledge-sourced, unverified: on
the Florida calendar a spring payment is the PRIOR year's bill.)* **If that holds, these paid the
2024 bill and the 2025 bill was paid elsewhere — or not in 2025 at all.** ⛔ **So asking "which number
is the year's bill" conflates the BILL year with the PAYMENT year. Ask for both.**

🔑 **The honest statement is: corroborated, not established.** ⛔ **The address itself is deliberately
not written here.**

### ③ Three more facts this sheet alone carries

- **SBA loan 32,500**, 2023 interest **1,749**, at **159.00 monthly** ✅ — matching the recurring
  159.00 debit §3E ④ found in the bank. ⚠️ **Six payments in 2025, not twelve** *(1,272.00 in total,
  five at 159.00 plus one at 477.00)* — **so it is the same loan, not a payment every month.**
- 🔴 **A 100,000 business line of credit, March–July 2023, *"used for auction property under Mema
  Colors LLC"*** — **the company's credit line funded the OTHER entity's property purchase.** That is
  an intercompany item, in the client's own words, and it is the second time Mema Colors appears
  inside Zakom's records. ⚠️ **A 100,000 also sits unexplained in `Other current assets` across 2023
  and 2024** *(§3A)*. ⛔ **Nothing connects them — the match is not the cause. §6 carries it as a
  question.**
- **A sales-tax exposure nobody has looked at:** he paid **3,061** of Florida sales tax on the 2023
  Utility trailer that *"had to be exempt"*, was told to file a **DR-1** exemption application, and
  **never completed it.**

### ④ The home-office PROPORTION has not changed since 2023 — and that says nothing about 2024

**2,766 sq ft total, 922 sq ft office, one third** — **identical in the 2023 sheet and the 2025
worksheet.** ✅ **So the office itself did not change.**

⛔ **An earlier draft concluded from this that "what changed between 2024 and 2025 is the COSTS being
allocated". That does not follow, and it contradicts §3A-bis ②:** **no 2024 home-office worksheet
exists or has been searched for**, so nobody knows what method 2024 used. ⚠️ **And the arithmetic
argues the other way:** 7,951 at one third implies a 2024 home-cost base of about **23,850**, against
2025's **81,745.48** on the same house — **which points at a changed METHOD, not changed costs.**
🔑 **§3A-bis ② already describes what that change looks like: the client has now built a Form

⛔ **AND NOTHING FOLLOWS FROM THAT.** An earlier draft of this section called it *"likely"* that the
client's interest line already **is** these three letters, on the strength of the 3.8%, with *"the
SBA's own interest would fit the size"* offered as the make-weight. 🛑 **BOTH HALVES ARE WITHDRAWN,
and this paper's own figures are what withdraw them:**

  32,500 loan with 2023 interest of 1,749**, and §3E ④ found **six 2025 payments totalling 1,272.00**.
  **Its 2025 interest alone is of the order of the whole 659.95, and plausibly twice it.**
- **And it is not the only other debt.** §3E ⑧ names **three BMO contracts, an Audi lease, IPFS,
  Financial Pacific, a Fifth Third credit line, a Fifth Third credit card, Macy's and the SBA.** For
  the "already is" reading to hold, **the 2025 interest on ALL of them together would have to come to
  659.95.** ⛔ **That was never tested, and it is not plausible.** ✅ 🆕 **AND §3V NOW TESTS IT, 2026-09-15.** **Two NON-EQUIPMENT interest components the client's line would also have to contain are now identified: the SBA's 1,272.00** *(§3V ②, exact)* **and the Fifth Third line of credit's — ✅ 🆕 9,755.76, the range having COLLAPSED on 2026-09-17** *(§3AA; it read "6,581.92 measured, 9,930.76 ceiling" between 09-15 and 09-17)*. 🔑 **Either one alone dwarfs 659.95.** ⛔ **So his 17,401 cannot already contain the equipment interest AND these — which all but settles decision 8a.** ☑️ **Not answered here: it is Julia's, and the lender's letter is her stated authority** *(decision 8)*. **What changes is that this is no longer an untested assertion.**
- **The competing reading is simpler and the client's own label supports it:** the 102,834 line says
  *"incld interest and principal"* on its face *(§3B)*. **A client who had stripped the equipment
  interest out into a separate line would not label it that way.** On that reading the 17,401 is the
  interest on the OTHER debt, the equipment interest is still inside the 102,834, and **the near
  equality is coincidence.**

🛑 **This is [`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/) §3A check 4 ② — *a figure that
matches is not a figure that is caused* — and §3G ② rejected a 0.55% coincidence on that rule earlier
in this same file. A 3.8% one cannot be accepted on the same page.**

⚠️ **The risk the arithmetic DOES flag is real and unchanged:** if the equipment interest turns out to
be in both places, deducting the split **and** the 17,401 line would deduct the same interest twice.
**That is a thing to rule out, not a finding.**

🛑 **So this is ONE question to the client, and it decides the deduction:**

> **"Your `Interest` line of 17,401 — does it already include the interest on the heavy-haul financed

year. **If he cannot, the lender letters plus the contracts' own payment schedules are the route**
*(§3H ③ gives the monthly payment on two of them)*, **not a guess from the 659.95.**

failed gate means **⛔ gross.** 🔑 **So 2025 is prepared GROSS regardless of what the bank work finds.**

### ① The year in one number — and the document reconciles EXACTLY

totals sum to it, **and** the **30 category subtotals give charges of 112,720.19 less credits of

### ② ⚠️ WHAT MIGHT BE PERSONAL — and the first draft picked it the wrong way round

⛔ **THE METHOD WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE.** An earlier draft chose **nine of Amex's own
category captions**, totalled them at **9,832.29**, and headed the column *"what the line items
actually say."* **The categories were chosen first and the line items read to illustrate them** —
which produced false positives, false negatives and two statements of fact the source contradicts.

**Corrections to what that draft asserted:**

| It said | The source says |
|---|---|
| `US SOCCER 5 LLC` — *"fortnightly all year"* | ⛔ **6 charges: 16 Jan · 20 Mar · 16 Oct · 30 Oct · 4 Dec · 11 Dec.** A **seven-month gap**. **99.91 for the year** |
| `SOCIAL & SPORT CLUB HALLANDALE` — *"weekly all year"* | ⛔ **8 charges, ALL between 21 Aug and 8 Dec.** Nothing Jan–Jul. **145.19 for the year** |
| *"a Florence boutique hotel and a Rome address, early September"* | ⚠️ **The trip runs 29 Aug – 8 Sep by TRANSACTION date** — Rome, Florence, Siena, San Cesareo and Trento, **five places, not two.** *"September"* is the **`Month Billed`** column, not the date |
| *"including Eataly and Mercato Centrale"* under **Groceries** | ⚠️ **`MERCATO CENTRALE` is in `Restaurant`**, not Groceries |
| The nine categories are the personal share | ⛔ **Both directions are wrong — below** |

**What the line items actually show, read merchant by merchant:**

- 🟢 **Much of `Travel Agencies` reads BUSINESS for a trucking company.** ≈ **1,327** is HotelTonight
  at **budget motels** — `DAYS INN` ×3, `SUPER 8` ×2, `ECONO LODGE`, `BEST WESTERN` — **spread over
  Jan, Mar, May, Jul, Oct, Nov and Dec, in ones and twos.** That is the ordinary shape of a driver
  overnighting on the road. **The Italy-clustered remainder is a different matter.**
- ⛔ **`Airline 4,613.21` is GROSS.** Three `Points for Amex Trvl` **credits** in Miscellaneous —
  293.18 + 342.96 + 673.61 = **1,309.75** — reverse part of it. **≈ 13% of the old 9,832 was charges
  that were paid with points.**
- 🔴 **Italy charges sit OUTSIDE the nine categories too:** `Bar & Café` **55.63** *(the dropped
  category — ①)* and **`LOCAUTO RENT`, Trento — 410.86**, in `Internet Purchase`.
- 🔴 **And recurring personal-looking charges sit in categories the draft never examined, because
  their Amex caption looked businesslike:** `SIMPLISAFE` **12 monthly charges ≈ 412** *(home security
  — and this company's office IS the shareholder's home, §3D)*, `EL CAR WASH` **10 charges ≈ 250**,
  and a **cleaning service 145.00**, all inside `Professional Services` and `Other Services`.

🔑 **So there is no clean total, and quoting one would repeat the error.** What is defensible:

| | |
|---|---|
| The nine captions, **gross** | 9,832.29 |
| less points credits | **(1,309.75)** |
| less the budget-motel lodging that reads business | **≈ (1,327)** |
| plus Italy charges in other categories | **+ ≈ 466** |
| plus recurring items in unexamined categories | **+ ≈ 807** |
| **Indicative only** | **≈ 8,469** ⛔ **not a figure to key or quote** |

⛔ **"Looks personal" is not a finding, and the CATEGORY IS NOT A PROXY FOR THE ANSWER.** A trucking
company can have deductible travel; a home-security subscription on a home-office property may be
partly deductible. **Only the client can classify these.** 🛑 **What is certain is the consequence:
whatever is personal is a DISTRIBUTION against a ZERO opening AAA** *(§3A)* — **not an expense.**

🔑 **The questions to put, and they are specific rather than accusatory:** *what were the trip to
Italy (29 Aug – 8 Sep), the May flight and the 24 December clothing purchase for; and are the
SimpliSafe, car-wash, cleaning, soccer and sports-club charges the company's or yours?*

### ③ 🟠 THE CARD CARRIES INTEREST — 7,001.63 — and where it belongs is NOT established

**Eleven monthly `Interest Charge on Promotional Balances` lines total 7,001.63.** ✅ **January
carries none** — its `Fees & Adjustments` monthly cell is 0.00 — **so 11 lines against a 12-month row
is correct, not a gap.** The rest of `Fees & Adjustments` is a **375.00 renewal fee** and a **39.00
late fee**; 7,001.63 + 414.00 = 7,415.63 ✅.

🔑 **What follows: "promotional balances" means the card carried a revolving balance**, so **there IS
a year-end figure to report.** The document does not state it.

⛔ **WHAT DOES NOT FOLLOW, AND AN EARLIER DRAFT ASSERTED IT.** It said the 7,001.63 *"has no home in
the client's P&L"* and that *"a deduction is visibly missing."* 🛑 **§3H ② contradicts it.** The
reading §3H ② actually prefers is that **the client's 17,401 `Interest` line is the interest on the
OTHER debt** — and it lists that debt as *"three BMO contracts, an Audi lease, IPFS, Financial
Pacific, a Fifth Third credit line, a Fifth Third credit card, Macy's and the SBA."* 🔑 **A revolving
Amex is exactly that kind of debt. It is simply not on the list.** **So the 7,001.63 is a CANDIDATE
COMPONENT of the 17,401, and the composition of that line is unknown.**

⚠️ **And "business interest" prejudges ②:** interest on a personal balance is not the company's
deduction. ⛔ **The two questions are linked and neither is answered.**

🔑 **One question, and §4 decision 7 forbids settling it by arithmetic:** *"Does your 17,401 Interest
line include the American Express interest?"*

### ④ 🟠 THE PAYMENTS DO NOT RECONCILE — and ✅ 🆕 THIS SECTION'S OWN HEDGE WAS RIGHT

> ✅ 🆕 **2026-09-15 — THE CLOSING BALANCE ARRIVED AND THIS SECTION PREDICTED IT.** Below it says
> *"7,931.75 assumes the card closed the year at ZERO … the real gap is 7,931.75 PLUS whatever the
> closing balance is."* 🔑 **The 31/12/2025 balance is now known to be AT LEAST 31,159.61** *(§3Q ③)*,
> **so the gap is at least `7,931.75 + 31,159.61 = 39,091.36` BEFORE interest, or `32,089.73` once the
> year's 7,001.63 of Amex interest is put on the charges side** *(§3Q ④ quotes the interest-inclusive
> figure — the two are the same gap on two bases, not two gaps)*. ⛔ **Do not quote 7,931.75 as the
> size of anything.** ⚠️ **And cause 1 — that the 2024 return's 4,623 understates what was owed — is
> now the front-runner** ⚠️ 🆕 **— RETIRED. §3Q ④ calls this ranking PREMATURE and "front-runner" an over-statement of a reasoned guess against a MEASURED result. Do not carry it forward** *(§3Q ④)*.

**The original section, unchanged below — its reasoning is what made the prediction.**

#### The four causes, as written before the balance was known

_(⛔ **An earlier draft headed this *"it points at the second Amex"* while the body said the opposite.
A heading is what survives skimming. Withdrawn.**)_

| | |
|---|---|
| Paid to Amex from the bank in 2025 *(39 payments matched on the string `AMEX` — §3E ②)* | **123,786.73** |
| Opening balance per the **2024 return** *(`CREDIT CARD AMEX`, §3I ④)* | **4,623** |
| Charges on this card in 2025, net of credits | **111,231.98** |
| 🔴 **Payments exceed opening + charges by** | **≥ 7,931.75** |

🛑 **AND 7,931.75 IS A FLOOR, NOT THE SIZE OF IT.** The roll-forward is
`opening + charges − payments = closing`. **7,931.75 assumes the card closed the year at ZERO** — and
③ shows **887.83 of interest charged on 19 December**, so it plainly did not. **The real discrepancy
is 7,931.75 PLUS whatever the closing balance is.**

**Four candidate causes — ✅ one now ELIMINATED, three untested. None is evidence for another:**

1. **The 2024 return's 4,623 understates what was owed.** ⚠️ §3I ④ already says it **is a return
   figure, not a statement balance** — and the same statement's SBA line sat unchanged across a year

   with "promotional balances sit outside the ordinary statement balance." **That is background
   knowledge, not something this document says, and it was not marked as such. Withdrawn** — the

2. **BASIS MISMATCH, and the document says so on its own page 2:** *"Any charges processed by a
   merchant after December 31, 2025 will appear on next year's Summary."* **A Year-End Summary is on
   a charge-processing basis; a balance is on a billing basis.** 🔑 **The evidence is in the report:
   the 973.70 charge dated 24 December carries `Month Billed: January`.** **Charges in this total
   were never in any 2025 balance.**
3. ✅ **THE CARD NUMBER — TESTED 2026-09-14, AND THIS CAUSE IS ELIMINATED.** All three summaries were

   one.**  🔑 **So 2025 is the same card as 2024**, so the opening balance and the year's charges are

   unproven: that the 2024 return's `CREDIT CARD AMEX` line is this card rather than another — the
   preparer's source for that figure is unknown.)*
4. **Some of the 39 payments went to another Amex account.** ⚠️ **Broader than Mema Colors:** the
   card is in the **shareholder's own name** and ② argues part of its spending is personal, so **his
   personal Amex is at least as available an explanation.** ⛔ **Collapsing this into the Mema Colors
   question narrows it without warrant, and an earlier draft did exactly that.**
