## 3K · 🔴 THE ACCOUNT-INVENTORY RULING — and it pushes MORE through the capital account, not less

_(Lilian, 2026-09-14: **"vamos a hacer lo mismo que hizo Julia: que asumamos que las únicas cuentas de
negocio son las mismas que se reportaron en 2024"**, with her reason stated: **monthly payments
leaving the business bank account to a credit card do not make that card a business account.**)_

### ① What the 2024 return ACTUALLY reported — and the count cannot be given

⛔ **"How many accounts" has no whole-number answer, and the reason is on the return.**

| Reported | Schedule L line | Named by the return's own statement? | 12/31/2023 → 12/31/2024 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Cash** | 1 | ⛔ **No caption, no statement** — a figure only | → **19,010** |
| **`Loan to Mema Colors LLC`** | 6 | ✅ quoted, **both years** | 100,000 → 100,000 |
| **`CREDIT CARD AMEX`** | 18 | ✅ quoted | 11,923 → **4,623** |
| **Mortgages, notes ≥ 1 year** | 20 | ⛔ **ONE AGGREGATE, no statement in the extracted text** | 86,337 → **169,863** |
| **`SBA Loan`** | 21 | ✅ quoted | 32,500 → 32,500 |
| **`Business Line Of Credit`** | 21 | ✅ quoted | **not on the 2023 balance sheet at all** → 98,000 |

🔑 **So: FOUR accounts named, one reported unnamed (cash), and line 20 is an UNKNOWN NUMBER OF
CONTRACTS.** ⚠️ **A ruling that adopts "the accounts 2024 reported" adopts a set whose size is
unknown on its largest liability line.**

⚠️ **And two Schedule L lines are never mentioned in this paper in either direction — 17 *(notes < 1
year)* and 19 *(loans from shareholders)*.** ⓘ *Inference, marked: the reported figures close to
§3A's own total of 244,817 — `4,623 + 169,863 + 130,500 + (1,000 − 61,169 + 0)` — which supports
**lines 17 and 19 TOGETHER summing to zero**, and since neither can be negative, **each of them was
zero**: no shareholder loan was reported in 2024.* ⛔ **Not proof** — an offsetting unreported asset
closes identically. ✅ **Though that escape is narrow: the ASSET side ties independently —
`19,010 + 100,000 + (536,259 − 410,452) = 244,817` — so an unreported asset would have to be
offset by a second unreported item.** **It matters because ③ below may need somewhere to put a
shareholder receivable, and the prior year offers no precedent for one.**

⚠️ **The 11,923 opening on line 18 is NOT established as this same card.** §1 and §6 both record that
**2023 carries a DIFFERENT masked Amex card number from 2024.** ⛔ **Assigning both columns to one
account is an unmarked inference and it is withdrawn here.**

### ② ✅ What the ruling genuinely closes — say this plainly, it is real

- **Schedule L cash at 31/12/2025 is 5,788.50, unconditionally.** §3E ①'s *"neither number is usable
  until the other accounts are identified"* and §4 decision 10a both close.
- **The Schedule L card liability is the Amex's alone** — ⚠️ **WHICH account, not WHICH FIGURE.**
  The opening is the 2024 return's **4,623**, and §3J ④ leaves two things live about it: it is **a
  return figure, not a statement balance** *(the same statement's SBA line sat unchanged across a year
  of payments — §3I ④ calls that a likely prior-year defect)* ⛔ 🆕 **— AND THAT SBA LEG IS WITHDRAWN: §3V ② shows the payments were going to DEFERRED INTEREST, so the unchanged balance was correct, not a defect**, and **it is unproven that the 2024
  `CREDIT CARD AMEX` line is this card rather than another.** ⛔ **"Unconditionally" belongs on cash,
  not on 4,623.** ✅ 🆕 **NARROWED 2026-09-15: the 4,623 IS the December 2024 statement balance, confirmed to the cent against the client’s own document** *(§3R ②)*, **and the 01/19/25 statement opens at exactly 4,623.02, so the chain corroborates that the return’s line refers to THIS card.** ⚠️ **What SURVIVES of this caution is now the whole of it: the statement closes 12/19 and line 18 asks for 12/31** *(§3R ⑧ — a convention question for Julia, not a defect)*. ⛔ **The “a return figure, not a statement balance” wording is WITHDRAWN; restatements elsewhere point here.**
- **§4 decision 10's "the cards" resolves from four accounts to one** — ⚠️ **plus the Fifth Third
  credit LINE, which 2024 reported on line 21 and which is not a card.**
- **§6's balance-sheet rebuild row loses its *"IF this is the only company account"* hedge** ✅ **(done
  — §6, and the card-statements row rewritten with it).**

### ③ 🛑 AND HERE IS WHAT TRAVELS WITH IT — the ruling does NOT make anything disappear

**🔴 (a) Payments to a card that is not the company's are NOT COMPANY EXPENSES — and this file does
NOT establish what they are instead.** If the Fifth Third credit card and the Macy's card are not the
company's accounts, then **every payment the business bank account made to them in 2025 was the
company discharging a liability that was never its own.**
🔑 **§3A-bis ② already wrote the rule that governs this, in another context:** *"a debit that stops
being an expense has to land somewhere — a **distribution**, a **receivable from the shareholder**,
or **officer compensation**. ⛔ Nothing establishes which."* **Nothing in this file evidences a
receivable or wages** — ⛔ **but "nothing evidences the other two" is not "it is the first."**
§3A-bis ② never resolved it, and §3K ① observes only that *the prior year offers no precedent* for a
receivable, which is an absence in a different year, not evidence about 2025. 🔑 **The choice is
open, it is JULIA's, and the three land in three different places on three different returns** — a
**distribution** reduces equity and goes to the shareholder's §1368 analysis; a **receivable** is a
Schedule L asset and nothing else; **officer compensation** is a page-1 deduction that carries
payroll tax and a W-2.
⛔ **An earlier draft went further and said a distribution here "is taxable to the shareholder" while
the other two "are not." Both halves are wrong and it is withdrawn.** Under **§1368(b)(2)** — which
applies because there is no accumulated E&P *(§8)* — what makes a distribution taxable is the
shareholder's **STOCK BASIS**, not the AAA, and 🛑 **his basis is an open item this file does not
hold** *(§6 — his prior-year Form 7203 line 15, filed with his 1040)*. **And officer compensation is
ordinary income to him plus payroll tax** — not taxable *under §1368*, which is not the same thing as
not taxable.
⛔ **An earlier draft headed this *"are DISTRIBUTIONS"* while the body said the opposite — the same
failure §3J ④'s own preamble withdrew on itself *("it points at the second Amex")*. A heading is what
survives skimming. Withdrawn.**

**🔴 (b) The same applies to the property tax, conditionally.** The company sent **14,140.18** to the
county *(§3E ④)*. **If that is the shareholder's home property tax, it is the same act as paying his
credit card** — a personal obligation discharged from the company's account, which does not become a
company expense by being paid from there. ⛔ **The condition is NOT closed** — §3G ② keeps the
commercial-vehicle-registration reading live.

**🔴 (c) The "sweep" reading of the four other Fifth Third accounts DIES.** §3E ③ concluded the four
were *"a pass-through / sweep — neither income nor expense."* 🛑 **A sweep is movement between a
company's OWN accounts. The ruling removes that premise.** **53 lines and 47,884.46 went OUT to an
outside party; 17 lines and 51,231.00 came IN — and they can no longer be netted.** ⚠️ *The ruling
establishes the accounts are not the company's; it does not establish whose they are.*

**🔴 (d) §3E ⑥'s bounded range "≈ 0 to ≈ 137,927" is WITHDRAWN, and it cannot be rebuilt from this
file.** Its floor of ≈ 0 required the transfers to net to nothing and the Amex and Broward to be
fully business. **Under the ruling the transfers alone contribute 47,884.46 gross, and two new rows
enter it — the 2025 payments to the Fifth Third card and to Macy's.** 🛑 **NEITHER OF THOSE TWO HAS A
DOLLAR TOTAL ANYWHERE IN THIS REPO** — §3E ⑦ and ⑧ count the payments and never total them.
**Closing it needs a fresh scan of the twelve 2025 statements for the Fifth Third web-pay and Macy's
strings.** ⛔ **§6 keeps it as an open item; the range is not replaced by a smaller one.**

⚠️ **And 47,884.46 is not yet a figure to put in a capital account either.** §3E ③'s two tables do
not tie: the per-account column sums to **96,805.38** against a direction split of
`47,884.46 + 51,231.00 = 99,115.46` — **2,310.08 apart.** ⛔ **THEY DO NOT TIE; THE REASON IS
UNTESTED.** *(An earlier draft said "one of the two scans is incomplete" — a cause, asserted. The two
tables may simply measure different populations: §3E's own method note records that **individual CHECK
rows parsed poorly, 5 of the ~30**.)* 🛑 **Re-run both before any of these numbers is keyed.**

**⚠️ (e) A class of open items converts from IDENTIFY to QUANTIFY — do not delete them.** §6 asks for
statements for every account **on the ground that "if any is the company's its balance belongs on
Schedule L."** **The ruling removes that ground and NOT the purpose:** money leaving the company to a
non-company account is a capital-account item and still has to be **measured**. 🛑 **Rewrite those
rows; striking them would silently drop 47,884.46.**

**🔴 (f) THE RULING'S OWN ADOPTED YEAR DID THE SAME THING — and nobody has asked what 2024 did with
it.** §3E ⑧ records **Macy's** paid *"~1/month"* from the business account **in 2024**, and §3E ⑦
records the **Fifth Third card** web-pay debited *"once every month from June 2024 through December
2025."* 🛑 **So in 2024 — the year the ruling adopts — the company made the SAME payments to the SAME
non-company cards.** ⛔ **③(a) derives an adverse consequence from "do what 2024 did" without ever
asking what 2024 DID with them, and this file cannot answer it:** 2024's K-1 carried **113,157**
against an M-2 of **51,988**, and §3I ③ says the mechanism behind the excess is **inferred and this
paper forbids stating it**. 🔑 **Two readings, and they lead to different places:** either those
payments are already **inside** 2024's distributions — in which case ③(a) is a **continuation, not a
discovery**, and the ruling costs far less than it looks — **or 2024 has an unbooked distribution**,
which is a **candidate DEFECT for the signer** in the §4C sense. ☑️ **This is the symmetry argument
that decides how much weight ③ carries, and it goes to JULIA** *(⑤'s handoff, §6)*.

### ④ ⛔ Three things the ruling must NOT be read as saying

1. **It adopts the 2024 return as the ACCOUNT INVENTORY, not as the BALANCES.** §3I ④: the SBA sits
   ⛔ 🆕 **WITHDRAWN — §3V ② proves it is a loan still working off DEFERRED accrued interest — ⚠️ 🆕 **not an "interest-only" product, which is what an earlier version of this said; §3V ② corrects it: the balance holds while the BACKLOG lasts, a condition that can end**.** _(As written:)_ at 32,500 in both columns across a year of payments — *"more likely a prior-year defect than a
   convention — do not copy it without asking."*
2. 🛑 **IT DOES NOT BAR AN ACCOUNT THAT FIRST EXISTED IN 2025.** The **Audi lease** *(from Aug/Sep
   2025)*, **IPFS**, the **Huntington/BP** fuel relationship *(Nov 2025)* and **every 2025 equipment
   note** are all post-2024 and all plainly the company's. **Read literally, "only the accounts 2024
   reported" would delete the year's own borrowings.** **The ruling is about which EXISTING accounts
   are the company's, not about forbidding new ones.**
3. ✅ 🆕 **ANSWERED 2026-09-27 — §4 DECISION 57, LILIAN: *"El cliente utiliza una cuenta checking de negocio y una tarjeta Amex de negocio."* IT IS THE COMPANY'S CARD.** ⛔ **So the rule-versus-reason standoff below is CLOSED, and it closed on her word rather than on either argument.** ⚠️ **What it does NOT close is the 46,344.31 that never reached this card — §3T ⑦, and decision 57 makes that MORE clearly a capital-account item, not less, because every other account is now established as PERSONAL.** _(As the question stood:)_ 🔴 **THE AMEX IS A QUESTION FOR LILIAN, NOT A CALL THIS PAPER MAY MAKE.** Her ruling has a **rule**
   *(only the accounts 2024 reported)* and a **reason** *(monthly payments from the business account to
   a credit card do not make that card a business account)* — **and on this one account they point
   opposite ways.** The rule keeps it: 2024 reported it on Schedule L line 18. The reason disqualifies
   it: it is paid **39 times a year** from the business account, it is a *"Business Gold Card"*
   **"Prepared for OLEG ZAKALA" with Zakom named nowhere**, and 2023 and 2024 are the same.
   ⛔ **An earlier draft resolved this itself — *"retained only because the 2024 return reported
   it"*. Withdrawn.** CLAUDE.md: a session may rely on a carve-out that is **written down** and **may
   never create one**, and *"a sound argument that the permission ought to cover this case is not the
   permission."* **The same governs a ruling whose rule and reason disagree: put the question.**
   🔑 **What rides on the answer: 123,786.73 of payments** *(§3E ②)*, **and whether Schedule L carries
   a card liability at all.** ☑️ **It is a §6 open item and it is in ⑤'s handoff — not a "worth
   knowing."**

### ⑤ 🛑 THE OBJECTION THAT TRAVELS TO THE SIGNER — this paper wrote it before the ruling existed

**§3I ②:** *"⛔ **And Schedule L cash is not a presentation convention.** If an account is the
company's, its balance belongs there whatever last year did — **copying a presentation cannot cure an
ownership question.**"*

🔑 **The ruling does precisely what that sentence forbids: it settles an OWNERSHIP question by
adopting the prior year's PRESENTATION.** ⚠️ **Under CLAUDE.md's §4C rule the objection reaches the
signer WITH the decision — and this is a DECISION, not a defect. Julia may confirm it.** **What must
travel with it:** what the position assumes *(that none of the four Fifth Third accounts and neither
of the two other cards is the company's)*, and what it costs *(an amount nobody has yet measured
has to move through the capital account instead of through the operating accounts — ③(d))*.

🛑 **AND TWO MORE THINGS TRAVEL WITH IT — neither is a "worth knowing":**

1. 🔴 **TO LILIAN, BECAUSE IT IS HER RULING: the AMEX is the one account where her rule and her stated
   reason disagree** *(④.3)*. **123,786.73 of payments and the only card liability on Schedule L turn
   on which of the two governs.** ⛔ **This paper does not pick.**
2. 🔴 **TO JULIA: what did 2024 do with the SAME card payments?** *(③(f))* The ruling adopts 2024, and
   2024 paid the same non-company cards from the same account. **Either they are inside its 113,157 of
   distributions — and ③(a) is a continuation — or 2024 has an unbooked distribution, which is a
   candidate defect on a return she signed.** **The file cannot tell which.**

⚠️ **AND ONE PIECE OF EVIDENCE THAT LOOKS LIKE SUPPORT AND IS NOT — read this before quoting it.**
§3E ⑦ shows a **CK** and an **SV** counterparty moving money **inside 2024** — the very year the
ruling adopts — **and the 2024 Schedule L cash still equals the operating account's closing balance to
the dollar.** So the prior preparer had traffic in front of him and still reported one account.
⛔ **That is a MATCH, not a CAUSE, and §3I ② already gave the three readings it is consistent with:**
the other accounts held ≈ nil; they are **not the company's**; or 🔑 ***the prior preparer simply had
no statement for them*** — **which §3I ② calls fully sufficient on its own**, because §3E ⑦
establishes the firm **has never been given a statement for any other account, in either year.**
🛑 **So the objection is NOT answered.** The honest form, and the one that goes to Julia: **the prior
year's presentation is CONSISTENT with the ruling and with two other explanations, one of which fully
accounts for it without the ruling being true.**
⚠️ **And the evidence is narrower than "those accounts":** §3E ⑦ names **one CK and one SV**
counterparty across **eight of twelve months** *(Jan–Apr 2024 unread; every count there is a
**FLOOR**)* — so it reaches **at most 2 of the 4** Fifth Third accounts, and neither of the two other
cards. *(`tax-return-sop` §3A check 4 ②: a figure that matches is not a figure that is caused — the
same test §3G ②.4 and §3I ⑤ already apply in this file.)*

## 3L · 📨 THE CLIENT'S MESSAGE OF 2026-09-14 — two things, and one of them is new

_(Relayed by Lilian, verbatim: **"Also Mema Colors LLC had no activity in 2025 again… Automatic
payments for 2024 have been withdrawn from my account but I am not sure if monthly amount was correct
if we can double check with IRS if 2024 is fully paid off at this point."**)_

### ① *"Mema Colors had no activity in 2025 again"* — ⛔ this does NOT answer what we asked

🔑 **"No activity" is a statement about OPERATIONS. The open item is a BALANCE.** The 2024 Schedule L
carries **`Loan to Mema Colors LLC` — 100,000**, unchanged across both columns *(§3I ⑤)*: **money
Zakom LENT to the other entity.** ⛔ **A dormant company can owe 100,000 perfectly well.**

**What to ask instead:** *"Did Mema Colors repay any of the 100,000 to Zakom during 2025, or is the
full 100,000 still owed at 31 December?"* ⚠️ **And a second:** the client has said twice that Mema
Colors *"only has Amex expenses"* — **if it had no activity, is Zakom's account still paying that
card?** *(§3E ② withdrew the bank evidence for that; the question is still open.)*

### ② 🔴 *"Automatic payments for 2024… is 2024 fully paid off?"* — NEW, and nothing in this file explains it

🛑 **An S corporation pays no federal income tax itself**, so a 2024 balance being paid by monthly
direct debit needs an explanation this paper does not currently hold. **Candidates, none established:**

| Candidate | Why plausible | Why it might not be |
|---|---|---|
| 🔑 **The SHAREHOLDER's personal 1040 for 2024** | He wrote *"withdrawn from MY account"* ⚠️ *(his emphasis)*. The 2024 K-1 passed him **77,424** with **no withholding**, so a 2024 personal balance is the ordinary consequence. ⛔ **An earlier draft supported this with a line from the organizer — struck twice over:** that is the **2025** organizer *(§1 row 4, 75% complete, never submitted)*, and §0A ③ warns its silence is conditional; and **corporate** estimated payments do not bear on the **shareholder's** 1040 anyway — his own would | Then it is not this return's matter at all — but it IS the firm's |
| **Company payroll tax (Form 941)** | 🔑 **2024 DID run payroll** — 25,000 of officer compensation on Form 1125-E *(§3A)*. Undeposited payroll tax produces exactly this shape | 2025 has none, so it would be a closing 2024 matter |
| **An Illinois balance** | ⚠️ *(the general proposition is from knowledge, not read off any file)* Illinois levies a **replacement tax on S corporations**. §6's *"which states"* item is open and the 2024 K-1 puts the shareholder in **Chicago**. ✅ **And the firm's own file corroborates the STRUCTURE, not the balance:** the client-intelligence file records that **JK itself filed Illinois for 2022** — *"It has to be separated as we are doing 2 state reporting,"* with an Illinois tax deduction on that return | The 2022 filing does not establish a 2024 one, still less a balance; and it would be a **state**, not the IRS |
| 🔴 **A late-filing penalty on the 1120-S (§6699)** | ⛔ **The strongest fit — but NOT, as an earlier draft said, "the only candidate that is the COMPANY's."** ⚠️ **The Form 941 row directly above is equally the company's**, and undeposited payroll tax produces the same shape. **TWO of the four candidates are the company's; that is part of why ② cannot be answered from this file.** 🔑 **What makes §6699 the strongest fit is its SHAPE:** ⓘ *(from knowledge, not read off any file)* **it runs per shareholder per month**, so with one shareholder it is small and recurring — exactly a monthly direct debit. **The 2024 return was signed 9/6/2025**; ⓘ *(knowledge)* **the unextended due date for a calendar-year 1120-S was 3/17/2025** | ⛔ **An earlier draft dismissed this by citing a 7004 — the WRONG YEAR's.** The only 7004 in this file is `2025 7004 EXT.pdf`, in Double's **`Tax Return Filed > 2025`** folder *(§1 row 6)*, and §1 row 3 fixes that convention as **by tax year**; Lilian's *"el 7004 ya está confirmado"* was said on 2026-09-13 about **this** return. 🔑 **NOTHING IN THIS FILE EVIDENCES A TY2024 EXTENSION.** ⚠️ **And a signature date is not a filing date** — the extended date of **9/15/2025** is arithmetically right and evidentially unsupported |

🔑 **AND THE ACCOUNT-INVENTORY RULING ALREADY DECIDES HALF OF IT.** *"My account"* is ambiguous — **but
under §3K, if the debits leave anything other than Fifth Third …7807 then that account is not the
company's and the payments never reach this return.** ⛔ **And if they DO leave …7807 for a personal
tax, that is §3K ③(a) again — a debit that is not a company expense, landing in one of three places
§3A-bis ② does not choose between, against an AAA that opens at ZERO.**

🛑 **TWO THINGS TO DO BEFORE ANY OF THIS REACHES THE CLIENT — `method.md` rule 1, look before you ask.**

1. 🔴 **RUN THE SEARCH THE FIRM CAN ALREADY RUN.** The pivot above turns on *"do the debits leave
   …7807?"* — and the firm holds **twelve 2025 statements and eight 2024 ones, already extracted**.
   §3E's whole method is string-matching transaction descriptions, which is how Amex, Broward,
   Macy's, Cash App and the SBA were all found. ⛔ **No scan for `IRS`, `USATAXPYMT` or `EFTPS` has
   ever been run on them** — it costs one pass over files on disk and it answers the question
   without the client. **Do it first; ask second.**
2. ⚠️ **ASK WHEN THE DEBITS RAN — the message never says.** *"Automatic payments for 2024 have been
   withdrawn"* carries no date. 🔑 **If they are 2026 debits they cannot touch this return whatever
   account they leave**, and the firm holds no 2026 statements — which disposes of the question more
   cheaply than either route below.

### ③ ✅ HOW THE FIRM CAN ACTUALLY CHECK IT — and who may be named

**[`firm-identity.md`](../../sops/firm-identity.md) §4 answers this directly:**

| | What it does | Who may be named |
|---|---|---|
| **Form 8821** — *Tax Information Authorization* | **Inspect and receive** the client's confidential information — *"verbally or in writing"*, **so they can call and be told things** | ✅ **Anyone.** 🔑 **Lilian is eligible — this is her form** |
| **Form 2848** — *Power of Attorney* | **Represent** — argue, agree, sign, resolve | ⛔ **Only someone eligible to practice before the IRS** → **Julia (EA)**, designation (c). **A 2848 naming Lilian is not valid** |

🔑 **"Check whether 2024 is paid off" is being TOLD something, not acting. That is a Form 8821, and
Lilian can be the designee.** ⚠️ **But an 8821 is signed by the taxpayer who owes** — **so which
taxpayer it names depends entirely on ② above.** ✅ **And there is a route needing no form at all: the
client's own IRS online account, or he calls himself.**

## 3M · 🔴 THE 2024 DISTRIBUTION — what the return says, what it does NOT, and what can be rebuilt

_(Lilian, 2026-09-14: **"dime cuáles fueron las distribuciones en 2024. Fueron cero o hay un monto de
distribuciones. Si es un monto, dime si eres capaz de saber cómo fue calculado revisando los
documentos del cliente de 2024."** Everything below was read on 2026-09-14 — the 2024 filed return,
the 2024 Amex Year-End Summary, and the 2024 bank statements — through
[`tools/redact-doc/`](../../../tools/redact-doc/).)_

### ① ⛔ NOT ZERO — 113,157, and its attached statement carries no breakdown

**The 2024 return carries an attached statement for Schedule K line 16d.** Its entire content is:

| `Line 16d, Schedule K (1120S) — Distributions` | |
|---|---|
| **1 Cash** | **113,157** |
| **Total distributions** | **113,157** |

🔑 **That is the whole of the statement as it extracts.** One line, one caption, no breakdown of what
the 113,157 was made of. ⚠️ **Scoped to the search that produced it** *(`method.md` rule 1b)*: this is
the **2026-09-14 re-read of `2024 ZAKOMINCORPORATED.pdf` through the redactor**, and §1's standing
caveat binds it — **pages 15 and 18 produce no text and 11 tokens are masked `[GLYPH]`**, so *"no
further breakdown was extracted"* is what can be said. ⛔ **An earlier draft said "itemises NOTHING…
established by reading it, not inferred from its absence" — §1 forbids exactly that, and it is
withdrawn.** 🔑 **What is solid: the statement that DID extract carries one caption and one figure, so
the breakdown is not in the place the return puts it.**

### ② ⚠️ WHAT IS NEW — the Schedule L split is CORROBORATED (not proven), and the ban on stating the mechanism STANDS

**§3A and §3I ③ both said the identity `113,157 − 51,988 = 61,169` was exact but the MECHANISM was
inferred, and forbade stating it.** The re-read settles the column assignment arithmetically:

| Schedule L | Beginning | End |
|---|---|---|
| Line 22 Capital stock | 1,000 | 1,000 |
| **Line 23 Additional paid-in capital** | 🔑 **no value extracted — read as ZERO** ⚠️ *an absence in this extraction, not a proven blank* | **(61,169)** |
| **Line 24 Retained earnings** | **(19,527)** | 🔑 **ZERO** |

⚠️ **CORROBORATED — and an earlier draft of this section said "PROVEN", which is FALSE. Withdrawn.**
Each column does balance to its own reported total, exactly:
- **Beginning:** `11,923 + 86,337 + 32,500 + (1,000 + 0 − 19,527) = 112,233` — **2024's opening total assets.**
- **End:** `4,623 + 169,863 + 130,500 + (1,000 − 61,169 + 0) = 244,817` — **2024's closing total assets.**

⛔ **BUT THAT ARITHMETIC CONSTRAINS ONLY THE SUM OF LINES 23 AND 24, NOT THE SPLIT BETWEEN THEM.**
Beginning it fixes `L23 + L24 = (19,527)`; end it fixes `L23 + L24 = (61,169)`. 🔑 **The opposite
assignment balances identically** — beginning APIC `(19,527)` / retained earnings `0`, end APIC `0` /
retained earnings `(61,169)` — **and tells the opposite story.** The balancing cannot tell them apart,
so it cannot be the proof.

✅ **What DOES point at the split is a source this section had not used: Schedule M-2 itself.** §3A
records M-2 **opening −19,527 and closing 0**, which is the retained-earnings column's shape, not the
paid-in-capital column's. ⚠️ **Corroboration, not proof** — for an S corporation with no accumulated
E&P the AAA and retained earnings routinely coincide but are not the same account.

🛑 **AND THE BAN STAYS IN FORCE — an earlier draft narrowed it by REASONING, which is exactly what
CLAUDE.md forbids.** §3A and §3I ③ do not say *"until an argument is good enough"*; they say **"do not
state it as what the preparer did WITHOUT ASKING HER."** 🔑 **The release condition is an ASK, and
nobody has asked.** ⛔ **So: the identity is exact, the split is corroborated, and what the preparer
did with it is still not statable.** ☑️ **§6 carries the question for Lilian.**

### ③ ⚠️ THE AMEX IS **NOT** ELIMINATED — an earlier draft said it was, and it tested the wrong quantity

⛔ **An earlier version of this section concluded *"the distribution is not the Amex"*. WITHDRAWN, for
two reasons the review caught.**

| 2024 Amex | |
|---|---|
| **CHARGES, net of credits, per the Year-End Summary** | **68,850.00** ⚠️ *(two cross-foots of the SAME document — the eight category "Total Spending" figures sum to it, and the category subtotals give 71,865.10 − 3,015.10 = the same. **Cross-foots, not independent corroboration**; an earlier draft called this "verified two ways")* |
| Implied payments *(charges + opening 11,923 − closing 4,623)* | ≈ **76,150** ⛔ **both inputs are contested** — ⚠️ 🆕 *(§3K ② as NARROWED 2026-09-15 — the 4,623 is document-confirmed; only the 12/19-versus-12/31 convention survives, §3R ⑧)* §3J ④ cause 1 says **4,623 is a RETURN figure, not a statement balance**, and leaves it unproven that the 2024 line is even this card |
| **PAYMENTS actually leaving the bank, May–Dec 2024 only** | ≈ **102,242** *(⑥ — approximate in BOTH directions, with a downward bias; **not a strict floor**)* |
| **The distribution** | **113,157** |

🔑 **WHY THE ELIMINATION FAILED: it compared the distribution against what the card CHARGED, when the
residual theory in ⑤ is built from what left the BANK.** A residual is assembled from **outflows**.
⛔ **So the quantity to test is the bank's Amex payments — and those reach ≈102,242 in EIGHT MONTHS,
within ≈11,000 of 113,157.** ⚠️ **So the Amex is NOT eliminated.**
⛔ **And it may NOT be called "the closest candidate" either — an earlier draft did, and that is an
absence reported as a fact.** 🔑 **The Amex is the ONLY 2024 counterparty this file has ever
TOTALLED.** §3E ⑦-⑧ list the others — BMO, Macy's, Financial Pacific, the SBA, the Fifth Third card
and line, the `CK`/`SV`/`CL`/`CC` transfers — **by payment COUNT only**, and 2025's analogue shows a
`ZAKOM 7807` flow of 1,001,294.18 never measured for 2024 at all. **Nothing can be ranked against a
set that was never enumerated** *(`method.md` rule 1b)*.

🛑 **AND ③ AND ⑥ CANNOT BOTH BE READ AS MEASUREMENTS OF THE SAME THING — they differ by ≈26,000.**
The implied-payment row says ≈76,150 for **twelve** months; ⑥ says ≈102,242 for **eight**.
🔑 **The paper does not treat that as a contradiction to resolve, because it is the FINDING:** the two
measure different things — what **this card** would need, against what **actually left the bank to
Amex** — and the gap between them is §3J ④'s unexplained excess in its 2024 form, appearing in 2024 as well
as 2025. ⛔ **Which of the two is the better number is NOT settled**, and neither may be keyed.

### ④ 🔑 THE BANK STATEMENTS — eight months tie EXACTLY, and four still cannot be read

**All twelve 2024 statements were fetched. Eight opened; January–April were refused by the
redactor's guard again** — the same `NNN.NN … NNNN` money-column false positive as in September
*(§6, row 12)*. ⛔ **Not bypassed.**

**Every month that opened ties to the cent against the bank's own Account Summary**, and December
closes at **19,010.25** — the 2024 Schedule L cash and the 2025 opening. **The parse is anchored.**

| May–Dec 2024 | |
|---|---|
| Checks | **45,780.88** |
| Withdrawals / debits | **574,173.80** |
| **Total out** | **619,954.68** |
| Deposits / credits | **637,942.57** |

⛔ **Eight months cannot settle a twelve-month figure**, and the four missing months are the gap.
**113,157 cannot be rebuilt from what can be read.**

### ⑤ ⓘ THE MOST LIKELY METHOD — marked as INFERENCE, and it is the same method Lilian just described · ⛔ 🆕 **BUT §4 DECISION 22 BARS IT FOR 2025** *(§3U ④: 76% of the 2025 outflows is one block that turns out to be driver payments, so a residual would sweep most of a million dollars into the capital account)*. **This section describes what the PRIOR preparer most likely did; it is not what this return will do.**

ⓘ *Stated from reasoning, not read off any document.* **A preparer with no books derives the
distribution as a RESIDUAL** — total cash out, less the uses that are identified as the company's
(deductible expenses, loan principal, asset purchases). What is left went to the owner.

🔑 **Two things support it, and neither is proof.** ① It is exactly the construction Lilian described
for the balance sheet on 2026-09-14 *(§4 decision 12)* — *"no lo vas a encontrar en un documento;
tenemos que construirlo nosotros"*. ② **A residual does not know about the AAA**, which is
consistent with 113,157 overshooting an AAA of 51,988: a figure derived from the *bank* cannot be
capped by a figure derived from the *return*. ⛔ **What the preparer then DID with the excess still
may not be stated — §3M ② and §6 carry that question for Lilian.**

🛑 **WHAT THIS MEANS FOR 2025, and it is the practical point:** if the 2025 distribution is built the
same way, **the ruling of §3K makes it BIGGER, not smaller** — every payment to a non-company card
and every transfer to a non-company account is now an unidentified use of company cash, which is
exactly what a residual sweeps up *(§3K ③)*.

### ⑥ ⚠️ AND ONE THING THE 2024 BANK DATA SHOWS THAT 2025 ALREADY SHOWED — the Amex payment gap is STRUCTURAL

**Amex payments leaving the bank in May–Dec 2024 alone: ≈ 19 payments, ≈ 102,242**, against
**68,850.00 of charges for the WHOLE year.** ⚠️ *The payment figure is from a row-level parse that recovers ≈ 89% of the statements' own debit
totals and is **column-misaligned in places**.* ⛔ **The coverage gap biases it LOW; the misalignment
can push a single row EITHER way — so it is approximate in BOTH directions, with a downward bias.**
*(An earlier draft called it simply "a FLOOR", which the misalignment does not support.)*

🔑 **Even as an approximation the direction is unmistakable, and it is the same shape §3J ④ found in
2025** *(payments 123,786.73 against charges 111,231.98)*. **So the payment-versus-charge gap is not
a 2025 anomaly — it recurs.** ⚠️ **It is consistent with §3J ④'s cause 4** *(some payments went to another Amex account — the
shareholder's own, or Mema Colors')*. ⛔ **But it is NOT differential evidence for it, and an earlier
draft claimed it was.** 🔑 **§3J ④ cause 2 — the Year-End Summary is on a CHARGE-PROCESSING basis
while a balance is on a BILLING basis, which the document states on its own page 2 — is a structural
property of the document type, so it PREDICTS recurrence.** A gap that recurs is what cause 2 expects.
⚠️ **And the two years are not the same shape:** 2024 is ≈33,392 of excess on 68,850 of charges
*(≈49%)*; 2025 is 12,555 on 111,232 *(≈11%)*. ⛔ **Cause 3 was ELIMINATED on 2026-09-14, so TWO causes
survive beside cause 4, not three** *(§3J ④)*.

## 3N · 📨 MEMA COLORS IN 2024 — what the return actually did, and what "the same treatment" means

_(Lilian, 2026-09-14: **"Si el cliente dijo eso acerca de Memacolors, yo entiendo que se le va a dar
el mismo tratamiento que en 2024. Revisa qué pasó en 2024."**)_

### ① ✅ THE ANSWER IS SHORT — 2024 did exactly ONE thing with it

**In the 2026-09-14 redactor extract of the 2024 return, `Mema` matches in exactly ONE place** —
⚠️ *scoped to that extraction, whose pages 15 and 18 produce no text (§1)* — the statement behind
Schedule L line 6:

| `Line 6, Sch L (1120S) — Other Current Assets` | Beginning | End |
|---|---|---|
| **1 Loan to Mema Colors LLC** | **100,000** | **100,000** |
| Total other current assets | 100,000 | 100,000 |

✅ **And `Mema` matches ZERO times across the eight 2024 bank statements that could be read
(May–Dec).** ⛔ **January–April were refused by the redactor's guard and were NOT searched** — nothing
is reported absent for those four months.

### ② 🔑 SO "THE SAME TREATMENT AS 2024" IS A COMPLETE INSTRUCTION — and here is what it is

**2024 carried the loan at 100,000, unchanged, as an OTHER CURRENT ASSET, and touched nothing else.**
⚠️ **And nothing else about it surfaced anywhere in that extract — no income, no expense, no
interest, no write-off, no bad debt, nothing on page 1 and nothing on Schedule K.** ⛔ **Scoped, not
absolute:** §3A records that page 1 lines 9–12 and 16–19 *"are all blank in the extracted text"* with
**column alignment unreliable**, so this is *"nothing was extracted"*, not *"nothing was reported"*. So the same treatment in 2025 is: **Schedule L line 6, `Loan to Mema Colors LLC`,
100,000 opening and 100,000 closing, with its own attached statement — and no other entry anywhere
on the return.**

### ③ ⚠️ THREE THINGS THAT TRAVEL WITH IT — none of them blocks the return

1. **The client's words are about ACTIVITY; the return's line is a BALANCE.** *"No activity in 2025
   again"* is consistent with the balance being unchanged and **does not state it**. ✅ **But it is
   now corroborated from two sides**: nothing moved in 2024 either, and Mema appears in no 2024 bank
   statement. **So carrying it at 100,000 is the position the evidence supports**, and asking him is
   confirmation rather than a blocker.
2. 🔴 **A related-party loan carrying NO interest is a real exposure, and 2024 reported none.**
   ⓘ *From knowledge, not read off the return:* **§7872** imputes interest on a below-market loan
   between related parties. ⛔ **Nothing in this file establishes the terms of this loan — whether it
   is documented, whether it bears interest, or when it is due.** **A candidate item for the signer
   under §4C, not something to change on our own.**
3. ⚠️ **A "current" asset that has not moved in two years is not current.** ⓘ *Also from knowledge.*
   **2024 classified it as an OTHER CURRENT ASSET.** ⛔ **Copy the classification — it is the
   analogy — and note the question rather than reclassifying it silently.**

  maxed-out 35,000 revolving facility is exactly the fact §163(j) asks about. ⓘ *(from knowledge)*
- **The twelve statements would give the year's interest EXACTLY**, cycle by cycle, which no other
  source does — ⛔ **if they could be read.**

a figure at all and only a **floor**. 🔑 **So: not a defect. A CONVENTION — and see ⑧ below, because
the convention is worth more to this return than the verdict is.**

**Three things follow, and all three are retractions this paper owes:**

### ⑥ 🛠️ HOW THESE WERE READ — a real capability, and ⚠️ it carries NO redaction

⛔ **The firm's tool cannot read them and that has not changed:** `tools/redact-doc/` refuses a scan
with no text layer *(exit 2)*, correctly — there is no text to redact. **All twelve 2025 statements
and `Dec Amex balance stmnt.pdf` have been unreadable to it since 2026-09-13.**

✅ **What works:** the scans are **JPEG images inside a PDF wrapper**, and **`pypdfium2` is already
installed in this environment**. Rendering each page and reading it as an image is exact — page 1 and
the transaction-detail pages both came back fully legible, and **every subtotal on both statements
cross-footed against the figures already in this paper.**

🛑 **AND THE WARNING, because this is the opposite of the redactor in every respect.** ⛔ **A rendered
page carries the identity block in full — the account number, the shareholder's home address, the
payment coupon's scan line.** **The redactor's three controls — never print the text, mask the
identity block, delete the download — ALL FAIL HERE, because none of them is in the path.**
🔑 **So the handling is by hand and by rule, not by tool:**

1. ✅ **Only when Lilian or Julia asks**, and **the ask is the permission** *(CLAUDE.md, 2026-09-04)*.
   ⛔ **Never from a subagent. Never from a scheduled or unattended session.**
2. ⛔ **The rendered files go to the session scratchpad, NEVER the repo working tree, and are deleted
   when the reading is done.** ⚠️ 🆕 **THIS IS THE ONE CONTROL IN THIS RULE THAT IS BY HAND, AND IT HAS
   NOW FAILED THREE TIMES — ALL THREE ON 2026-09-15, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM FOUND BY AN UNRELATED SWEEP
   RATHER THAN BY THE SESSION THAT OWED THE DELETE.**
   - ① **106 rendered pages** sat in the scratchpad after the reading was done and committed; an
     independent review found them. **Deleted on discovery.**
   - ② 🔴 **Worse, because it was two days old: the client's own BOOKS WORKBOOK**, downloaded
     2026-09-13, was still there on 2026-09-15. **Deleted on discovery.**
   - ③ 🔴 🆕 **NINETEEN REDACTOR EXTRACTS — 1.1 MB in `scratchpad/zakom/`**: three years of Amex
     statements, four finance contracts, two lender letters with their amortization schedules, and
     **both prior-year returns.** Written 2026-09-13/14, still on disk 2026-09-15, found while
     sweeping after a merge. **Deleted on discovery, together with three empty render directories and
     the rendered markdown pages beside them.**

   - ④ 🔴 🆕 **2026-09-28, AND IT IS THE WORST OF THE FOUR: `scratchpad/ret/raw.txt` — the FULL TEXT OF THE
     KEYED 1120-S, UNREDACTED — had been on disk since 2026-09-27**, written by the session that reviewed the
     draft *(§3AW)*, **beside a `safe.txt` that was only masked by a throwaway script.** ⛔ **That session
     wrote §4F, which tells the next one to transcribe a draft in full, and never deleted its own extract.**
     **Found by the routine sweep at the end of THIS session's reads. Deleted on discovery.**
   - ⑤ 🟠 🆕 **2026-09-28: an old `pr392.diff` in the scratchpad root carried FOUR VINs.** **Deleted on
     discovery.**

   🛑 🆕 **SO THE CONTROL HAS NOW FAILED FIVE TIMES AND NEVER ONCE BEEN CAUGHT BY THE SESSION THAT OWED THE
   DELETE.** 🔑 **The pattern is not carelessness about the rule — every one of these sessions knew it. It is
   that the delete is owed at a moment nobody is looking: the reading is over, the finding is written, and
   the session has moved on to the write-up.** ✅ **What actually worked, twice now, is a SWEEP — walk the
   whole scratchpad for SSN, VIN and street-line patterns before finishing, not just the directory you
   created.** ☑️ **This session did that and it is why ④ and ⑤ were found.**

   ⚠️ **③ IS THE MOST INSTRUCTIVE, AND IT IS NOT ABOUT RENDERING AT ALL:** these are the **redactor's own
   output**, so the sessions that left them were the ones using the safe tool correctly — *"it came out of

   🔴 **AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS ITEM SAID "THE IDENTITY BLOCK WAS NEVER THE EXPOSURE, BECAUSE THE
   EXTRACTS ARE REDACTED." ⛔ THAT IS WITHDRAWN — THIS PAPER DISPROVES IT IN TWO PLACES, AND AN INDEPENDENT
   REVIEW HAD TO POINT AT THEM:**

     return is one of the nineteen files that sat on disk for two days.**
   - [`tools/redact-doc/README.md`](../../../tools/redact-doc/README.md) records the cause as confirmed and
     **unfixed**: the street-name token class requires a leading letter, **so any street whose name is a

   ✅ **What survives of the point, stated at its real scope:** the extracts carry the redactor's **NARROW**

   text** — ⛔ **but "the identity block was
   safe" is exactly the false comfort this item exists to warn about, and I wrote it.** 🔑 **Three for

   widens it: not only a render MODE, but whether the tool should own the lifetime of its OWN output, in a

   ☑️ **⚠️ AND NOTE WHAT 2bis BELOW ALREADY DID, because ③ is not evidence that the RULE is missing:** the

✅ **TWO checks against the documents, and both pass on all thirteen:** every row **cross-foots** to
its own New Balance, and every **Previous Balance equals the prior statement's New Balance.**
⚠️ **The year's identity —** `4,623.02 + 104,743.84 + 414.00 + 7,001.63 − 79,861.01 = 36,921.48` **—
is NOT a third check: it is FORCED by those two.** If every cycle cross-foots and every link joins,
the sum telescopes to that identity and cannot fail. ✅ **It verifies this session's own addition of
the columns, and nothing more.** 🔑 **Said plainly because an earlier version called it "three

## 3T · ✅ THE MATCH IS DONE — 39 bank debits against 20 card payments, and it RECONCILES TO THE DOLLAR

_(🆕 **2026-09-15, and it needed NOTHING from the client.** Lilian asked why the firm was requesting
bank statements it already had. **It was right to ask — they were on file and readable.** The twelve
Fifth Third statements were **re-read from Double through [`tools/redact-doc/`](../../../tools/redact-doc/)**,
every `AMEX EPAYMENT` debit extracted with its date and amount, and matched one by one against the
twenty payments the card's own statements record. ⛔ **Every earlier figure in §3J ④, §3Q ③bis, §3Q ④,
§3R ③ and §3S ④ is superseded by this.**)_

### ① ✅ FIRST, THE STRING-MATCH CAVEAT IS RESOLVED — the 39 are real and they total exactly

§3E ② warned that **123,786.73 was a STRING MATCH** — *"39 lines whose description contained `AMEX`,
not 39 payments somebody identified"* — and §3S ④ carried that as the floor's weakest input.
✅ **Re-read today: 39 lines, every one captioned `AMEX EPAYMENT ER AM ACH PMT`, summing to
123,786.73 to the cent.** ⛔ **The caveat is discharged, not merely repeated.**

### ② 🔑 THE MATCHING RULE, and it is the document's own behaviour rather than an assumption

**A card payment is initiated at Amex and pulled from the bank afterwards**, so the card's posting
date comes **first** and the bank's debit follows. ✅ **Nineteen pairs match on amount with a lag of
+1 to +4 days, and every single one falls in that window** — **+1 day on thirteen of them**. 🔑 **A
pattern that tight across nineteen independent pairs is the rule being right, not a coincidence
being fitted.**

### ③ 🔴 THE RESULT — and it balances exactly, which no earlier version of this reconciliation did

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| ✅ **MATCHED** — a bank debit 1–4 days after the card posted the payment | **19 debits** | **71,680.55** |
| 🔴 **UNEXPLAINED** — left the bank as `AMEX EPAYMENT`, **never reached this card** | **18 debits** | 🔴 **46,344.31** |
| ⏳ **IN TRANSIT** — after the 12/19 close, so they SHOULD post in the 01/19/26 cycle *(12/23 · 12/29)* | **2 debits** | **5,761.87** |
| 🔑 **TOTAL** | **39** | ✅ **123,786.73** |

⚠️ **71,680.55 + 46,344.31 + 5,761.87 = 123,786.73 is an ADDITION CHECK, NOT A CORROBORATION.** The
three buckets **partition the same 39 debits by construction**, so the sum holds whatever the matching
says — move one debit from MATCHED to UNEXPLAINED and the identity is untouched. ⛔ **An earlier
version of this paragraph called it *"the check every earlier attempt claimed and none of them had"*,
which is precisely the error §3S ① was written to purge, reintroduced one section later.**

✅ **THE CHECK THAT IS REAL IS THE CARD SIDE, and it crosses two independently produced document sets:**

| The card's own twelve statements | | |
|---|---|---|
| Payments the card received in calendar 2025 | **20** | **73,010.55** |
| ✅ of which MATCHED to a bank debit | 19 | **71,680.55** |
| 🔴 **received with NO bank debit behind it** *(03/01 — ⑥ below)* | **1** | **1,330.00** |

🔑 **The bank side and the card side are counted separately and the residual is a single named
payment.** ✅ **It also closes §3S ④'s open upward boundary** — that section allowed the card's 01/10
payment of 2,000.00 might answer to a **December 2024** bank debit, which would have widened the gap.
**The card side now accounts for every 2025 payment, so it does not.**

⛔ **So the answer is 46,344.31 — EXACT on the thirty-seven debits whose disposition is established, and a FLOOR with respect to the one that is not** *(the uncorroborated 12/29 debit of 3,000.00, which would make it 49,344.31 — see the in-transit caveat below)*. **37% of everything that left
the company's bank account captioned as an Amex payment did not reach the company's Amex card.**

⚠️ **THE IN-TRANSIT BUCKET IS AN ASSUMPTION, NOT A MEASUREMENT — say it here rather than let the word
"exact" cover it.** Nothing tests that those two December debits reached this card: **the 01/19/2026
statement has never been read.** ✅ **The 12/23 of 2,761.87 is corroborated — it equals this
statement's own minimum due exactly** *(§3Q ②)*. ⛔ **The 12/29 of 3,000.00 has none, and 3,000.00 is
the MODAL amount among the eighteen** *(three of them in ④'s large-round list)*. 🛑 **If it went where
they went, the figure is 49,344.31.**

⚠️ **AND ONE BOUNDARY ITEM MOVED TOWARD THE FINDING — which has to be said, because §3S ④'s whole
discipline was that every adjustment be taken AGAINST it.** §3S ④ deducted the **12/15 debit of
494.88** as one that could still post in the 01/19/26 cycle; **this section counts it among the
eighteen.** 🔑 **The reason is ②'s own rule: a 12/15 bank debit implies a card posting 12/11–12/14,
inside the 12/19 cycle, and §3R ④ reads that cycle payment by payment and does not find it.**
⛔ **Defensible — but it is this section's one adjustment in its own favour, and §3R ④ still carries
the opposite sentence** *(marked there)*.

⚠️ **AND THE TIE-BREAK RULE, because equal amounts are everywhere in this data.** The card took **five**
payments of exactly 3,000.00, three of 2,000.00, three of 1,000.00 and two of 300.00; the unexplained
list adds three more 3,000.00s and two 5,000.00s. **Pairing was nearest-forward, first-unused, one
debit to one payment.** ✅ **The bucket TOTALS do not depend on the assignment — equal amounts are
interchangeable.** ⛔ **But the DATE this section publishes for an individual unexplained debit does**,
and ⛔ **nothing here tests whether one card payment could have been funded by TWO debits, or one debit
have covered two payments.**

### ④ 🔑 AND THE 18 HAVE TWO CLEAN SHAPES — which is itself evidence

| Pattern | Count | Total | What it looks like |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Small, roughly MONTHLY** — 400.00 · 248.19 · 237.82 · 250.00 · 500.00 · 721.42 · 370.00 · 622.00 · 494.88 | **9** | **3,844.31** | 🔑 **The signature of a SECOND Amex account's minimum payment** — one small, varying amount a month, Feb and May through December. ⚠️ **MARCH AND APRIL ARE ABSENT**, and a second card's minimum would normally appear every month — named because the shape is the evidence |
| **Large and ROUND** — 4,000 · 5,000 · 8,000 · 3,000 · 3,000 · 10,000 · 5,000 · 1,500 · 3,000 | **9** | **42,500.00** | Paydowns on something |

⚠️ **Read as evidence, not as a conclusion.** **Nine near-monthly small payments to an Amex that is
not this card is the shape a second card makes**, and §3J ④ cause 4 named exactly that possibility —
*"the shareholder's own Amex, as readily as Mema Colors'."* ⛔ **Nothing here says WHICH**, and this
paper does not guess. ✅ **But "some of the 39 went to another Amex account" has moved from a
candidate to a measured 46,344.31 across 18 dated debits.**

🔴 **April is the concentration: 19,000.00 unmatched across four debits** *(5,000 on 04/11, 8,000 on
04/16, 3,000 on 04/21, 3,000 on 04/23)* — **the same month the 34,000 went through.**

### ⑤ ✅ THE 34,000 QUESTION IS CLOSED — it DID come from the company's account

⚠️ **FIRST, A READER TRAP: there are TWO unrelated 34,000s in this paper.** **This one is an APRIL card payment.** **The other is the client's own note that the totalled truck's 34,000 of towing was paid in cash from the line of credit, on a truck totalled 2025-06-01** *(§4 decision 18)*. ⛔ **Different months, different matters, no connection established between them.**

**§3S ⑥ made this the highest-value read left on the client.** ✅ **Answered: the bank debits
34,000.00 on 04/11/2025, one day after the card posted it on 04/10.** ⛔ **The hypothesis that it
came from outside the company — which would have made the gap larger — is eliminated.**

### ⑥ 🔴 AND ONE THING RUNS THE OTHER WAY: 1,330.00 REACHED THE CARD FROM OUTSIDE THE COMPANY

**The card posted a payment of 1,330.00 on 03/01/2025. There is no `AMEX EPAYMENT` debit for it in
any of the twelve bank statements, and none of that amount among the AMEX-captioned lines extracted
today.** ⚠️ **NAME THE SEARCH, NOT THE WORLD** *(`method.md` rule 1b)*: **only the AMEX-captioned lines
were extracted** *(⑧ ③)*, so the statements were **not** searched for a 1,330.00 under another caption.
⛔ **An earlier version of this sentence said *"no debit of that amount at all"*, which claims a search
nobody ran.**

🔑 **It is also the TWENTIETH card payment** — the one ③'s card-side table leaves over. **So it is not
a curiosity sitting beside the reconciliation; it is the reconciliation's closing item.**

shareholder receivable · or money from one of the other accounts — which under decision 11 is not the
company's either, so it lands as one of the first two.** ⛔ **WHICH, and from whom, is NOT established,

and §3E ④ records inbound money from them — so *"not this checking account"* does not yield *"the
shareholder"*, and an earlier version of this paragraph made exactly that jump.**

✅ **What holds either way: it moves the capital account in the OPPOSITE direction from the
46,344.31**, and it is a second, independent sign that this card and this bank account are not a
closed system.

### ⑦ ℹ️ WHAT AN "SBA EIDL" IS — because Lilian asked and the paper never said

**SBA = the U.S. Small Business Administration. EIDL = Economic Injury Disaster Loan.** It is a
**federal loan**, made directly by the SBA — not by a bank — to businesses hit by a declared disaster.
**Almost every EIDL on a 2020-vintage balance sheet is a COVID one**: 30-year term, **3.75%** for a
business, no collateral under 25,000, and repayment deferred for the first years — which is why the
payments are small and why the balance barely moves.

**Here:** six payments in 2025, **1,272.00** — 159.00 recurring plus one 477.00.
✅ 🆕 **ANSWERED 2026-09-15 — §3V ②: the payments are 100% interest and 0% principal, so the balance is held **while the deferred-interest BACKLOG lasts — a condition that can END** *(§3V ②; an EIDL is not an interest-only product)*. Nothing is asked of the client, and the 2024 presentation was correct.** _(As written:)_ 🔴 **And the prior year is what makes it a question: the 2024 return reported 32,500 in BOTH
columns** — the same balance at the start and the end of a year in which payments were made.
**§3I ④ calls that a likely prior-year defect.** ☑️ **What we need is one number: the principal
balance at 12/31/2025** — and the SBA publishes it in the borrower's own portal, so the client can read
it off a screen rather than request a letter.

### ⑧ ℹ️ WHAT "IPFS" IS — and which insurance

**IPFS is a premium finance company.** The mechanism, because it is not obvious: a commercial trucking
policy is billed as one large annual premium. **IPFS pays the insurer the whole premium up front, and
the business repays IPFS monthly with interest.** So the money leaving the bank each month is
**part insurance, part interest on a loan** — and there is a **balance owed to IPFS at 12/31**.

**Here:** a **10.00** debit in September *(a verification charge)*, then **3,138.90 in each of October,
November and December** — **9,426.70** in 2025, starting in the fourth quarter.

☑️ **WHICH insurance: not established, and §3E ⑧ records the relationship as new in 2025.** ⚠️ **The
2024 return does not answer it either** — there is no IPFS line anywhere in it and no IPFS debit in the
2024 statements, which is consistent with the policy being **new in 2025**. 🔑 **So it is not a document
we already hold and failed to read; it is one that does not exist yet on our side.**
⛔ 🆕 **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-27 — §4 decision 53 / §3AH ① row 3: the ASK IS A 2025 YEAR-END STATEMENT from
IPFS, not the agreement. The agreement states the WHOLE CONTRACT's SCHEDULED finance charge, and on the
CASH basis this return deducts what was PAID in 2025.** ☑️ **Take the agreement as well — it carries the
amount financed, the payment count and the APR, so it lets us amortize if no statement exists.**
_(As written:)_ ☑️ **The document to ask for is the IPFS PREMIUM FINANCE AGREEMENT** — one page, and it carries all
three things at once: the **total premium financed** *(which is the insurance expense)*, the **finance
charge** *(which is interest)*, and the **balance at 12/31/2025** *(which is a Schedule L liability)*.

### ⑨ ✅ AND TWO INTEREST LETTERS WE DO NOT NEED TO ASK FOR — the notes let us compute them

**Both AP Equipment notes are on file in full** — amount, rate, term and payment — and both amortize
to within a few cents of their stated payment — **1,983.00 against a computed 1,983.07, and 1,271.17
against 1,271.14** — ⚠️ **which checks only that the LOAN, RATE and TERM were read correctly off the note. It does NOT check the payment COUNT or the start month — and those come from the bank, and are what the interest and the balance actually turn on**:

| Note | Loan | Rate | Term | Payment on the note | Payment the formula gives |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `160544` — the **1839** | 95,550.00 | 8.99% | 60 | **1,983.00** | ✅ **1,983.07** |

### ② ✅ THE SBA EIDL IS SETTLED — and §3I ④'s "likely prior-year defect" is DISPROVED BY DOCUMENT

**Two SBA statements**, dated **12/17/2024** and **01/17/2025**, both for the same loan, `Loan Type:
Disaster COVID-19 Economic Injury` — ✅ **which confirms §3U ⑦'s account of what an EIDL is.**

| | 12/17/2024 | 01/17/2025 |
|---|---|---|
| Last payment amount | 159.00 | 318.00 |
| 🔴 **Applied to PRINCIPAL** | 🔴 **0.00** | 🔴 **0.00** |
| ✅ **Applied to INTEREST** | 159.00 | 318.00 |
| 🔑 **Outstanding balance** | 🔑 **32,500.00** | 🔑 **32,500.00** |

🔴 **ON BOTH STATEMENT DATES THE ENTIRE PAYMENT WAS APPLIED TO INTEREST AND NOTHING TO PRINCIPAL, AND THE BALANCE IS THE SAME ON BOTH.**

⚠️ 🆕 **AND THE MECHANISM MUST BE STATED CORRECTLY, BECAUSE AN EARLIER VERSION OF THIS SECTION CALLED IT "AN INTEREST-ONLY LOAN" AND THAT IS WRONG.** **An EIDL is NOT an interest-only product** — §3U ⑦ describes it correctly: a 30-year term at 3.75% with **repayment deferred for the first years**, *"which is why the payments are small and why the balance barely moves"*. 🔑 **What these statements show is a BACKLOG being worked off**, and ② below carries the evidence: `Payment Due` of **477.00** and **318.00** against a monthly statement amount of **159.00** — three months and two months owed at once. ⚠️ **On a 32,500 balance at 3.75% the year accrues about 1,219, and 1,272.00 was paid — MORE than a year's accrual, and still nothing reached principal.** ⛔ **That is only explicable as deferred interest being caught up, not as a product feature.**

🛑 **WHY THE DISTINCTION MATTERS AND IS NOT PEDANTRY: the balance does not move WHILE THE BACKLOG LASTS — a condition that can END — rather than by design, which could not.** ⛔ **A future session must not roll 32,500 forward year after year on the strength of this paragraph.**

✅ **SO THE 2024 RETURN IS RIGHT AND THIS PAPER WAS WRONG** — ⚠️ **at the exact scope the documents reach.** **These two statements are dated 12/17/2024 and 01/17/2025, so they evidence the CLOSING column.** ✅ **The OPENING column is corroborated independently by the client's own 2023 sheet** *(§3G ③: SBA loan 32,500, 2023 interest 1,749, at 159.00 monthly)* — **a different source, and it agrees.** 🔑 **The retraction survives on either column alone.** §3I ④ called *"32,500 in both columns
after a year of payments"* **more likely a prior-year defect than a convention**, and §3U ⑪ asked the
client for the balance on that basis. ⛔ **Both are withdrawn.** **A loan whose payments are entirely
interest reports the same balance at both ends of the year because that is what happened.**

✅ **WHAT THE 2025 RETURN TAKES, and it needs nothing from the client:**
- **Schedule L, the SBA line: 32,500.00 at 12/31/2025**, unchanged from the opening column.
- **Page 1 line 13: the 1,272.00 the bank paid in 2025** *(§3E ④ — six payments, 159.00 recurring plus
  one 477.00)*, **all of it interest.**

⚠️ **TWO THINGS MARKED, because this is an inference about 2025 drawn from 2024 documents:**
① **Neither statement covers 2025.** The mechanism is fixed and the balance has not moved in the years
these two statements straddle, **but a 2025 statement would settle it outright** — ☑️ *worth having if
it costs nothing, not worth asking for on its own.*
② ⚠️ **HE IS BEHIND.** The statements show `Payment Due` of **477.00** and **318.00** against a monthly
statement amount of **159.00** — **three months and two months owed at once** — and 2025's six payments
total eight months' worth. ⓘ *That is a cash-flow observation, not a return item: on the cash basis only

### ③ 🔴 `46622-1M` IS **CHANNEL PARTNERS**, NOT MITSUBISHI — a lender nobody had inventoried, and the guess is dead

§3O row 5 placed `Zakom Incorporated 46622-1M Interest Statement 9.9.24.pdf` beside **Mitsubishi**, and
**flagged the placement as a guess.** ✅ **The flag was right and the guess was wrong.**

**The document is from `CHANNEL PARTNERS LLC`** *(the letterhead reads `CHANNEL — YOUR PARTNER IN
FUNDING`)*, and it is a complete life-of-loan interest statement:

| Contract `46622-1M` · funded **6/14/2021** · financed **50,000.00** | Interest paid | Principal paid |
|---|---|---|
| 2021 | 2,129.28 | 8,913.50 |
| 2022 | 2,975.12 | 15,955.36 |
| 2023 | 1,516.14 | 17,414.34 |
| **2024** | **170.90** | **7,716.80** |
| 🔑 **Principal balance 12/31/2023** | | 🔑 **7,716.80** |

⚠️ **FIRST, THE CHECK THAT LOOKS LIKE ONE AND IS NOT:** `financed − Σ principal paid = balance` is **FORCED** on any internally consistent amortization statement. ✅ **It confirms the transcription and that no year is missing** — ⛔ **it does NOT confirm the lender, which came from the letterhead.** *(Decision 21 flags exactly this move about BMO.)*

✅ **THE REAL CHECK IS IN THE SAME FOUR ROWS, and it is much stronger — add each year's interest to its principal and you get the CASH PAID:**

| | Interest | Principal | **Total paid** |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2021 | 2,129.28 | 8,913.50 | **11,042.78** |
| 2022 | 2,975.12 | 15,955.36 | **18,930.48** |
| 2023 | 1,516.14 | 17,414.34 | **18,930.48** |
| 2024 | 170.90 | 7,716.80 | **7,887.70** |

🔑 **18,930.48 ÷ 12 = 1,577.54 EXACTLY.** ✅ **1,577.54 × 7 = 11,042.78** *(2021, exact)* · ✅ **1,577.54 × 5 = 7,887.70** *(2024, exact)*. ⇒ **7 + 12 + 12 + 5 = 36 payments**, and **36 × 1,577.54 = 56,791.44**, less the 50,000.00 financed = **6,791.44** — ✅ **exactly the sum of all four interest rows.**

🔑 **THAT PROVES WHAT THE CROSS-FOOT CANNOT: a genuine 36-MONTH contract at 1,577.54 a month, no year missing, retiring at its 36th payment in MAY/JUNE 2024** — not merely "during 2024". ✅ **THREE independent arithmetic identities** — ⚠️ **the fourth (36 × 1,577.54 − 50,000 = Σ interest) FOLLOWS from the first three plus the cross-foot this paragraph itself calls forced, so it is their consequence, not a fourth check.**

✅ **And the forced one, for completeness:** `8,913.50 + 15,955.36 + 17,414.34 = 42,283.20`, and `50,000.00 − 42,283.20 =
7,716.80` — **exactly the stated 12/31/2023 balance, to the cent.**

🔑 **AND THE 2024 ROW IS THE WHOLE ANSWER: principal paid in 2024 = 7,716.80 = the entire remaining
balance.** ⛔ **THE LOAN WAS PAID OFF DURING 2024.** ✅ **So it is not a 2025 item at all** — no 2025
interest, no 12/31/2025 balance, nothing to ask anyone for, and **no payments to look for in the 2025
bank statements** *(§3U ② found none, which now makes sense)*.

🔴 **THREE THINGS FOLLOW, AND THE THIRD IS THE UNCOMFORTABLE ONE:**
1. ✅ **Channel Partners is a relationship the firm did not know existed** — §3O's inventory had twelve
   and this is a **thirteenth**, closed in 2024. ☑️ **Added to §3O.**
2. ⛔ **MITSUBISHI IS NOT HELPED.** The one document that might have carried its rate and term belongs to
   somebody else. ☑️ **§3U ⑪ ask #4 survives and is now the only route to that contract.**
3. ⚠️ **IT BEARS ON THE 2024 OPENING COLUMN, and this paper cannot test it.** Channel Partners was
   **7,716.80** at 12/31/2023 and **zero** at 12/31/2024; the 2024 return's Schedule L line 20 closed at
   **169,863** as a single aggregate *(§3I ④)*. ⛔ **Nothing here contradicts that** — a paid-off loan
   correctly contributes nothing to a closing balance — **but it does mean the 2024 line-20 aggregate was
   built from a set of contracts this paper has never fully enumerated.** ⓘ *Recorded, not chased:
   decision 15c holds 2024 as the immovable base.*

## 3X · ✅ "WHERE DOES MITSUBISHI COME FROM?" — traced to its sources, and the reason it is invisible is that THERE ARE TWO BENSON TRAILERS

_(🆕 **2026-09-15. Lilian:** *"no veo ningún Mitsubishi en los libros de la compañía, en el tax return,
dentro de los fixed asset… No he encontrado ningún documento relacionado con Mitsubishi. ¿De dónde sacas
eso?"* 🔑 **The right question, and the same shape as the `FPL` error — so it was traced from scratch
rather than defended.**)_

### ① ✅ IT IS NOT AN INFERENCE — TWO INDEPENDENT SOURCES FOR THE LENDER, AND A THIRD FOR THE ASSET

| Source | What it says |
|---|---|
| **The CONTRACT** — `2022 Benson trl Contract__9827076-001.pdf`, Double, 2025 organizer | ✅ **Names `Mitsubishi HC Capital America` SEVEN times** *(re-read through the redactor 2026-09-15)*. Rate **9.99%**, financed **44,250.00** |
| **The BANK** — the Aug–Dec 2025 statements | ✅ **The word `MITSUBISHI` appears 27 times across those five statements**, and **the contract number `9827076` 21 times.** ✅ **All five 943.54 debits carry `MITSUBISHI` in their own caption** |
| **THE CLIENT'S OWN ASSET LIST** — the image Lilian sent the same day | ✅ **Row 5: `2022 Benson trailer · Aug-25 · 43,650`.** ⚠️ **THIS ROW DOES NOT NAME MITSUBISHI** — it evidences **the trailer it finances**, not the lender. ✅ **What it does add is a genuine cross-document tie: 43,650 is the contract's own invoice figure** *(§3H ③)* |

🔑 **AND THE FPL TEST WAS RUN EXPLICITLY THIS TIME.** The `FPL` error happened because a short string
matched the wrong payee *(§3U ⑤)*. ✅ **Here the caption contains the lender's FULL NAME, not an
abbreviation.** ⛔ **There is no room for a collision of that kind.**

⚠️ **AND THE TWO SUPPORTING POINTS ARE SCOPED, because this paper has been wrong about exactly this:**
- ⛔ **The contract number in the caption does NOT independently confirm the PAYEE** — it sits in the SAME
  caption field as the name, so it cannot disagree with it. ✅ **What it confirms is WHICH Mitsubishi
  agreement**, which is a real and different job.
- ⚠️ **"`MHC` appears 0 times in the contract" is a negative from an INCOMPLETE extraction** — pages 12–13
  of 16 barely extracted *(§3W ④)*, and [`method.md`](../../pre-return-review/method.md) rule 1b says a
  negative belongs to the search that produced it. ✅ **It is belt-and-braces: the conclusion rests on the
  FULL NAME being present, not on an abbreviation being absent.**

### ② 🔑 SO WHY CAN IT NOT BE FOUND? — THREE REASONS, AND THE THIRD IS THE REAL ONE

**1. ⛔ It cannot be on the 2024 tax return, and it is not supposed to be.** **The trailer was acquired
in AUGUST 2025** *(the client's own list)*. ✅ **A 2025 acquisition appears on the 2025 return, not the
2024 one.**

**2. ⛔ It cannot be in the client's books either.** §3E and §3F record that **his balance sheet has no
equity section and ZERO fixed assets**, and his P&L is built by category, not by vendor. ⛔ **No
lender's name appears anywhere in those books** — not Mitsubishi's, not BMO's, not AP Equipment's.

**3. 🔴 AND THE ONE THAT ACTUALLY EXPLAINS THE CONFUSION: THERE ARE TWO BENSON TRAILERS, AND THE ONE ON
THE 2024 RETURN IS THE OTHER ONE.**

| | On the **2024** depreciation schedule | The **2025** addition |
|---|---|---|
| Description | **`2025 Benson`** | **`2022 Benson trailer`** |
| In service | **9/25/2024** | **2025-08-01** |
| Cost | **67,050** | **43,650** |
| Financed by | ⚠️ **not established** — ③ below | ✅ **Mitsubishi HC Capital America**, contract `9827076-001` |

🔑 **Anyone opening the 2024 return's fixed assets, looking for a Benson trailer, finds the `2025
Benson` at 67,050 — a DIFFERENT trailer, bought a year earlier, at a different cost, with a different
lender.** ✅ **That is exactly what it looks like when Mitsubishi "is not there", and the trailer that
IS there is not this one.**

⚠️ **The model year in the name is not the purchase year, and on this client it is routinely NOT** —
the `2026` Reitnouer was bought in **2025**, the `2022` Benson in **2025**, the `2023` Cascadia `1839`
in **2025**, and the `2025` Benson in **2024**. 🔑 **Reading a purchase year off a description has
already caused one wrong conclusion on this return** *(the "2022 trailer whose payments start in
August" anomaly — §3W ②)*. ⛔ **Do not do it.**

America` on 14 September to the client: *"Per your request, please find your 2025 year end totals."*

| | |
|---|---|

prefix is withheld here**, on the same convention that withholds the Fifth Third loan account suffix
*(§1 row 30; [`FOLLOW-UPS.md`](../../../FOLLOW-UPS.md) row 113)*.

| | |
|---|---|
| Five payments Aug–Dec 2025, 943.54 each *(§3U ② row 6)* | **4,717.70** |
| less the interest the lender states | **(1,823.59)** |

| plus the balance the lender states | **41,355.89** |

🛑 **AND THE LIMIT OF THAT CHECK, STATED AT ONCE, BECAUSE §3U ⑩ ALREADY CARRIES IT AND A FIRST DRAFT OF THIS SECTION CALLED IT *"not a figure taken on trust"*:** ⛔ **THIS IDENTITY HAS NO POWER OVER THE INTEREST FIGURE.** Given the two inputs, `balance = 39,532.30 + interest` **identically** — one equation, two unknowns — so the identity **DETERMINES THE BALANCE once the interest is given** and therefore **cannot test the interest at all.** ✅ **The ⓘ note at the end of this subsection proves it by exhibiting a second, WRONG pair — the model's 1,793.62 / 41,325.92 — that foots to the cent on the same identity.** ✅ **What the check DOES establish, and it is real:** the financed amount, that exactly **five** payments were applied, that **no other principal moved**, and that the balance is a **12/31** figure rather than a current one. ⚠️ **The INTEREST rests on the lender's word** — which §4 decision 8 makes the authority in Julia's own words, so nothing turns on it.

🔑 **AND THAT SETTLES THE QUESTION THE CLIENT HIMSELF ASKED, without waiting for the lender to answer
it.** In the same thread Oleg wrote back seven minutes later: *"is it a principal balance – $41,355 as
of 12/31/2025 or as of today?"* ⇒ ✅ **It is 12/31/2025.** Only **five** payments are accounted for in
the arithmetic above; ⓘ *for scale only, do not key:* re-amortizing to September 2026 is **fourteen** payments and a balance near **35,700**. **The
email says so too** — *"your 2025 year end totals"* — so the document and the arithmetic agree, and they
are independent of each other.

⚠️ **AND IT RETIRES §3W ④'s ESTIMATE, which was close enough to be dangerous.** The paper carried *"of
the order of 1,800"* and *"around 41,400"*, both marked **do-not-key**. The real figures are **1,823.59**
and **41,355.89** — the interest estimate **23.59** out and the balance estimate **44.11** out. ⚠️ *(An earlier version said "within thirty dollars" of both; that is true of the MODEL figures, which are 29.97 out on each, not of the rounded estimates quoted here.)* ✅ **So the reading behind the estimate was right**
*(9.99% on 44,250, roughly sixty months, starting August 2025)*. ⛔ **The estimate is now SUPERSEDED and
must never be keyed; the lender's figures govern.**

ⓘ **Why the model was 29.97 out, and why it is out by the SAME amount on both figures:** the cash pins
them together — interest **minus** balance must equal `4,717.70 − 44,250.00 = −39,532.30`, and the model
*(1,793.62 − 41,325.92)* and the lender *(1,823.59 − 41,355.89)* **both satisfy it exactly**. ✅ **So the lender charges a little more interest
than a flat 30-day model.** ☑️ **And the likely mechanism is named rather than left as a shrug: an
ACTUAL/360 count**, which grosses a 9.99% nominal rate up by 365/360 to about **10.13%** — the same

read off the contract**, whose payment pages did not extract *(§3W ④)*. **It is also why the real payment
is 943.54 where a flat model computes 939.96.**

⇒ ✅ **Page 1 line 13 takes 1,823.59.** ⚠️ 🆕 **BUT THE BALANCE HALF IS NOW §6'S OPEN RULING, NOT SETTLED: [`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/) §1B.1 puts an ENDING BALANCE with the CLIENT, and his cell says 41,355.00. ### ⑤ 🛠️ AND THE REDACTOR LET ANOTHER STREET LINE THROUGH — instance SIX, and it is the documented bug

Reading the BMO letter, `redact.py` reported **`0 street lines`** and the company's street line came
through into the output.

🔑 **IT IS THE DOCUMENTED CAUSE, CAUGHT LIVE: the street name is a NUMBER**, and the tool's street-name
token class requires each token to begin with a **letter**
*([`tools/redact-doc/README.md`](../../../tools/redact-doc/README.md) — "the gap is ANY street whose name
is a number")*. ⛔ **The line is written nowhere. Recorded by existence only.**

handed straight into a session rather than fetched from Double** — the route §3R ⑥ item 2 was narrowed
for on 2026-09-15. 🔑 **And it lands the day after [#403](https://github.com/JuliaJKAcct/JKAccountingGroup/pull/403)
recorded this same tool's output sitting undeleted for two days.** ✅ **These four documents' extracts
were deleted in the pass that read them.**

## 3AA · 🔴 THE DECEMBER 2025 FIFTH THIRD STATEMENT — the balance NEVER MOVED. It was the RATE, and §3V ⑦'s reading is WITHDRAWN

_(🆕 **2026-09-17. Lilian sent two images the client passed her.** ⛔ **The first is the Mitsubishi
year-end email, already read and keyed on 2026-09-15** *(§3Y ①, decision 26)* — **not new.** 🔑 **The
second is the document ask #1 has been specifying for two days: the DECEMBER 2025 `COMMERCIAL LOAN
STATEMENT`.**)_

### ① ✅ WHAT IT SAYS

| | |
|---|---|
| Statement date | **December 26, 2025** |
| **`CURRENT PRINCIPAL BALANCE`** | 🔑 **98,000.00** |
| **`CURRENT RATE`** | **9.20000** |
| `MATURITY DATE` | **00/00/00** — *a revolving line, no maturity* |
| `PRINCIPAL DUE` · `INTEREST DUE` · `TOTAL PAYMENT DUE` | **0.00** · **782.50** · **782.50**, due **January 1, 2026** |
| Previous payment due · payments | **769.71** · **769.71** |

**Transaction summary, three rows:**

| Processed | Effective | Description | Interest | Total |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 12/01/25 | 12/01/25 | `Auto BillPayer PYMNT` | **769.71** | **769.71** |
| 12/10/25 | **10/29/25** | **`NEW ACCRUAL`** | — | 🔑 **9.45000000** |
| 12/10/25 | **12/10/25** | **`NEW ACCRUAL`** | — | 🔑 **9.20000000** |

⛔ **The loan account number, the customer and invoice numbers, the payment-coupon scan line and the
company's street line are on this document and are written NOWHERE** *(§1 row 30's convention;
[`FOLLOW-UPS.md`](../../../FOLLOW-UPS.md) row 113)*.

### ② 🛑 THE BALANCE IS 98,000.00 — SO §3V ⑦'s CENTRAL FINDING IS WRONG, AND THIS PAPER SAID IT PLAINLY

§3V ⑦ reads: ***"SO THE 12/31/2025 BALANCE IS NOT 98,000, AND THIS PAPER MUST NOT KEY IT AS IF IT
WERE"***, and inferred *"roughly 16,000 was DRAWN"* in December. ⛔ **Both are WITHDRAWN.** **The
lender's own statement, five days before year end, prints `CURRENT PRINCIPAL BALANCE 98,000.00`.**

✅ **AND §3V ⑦'s OWN HEDGE IS WHAT SAVES IT** — it never claimed to know which:

> *"two unknowns are tangled: the implied balances assume the rate held at **9.95%**, and ⛔ the rate is
> known to move on this facility… **What is PROVED is that the balance or the rate changed after
> September; which, and by how much, is not.**"*

🔑 **The answer is the RATE.** ✅ **That hedge is why this correction is a completion rather than a
retraction, and it is the argument for writing hedges that way.**

### ③ ✅ THE PROOF IS EXACT, AND THE STATEMENT SUPPLIES BOTH HALVES OF IT

**The payment due 1 January 2026 is 782.50. The statement's own two `NEW ACCRUAL` rows give the rate
and the date it changed. On a balance of exactly 98,000.00, actual/360:**

| | |
|---|---|
| December 1–9 at **9.45%** *(9 days)* | **231.53** |
| December 10–31 at **9.20%** *(22 days, the `NEW ACCRUAL` effective 12/10/25)* | **550.98** |
| **TOTAL** | ✅ **782.50** — **the statement's figure, to the cent** ⚠️ *(unrounded 231.525000 + 550.977778 = 782.502778 → 782.50. The DISPLAYED components add to 782.51 — the total is right, the two-decimal display is what rounds)* |

⇒ 🔑 **The identity confirms a SET jointly — balance 98,000.00, day count actual/360, and the two rates —
and it is ONE test, not three.** ⚠️ 🆕 **Corrected on review 2026-09-17** — *an earlier wording called these "three
independent things", which is the same counting error §3Z ⑤ corrects two sections earlier.* 🔑 **The
balance and both rates are PRINTED on the document; the identity does not establish them, it checks them.
The only thing genuinely INFERRED is the day-count convention.**

### ④ 🔑 EVERY ACCRUAL THE LENDER RECORDS NOW FITS — the implied rate walks DOWN a monotone curve on a CONSTANT balance ⚠️ *(not every BANK debit: the 12/01 944.71 is the one that does not, which is ⑤)*

**Back out the rate each debit implies, holding the balance at 98,000.00:**

| Debit | Days | Implied rate |
|---|---|---|
| 01/31 · 02/28 · 04/01 · 05/01 · 05/30 · 07/01 · 08/01 · 08/29 *(the eight §3V ⑥ already matched)* | 28 / 30 / 31 | **9.95%** ×8 |
| **10/01** 803.06 | 30 | **9.8334%** |
| **10/31** 818.57 | 31 | 🔑 **9.7000%** — *a clean two-decimal rate* |
| **12/01** 769.71 *(the lender's figure)* | 30 | **9.4250%** |
| **12/31** 782.50 | 31 | **9.2725%** *(the blended 9.45 / 9.20 of ③)* |

✅ **9.95 → 9.83 → 9.70 → 9.43 → 9.27 is a rate-decline curve**, which is what a prime-linked line does
through an easing autumn. ⛔ **§3V ⑦'s competing reading — a constant 9.95% on a falling balance
(≈96,900 / ≈95,500 / ≈91,300) — is arithmetically possible and is EXCLUDED BY THE DOCUMENT**, which
prints 98,000.00 on 12/26/25. ⚠️ **A mid-year excursion that returned exactly to 98,000 is not
excluded by arithmetic — it is excluded by having no evidence and no need: the rate curve explains
every debit without it**, and §3V ① already pinned 98,000 at 12/27/2024 and 01/27/2025.

### ⑤ 🔴 AND IT SURFACES A DISCREPANCY THE PAPER MUST NOT SETTLE FROM HERE — the 12/01 debit

| | |
|---|---|
| What §3V ⑦ recorded as the **bank** debit on 12/01 | **944.71** |
| What the **lender's statement** says it received on 12/01 | **769.71** |
| **Difference** | 🔴 **175.00 — exactly round** |

🔑 **AND THE RATE CURVE PICKS A SIDE.** 944.71 over 30 days on 98,000 implies **11.5679%** — ⛔ **a rate
RISE, against a monotone decline, and the only outlier in twelve debits.** 769.71 implies **9.4250%**,
which sits exactly where the curve wants it.

⚠️ **That is evidence, not proof, and the paper does not get to close it from a rate curve.** ☑️ **Three
readings, none established:** the December bank statement was misread; the 944.71 debit is **not this
loan** *(§3V ⑤ found the twelve by caption, and §3U ⑤'s `FPL` error is exactly what a caption match can
do)*; or the bank really debited 944.71 and **175.00 of it is a fee or a rider the lender booked
elsewhere.**

☑️ **The check is free and in-house: re-read the DECEMBER 2025 bank statement** *(already read once —
§3T)* **and look at what actually left on 12/01.**

⚠️ 🆕 **AND ONE THING IN ④ DOES NOT TIE — found on review, 2026-09-17, and left OPEN.** The
statement's own rate history says **9.45% effective 10/29/25**. A full November on a constant 98,000
actual/360 at that rate is **771.75** — but the lender says it received **769.71**, **2.04 short of its
own documented rate.** 🔑 **The statement supplies the likely reason in its two date columns: the 9.45%
row reads `Processed 12/10/25, Effective 10/29/25` — a BACKDATED rate change — so November was billed
at the rate then on the books and the 9.45% was applied retroactively.** ⚠️ **Which means ④'s
"implied rate" column silently mixes BILLED rates (Sep–Nov) with EFFECTIVE ones (Dec), and the paper
does not say so.** ⛔ **Not resolved here, and it does NOT rescue the 944.71: a retroactive true-up from
10/29 to 11/30 is worth about 2.25, not 175.00.** ☑️ **Nor does it disturb the constant balance —
holding 9.45% flat, 769.71 would need a balance of ≈97,741, which would then have to return to exactly
98,000 by 12/26 with no evidence and no need** *(④)*. ☑️ **Worth one line to the lender if anyone
ever calls them; not worth a client question, and NOT a §6 row** — 🔑 **a 2.04 item that moves no
return line: the deduction is interest PAID, and 769.71 is what the lender says it received whatever rate

⚠️ 🆕 **BUT IT DOES NOT DECIDE THE INTEREST FIGURE — added on review, 2026-09-17, because this

|---|---|---|

| ② the 944.71 is not this loan | 8,986.05 *(eleven debits)* + 769.71 = **9,755.76** | ⚠️ **not necessarily another PAYEE** — the twelve were found by CAPTION, and §3E ⑧ lists a Fifth Third credit CARD, so a collision could be another facility of the same bank. **If so the 175.00 could still reach line 13, as a DIFFERENT component** |
| ③ 944.71 left, 175.00 is a fee or rider | **9,755.76** | a deductible cost, but **not on line 13** |

need a FOURTH reading — that the LENDER'S OWN STATEMENT is wrong — and nothing here asserts it; the
