there is no form for him in EITHER year UNDER ANY NAME RESEMBLING HIS HEADING, and the 2024 folder gives the
firm no corporate name IDENTIFIABLE AS HIS.**

🔑 **ON THE *EITHER-YEAR* CLAIM, ALWAYS CARRY THE QUALIFICATION — either "no MATCHING form" or "no form under any name RESEMBLING his heading".** ⚠️ **This governs the EITHER-YEAR claim only.** ☑️ **The bare *"no 2025 form on file"* needs no qualifier: ②'s five-for-five pairing with NO unmatched form left over is a CLOSED SET, which is a different and stronger argument than resemblance alone.** ⛔ **"He has no form in either year" is NOT what
was established and may not be written** — **across the TWO folders, where three 2024 recipients are unmatched,**
a listing of filenames can only show that **no form is filed under
a name that resembles his heading.** ⚠️ **The two sentences "he has no form" and "this does not exclude him"
cannot both be true, and an earlier draft of this section put them side by side on five surfaces.**

⛔ **IT DOES NOT EXCLUDE HIM, AND THE RESIDUAL IS ALL THREE — NOT ONE OF THEM.** A driver the client lists as
`Jose` could trade under a name that resembles nothing, and **ANY of `Winder Thius`, `Push Trucking` or
`Metayer` could be his: by the table above, NONE of the three resembles ANY of the six driver headings.**
⛔ **Do not single one out as "the company-looking one"** — that reads a corporate character off a filename,
which is exactly what the rule below forbids, **and the 2024 filenames carry no corporate stem at all, so a
bare name there is not evidence of a person.** ☑️ **①'s ordinary reading stands** *(the company stopped
paying three contractors and started paying two)*, **and the only thing that tests it is §3E ④'s statement
search**, which is already queued.

🔴 **AND THE CHECK TURNED UP SOMETHING ② HAD NOT ALLOWED FOR — NEITHER YEAR'S FILENAME MAY BE READ AS
EVIDENCE OF CORPORATE STATUS.** 🔑 **The three recipients common to both years are named with NO corporate
stem at all in 2024** — `1099 Bovtruck.pdf`, `1099 Roscheh.pdf`, `1099 Fritziam.pdf` — against 2025's
`Bovtruck Inc`, `Roscheh Inc`, `Fritziam Logistics LLC`. ⚠️ **"Same recipients" is itself the RESEMBLANCE
method again, not an identity the listing proves** — *(a further reading nobody has excluded, sitting OUTSIDE the
three set out below: the similarly named 2024 and 2025 files could be DIFFERENT payees)*. ☑️ **Taking them as the same: same firm, different
naming — and
a folder listing cannot say WHICH naming is the recipient's legal name, or whether the recipient's OWN NAME
CHANGED between the two years.**

⚠️ **TAKING THEM AS THE SAME RECIPIENTS, THREE READINGS FIT THE SAME LISTING, NONE OF THEM ESTABLISHED, AND THE PAPER MUST NOT PICK.**
☑️ **(a) 2025 carries the legal names and 2024 ABBREVIATES** — under which the 2025 filenames really do
describe the payees. ☑️ **(b) 2025 adds a corporate stem the recipient may not have.** ☑️ 🆕 **(c) THE
RECIPIENT'S OWN NAME CHANGED — the payee INCORPORATED between the two years, in which case BOTH filenames are
right and neither year misnames anyone.** 🔑 **(c) is not exotic on this client — it is what the client's own
note is ABOUT** *("there are some people didn't have corp name")*: **a payee who had no corporate name and then
acquired one is precisely (c).** ⚠️ *(①'s trade-name reading is NOT offered as support here — a trade name is
a different phenomenon from a change of legal name, and conflating them was an earlier draft's error.)*
⛔ **A folder listing separates none of the three.**

⛔ **TWO EARLIER DRAFTS OF THIS PARAGRAPH ARE WITHDRAWN:** the first asserted (b) as established; the second
asserted that **at least one year must be misnaming the recipient**, which needs the unstated premise that the
legal name was the SAME in both years — **and (c) is exactly the case where it was not.**

🔑 **WHAT SURVIVES ALL THREE, AND IT IS ALL THAT IS NEEDED: a filename is not the form, and only ONE of the
five has been opened** *(①)* — ⚠️ **and that one was read THROUGH THE REDACTOR, which suppresses the identity
block, so its recipient's NAME was never seen either.** ⛔ **So corporate status is not established for ANY of
the five — not for four of them.** ⛔ **Never infer corporate status from a filename on this client.**

⚠️ 🆕 **AND `LLC` IS NOT `CORPORATION` — a trap that matters in exactly ONE place.** Of the five 2025

elected to be TAXED AS A CORPORATION** — a disregarded or partnership-taxed LLC **must** get one ⚠️ *(stated
from knowledge, not verified against the current-year instructions)*. ⛔ **So even under reading (a), what the

have forms** — ⛔ **but it must NOT travel to ③ item 2 ①, which is the one place *"is he a corporation?"*
decides whether a form is OWED.**

☑️ **What survives of ② after this:** **five of the six drivers have a 2025 form on file and `Jose` does
not** — that is the finding, it is unaffected, and it is still what makes his organizer note consistent
rather than contradictory. ⚠️ **What is weaker than ② first put it:** the *corporate* half of the reading
rested on a filename, so ③ item 2's *"is he a corporation?"* is open for the OTHER FIVE too, not only for
him. ☑️ **Nothing on the return turns on it** — a 1099-NEC issued to a corporation is unnecessary but
harmless, and all five have forms.

## 3AD · ✅ THE THREE FREE IN-HOUSE SEARCHES, RUN 2026-09-27 — two of them REVERSE the paper's own finding, and the method is why

> **Lilian, 2026-09-27: *"haz las tres búsquedas gratis en los statements"*.** All twelve 2025 statements
> were re-fetched and re-read through [`tools/redact-doc/`](../../../tools/redact-doc/); **every extract was
> deleted in the same pass.** ⛔ **Nothing was asked of the client.**

### ① 🛑 THE METHOD FAILED, AND IT FAILED THE SAME WAY TWICE — read this before the findings

**Both open items rested on a search BY EXACT AMOUNT.** §3U ② grouped every 2025 debit by its value and
traced each recurring value to its months. ⛔ **That is blind to a lender who varies the draft** — a
documentation fee on the first payment, interim interest between funding and the first due date, a late
charge, a sales-tax adjustment. **The payment is there; the amount is not the one being searched for.**

🔑 **THE RELIABLE KEY IS THE CAPTION, NOT THE AMOUNT.** Every lender pays under its own fixed caption
all year, and the caption does not move when the amount does. ✅ **Both searches were re-run on the
caption, and both reversed.**

⚠️ **This is [`method.md`](../../pre-return-review/method.md) rule 1b in a new place:** a negative belongs
to the search that produced it, and *"nine payments, not twelve"* was a fact about an amount query, not
about the bank account.

| Month | Date | Amount | |
|---|---|---|---|
| January | 01/15 | **1,146.51** | 🔴 **missed by the amount search** |
| February | 02/18 | **742.11** | 🔴 **missed by the amount search** |
| March – November | 15th–18th | 1,011.71 × 9 | found before |
| December | 12/15 | **1,149.74** | 🔴 **missed by the amount search** |
| **Total paid in 2025** | | 🔑 **12,143.75** | *(twelve at 1,011.71 = 12,140.52 — **3.23** more)* |

✅ **Every one carries the identical caption** — `Financial Pacifi LEASE PYMT 001-…-302` — **all twelve
months, no gaps.** ☑️ **So the schedule assuming TWELVE 2025 payments is CORRECT.**

✅ **KEY 3,198.13 of interest and 18,837.47 as the 12/31/2025 balance.** ⛔ **§3Y ③'s and §3U ⑤'s
*"DO NOT KEY"* is LIFTED, and the *"nine where the schedule assumes twelve"* finding is WITHDRAWN.**

✅ 🆕 **AND IT WAS CLOSED THE SAME DAY — §4 DECISION 50, LILIAN: the `2023 Utility Trailer` is asset #6 of
the eight on the 2024 depreciation schedule, in service 11/11/2022, at 100% business use.** ⛔ **A true
lease is never on a depreciation schedule, so the firm's own filed position already answers it.**
🔑 **The 3,198.13 IS interest and the 18,837.47 IS a Schedule L liability, unconditionally.**
⚠️ **The ruling covers the `2023` trailer only — the `2015 Utility TRL` is a different asset with no
contract at all** *(§3F)*. _(As written, before she ruled:)_ ⚠️ **ONE THING THIS DOES NOT CLOSE, and it is
a different question:** the caption says **`LEASE PYMT`**, and §3H ③ter records that this lender's product
**may not be a loan at all**. ⛔ **If it is a true lease, the 3,198.13 is not interest and the 18,837.47 is
not a Schedule L liability** — the whole payment would be rent. **That is untouched by this search and
stays open.**

bank)* **or reading 4** *(paid on the card or by the owner — decision 38)*. ⚠️ **Still not picked.**

### ⑤ 🔴 AND THE SWEEP FOUND SOMETHING NOBODY HAD MEASURED — an IRS monthly withdrawal

✅ **`IRS USATAXPYMT` — 365.00, ELEVEN months (January–November) = 4,015.00.**

🔑 **The client TOLD the firm this on 2026-09-14** — §3L records him saying he is paying a **2024 balance
to the IRS by automatic monthly withdrawal**. ⛔ **Nobody had ever measured it**, and it has no row anywhere
in this paper.

⛔ **A PAYMENT OF A PRIOR-YEAR TAX BALANCE IS NOT DEDUCTIBLE.** ☑️ **So two things have to happen:**

1. **Check whether the 4,015.00 is inside the client's own expense base.** If it is, it comes OUT — and
   that moves page 1 line 20.
2. ⚠️ **Establish WHOSE balance it is, because it decides the treatment.** An S corporation normally pays
   no federal income tax, so a *company* balance is more likely payroll or excise than income tax — **and a
   payment of the SHAREHOLDER's personal tax out of the company account is a DISTRIBUTION**, which lands in
   the very figure §6 is trying to build. ⛔ **Not established.**

🛑 **AND HERE IS THE PART A FIRST DRAFT OF THIS SUBSECTION GOT WRONG — IT WROTE *"Ask"*, WHICH LILIAN HAS
ALREADY RULED AGAINST. §4 DECISION 14 PARKED THIS EXACT SUBJECT** *(2026-09-14: "vamos a dejarlo pendiente.
Eso fue parte de su declaración personal y, por ahora, no nos interesa")*. ⛔ **The measurement does not
reopen her ruling, and no reasoning in this paper may** *(the standing rule: a permission is widened by
asking, never by reasoning)*. ☑️ **What it does is SPLIT the matter in two, and only ONE half was ever
hers:**

- **Half ①, whose balance it is → STAYS PARKED.** That is the question decision 14 answered, and it stays
  written down for one reason only: if it is the shareholder's, the payment is a **distribution**.
- **Half ②, whether it sits inside the client's own expense base → WAS NEVER PARKED, AND IT IS OURS.**
  ⛔ **It is not a question about his personal return at all** — it is whether **page 1 line 20 of THIS
  return** carries a deduction that is not allowable. **Decision 14 could not have weighed it, because the
  figure did not exist when she ruled.**

☑️ **AND HALF ② HAS A FREE NARROWING, RUN HERE.** ✅ **His `Total expenses` reconciles to the DOLLAR against
named captions with NO residual bucket and NO `Taxes` caption** *(§5A ② — 1,094,423 + 17,401 + 24,750 +
102,834 + 27,248 + 6,857 = 1,273,513)*. 🔑 **So if the 4,015.00 is in his base at all, it is inside a NAMED
caption, not in an "other" line.** ⚠️ **The one caption whose own name reaches a tax is `IFTA/HUT/Permits`,
and the federal heavy highway vehicle use tax (Form 2290) IS an IRS tax paid to the IRS** — ⛔ **but IFTA is
a state agreement and `HUT` may equally be a STATE highway-use tax, so this is WHERE TO LOOK FIRST, not an
identification.** ⛔ **And `Automobile and truck expenses` is broad enough to hold anything, so elimination
does not close it either.**

### ⑥ ✅ THE 12/01 DEBIT IS SETTLED — and it explains the ENTIRE 175.00

✅ **December carries TWO line-of-credit debits:** **12/01 — 944.71** and **12/31 — 782.50**, both
`PAID BY AUTO BILLPAYER`. ☑️ **The bank's 12/01 figure is 944.71.** *(§3V ⑦ recorded 944.71; §3AA ⑤
recorded the lender crediting 769.71. Both readings were right — they are different quantities.)*

🔑 **944.71 − 769.71 = 175.00** — and **that one item is the entire difference between what left the bank
across the year and what the lender credited to interest.**

⛔ 🆕 **AND A SECOND "CONFIRMATION" IS WITHDRAWN ON REVIEW, because it was a FORCED IDENTITY.** An earlier
version added *"and the whole-year gap is the same number: 9,930.76 − 9,755.76 = 175.00"* **as though it
were independent.** 🔑 **It cannot be: §3AA ⑤ DERIVED 9,755.76 as `9,930.76 − 944.71 + 769.71`, so the
year-gap and the month-gap are the same subtraction written twice.** ⚠️ **This is the trap §3Z ④ names in
its own words — an allocation cannot fail, so it is not a check.** ✅ **The conclusion still stands on the
evidence that IS independent: the lender's own December statement fixes the principal balance at 98,000.00
all year** *(§3AA ①–④)*, **so a 175.00 that is neither interest nor principal is a fee.** ⓘ *And note the
12/01 and 12/31 debits were already enumerated at §3V ⑦ — so "the 12/01 debit is settled" is a RE-READING
of a figure this paper held, not a discovery.*

✅ **And it is NOT principal:** §3AA established from the lender's own December statement that the
principal balance never moved from 98,000.00 all year. **A 175.00 that is neither interest nor principal
is a fee.**

☑️ **So §3AA's ruling stands and is now EXPLAINED rather than merely chosen: KEY 9,755.76**, and the
9,930.76 is the bank outflow including one 175.00 fee. ⛔ **§6's *"re-read the December statement"* row is
CLOSED.**

## 3AE · ✅ THE 2024 SCHEDULE L AND M-2, READ IN FULL — the three figures are PRINTED, and the identity is EXACT ⚠️ *(⛔ NOT "the mechanism is on the face of the return" — an earlier heading said that and it claims more than this section proves. The COLUMN ASSIGNMENT on lines 23 and 24 is still CORROBORATION, not proof — ③ below and §3M ② — and whether the preparer PARKED the excess in paid-in capital is a question to Lilian that she DEFERRED on 2026-09-27, decision 51.)*

> **Lilian, 2026-09-27: *"revisa también el Schedule L / M-2 de 2024"*.** The filed 2024 return was
> re-read through the redactor and **the extract deleted in the same pass.**

### ① ✅ SCHEDULE M-2, COLUMN (a) — the AAA, printed

| M-2 line | | |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Balance at beginning of tax year | **−19,527** |
| 2 | Ordinary income from page 1, line 22 | **77,424** |
| 3 | Other additions | *(blank)* |
| 5 | Other reductions | **5,909** |
| **6** | **Combine lines 1 through 5** | 🔑 **51,988** |
| **7** | **Distributions** | 🔑 **51,988** |
| **8** | **Balance at end of tax year** | ✅ **ZERO** |

✅ **−19,527 + 77,424 − 5,909 = 51,988**, exactly. ☑️ **And the 5,909 is identified**: Schedule M-1 line 3b
prints `Travel and entertainment $5,909` — **the nondeductible half, which is what an M-2 other reduction
is supposed to be.**

### ② 🔑 THE THREE PRINTED FIGURES, AND THE ARITHMETIC THAT TIES THEM

| Where | What it prints |
|---|---|
| Schedule K, line 16d *(and its attached statement)* | **113,157** — total distributions |
| Schedule M-2, line 7 | **51,988** — what ran through the AAA |
| Schedule L, line 23 — additional paid-in capital | **(61,169)** |

✅ **113,157 − 51,988 = 61,169**, exactly. ☑️ **The AAA absorbed as much of the distribution as it had and
went to zero; the remainder equals the paid-in-capital figure to the dollar.**

🛑 **AND THE BAN STANDS — do NOT write that as what the PREPARER DID.** §3A and §3I ③ bar stating the
mechanism as the preparer's act *"without asking her"*, and **nobody has asked.** ⛔ **What is statable is
what is above: three printed figures and an exact identity.** ☑️ **§6 carries the question for Lilian.**

### ③ ✅ SCHEDULE L — the closing column, which IS 2025's opening column

| Schedule L | Beginning 2024 | **End 2024 = OPENING 2025** |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Cash | 3,115 | **19,010** |
| 6 Other current assets | 100,000 | **100,000** |
| 10a Buildings and other depreciable assets | 383,709 | **536,259** |
| 10b Less accumulated depreciation | 374,591 → 9,118 | 410,452 → **125,807** |
| **15 Total assets** | 112,233 | 🔑 **244,817** |
| 18 Other current liabilities | 11,923 | **4,623** |
| 20 Notes payable, 1 year or more | 86,337 | **169,863** |
| 21 Other liabilities | 32,500 | **130,500** |
| 22 Capital stock | 1,000 | **1,000** |
| 23 Additional paid-in capital | — | **(61,169)** |
| 24 Retained earnings | (19,527) | — |
| **27 Total liabilities and equity** | 112,233 | 🔑 **244,817** |

✅ **Both columns balance exactly:** `3,115 + 100,000 + 9,118 = 112,233` and
`11,923 + 86,337 + 32,500 + 1,000 − 19,527 = 112,233`; `19,010 + 100,000 + 125,807 = 244,817` and
`4,623 + 169,863 + 130,500 + 1,000 − 61,169 = 244,817`.

⚠️ **THE COLUMN ASSIGNMENT ON LINES 23 AND 24 IS STILL CORROBORATION, NOT PROOF — §3M ② is unchanged.**
The extraction prints one value on each line and cannot say which column it sits in; what fixes the SUM is
the balancing, and what points at the split is M-2 line 1's **−19,527** opening, **which is the
retained-earnings shape.** ⛔ **An AAA and retained earnings routinely coincide for an S corporation with
no accumulated E&P and are still not the same account.**

### ④ 🔑 WHAT THIS GIVES 2025 — the answer to *"how do we build the distributions"*

✅ **THE 2025 AAA OPENS AT ZERO.** That is 2024's M-2 line 8, printed.

☑️ **So the 2025 sequence is fixed and there is nothing to guess about its SHAPE:**

1. **AAA = 0 + 2025 ordinary income − 2025 nondeductible expenses** *(the meals/entertainment disallowance
   is the 2024 analogue, §6's meals row)*.
2. **Distributions run through M-2 line 7 only to the extent the AAA carries them**, and line 8 cannot go
   below zero.
3. **Whatever the distribution exceeds that by has to go somewhere** — and in 2024 the excess equalled the
   paid-in-capital figure exactly.

🛑 **AND ONE THING GOES TO JULIA BEFORE 2025 REPEATS IT.** ⚠️ **Driving additional paid-in capital
NEGATIVE is not the ordinary treatment.** A distribution in excess of AAA **and of stock basis** is normally
a **capital gain to the shareholder**, reported on his own return — not a negative equity account on the
company's. ⛔ **2025 faces the same question with LESS room**, because the AAA opens at **zero** rather than
at −19,527 plus a full year of income. ☑️ **It is a presentation question for the signer, and it connects
to [`form-1120s-preparation.md`](../../sops/form-1120s-preparation.md) §5C-v** *(Julia's netting policy)*.
⚠️ **Stated as a question, not as a defect** — the 2024 return is the other principal's, and §4C's rule is
that a decision is confirmed or overturned, never dressed up.

## 3AF · ✅ THE REITNOUER LOAN AND SECURITY AGREEMENT, READ IN FULL — the 395.00 is SETTLED, the 262.85 is NOT, and one earlier reading of this very document was WRONG

> **Lilian, 2026-09-27: *"si necesitas leer el loan y security agreement del trailer Reitnouer, hazlo para
> que puedas responder las dudas que tienes."*** 🔑 **That is the ASK that opens the document permission**
> *(CLAUDE.md — the ask is itself the permission; it is a request each time and never a standing grant)*.
> `2026 Reitnouer Finance_Documents_ZAKOM_INCORPORATED_-_160562.pdf`, **14 pages**, read through
> [`tools/redact-doc/`](../../../tools/redact-doc/) and **deleted in the same pass.** ⛔ **Nothing was asked
> of the client.**

### ① ✅ THE 395.00 IS A DOCUMENTATION FEE, PAID IN CASH WITH THE FIRST PAYMENT — the document says it in words

**Page 11 is an INVOICE.** Its three lines are unambiguous:

| Invoice line | Amount |
|---|---|
| `Down Payments` | **0.00** |
| `Estimated Title & Registration Fees` | **N/A** |
| `Documentation Fee - Includes Inspection, UCC Lien Search if applicable` *(sales tax included)* | **395.00** |
| `Less down payment paid to Allegiant Partners` | **0.00** |
| 🔑 **`Amount Due With 1st Payment`** | 🔑 **395.00** |

✅ **So April's 1,666.17 is the first payment (1,271.17) plus the documentation fee (395.00).** ⇒
⛔ **It is NOT principal and NOT interest.**

🔑 **AND THAT SETTLES HALF OF §3AD ③'s OPEN 657.85 IN THE CLIENT'S FAVOUR.** **His 63,651.77 balance
reproduces nine REGULAR payments, and the 395.00 never touched principal — so it does not belong in his
balance and its absence is CORRECT, not a 395.00 understatement.** ⛔ **The "extra principal" reading dies
for the 395.00 and survives only for the 262.85** *(which would put the balance at 63,388.92, not the
62,993.93 §3AD ③ computed on the whole 657.85 — ⚠️ that figure is superseded, and it was also one cent
out: 63,651.77 − 657.85 = 62,993.92)*.

⚠️ **ITS OWN TAX TREATMENT IS NOT "INTEREST" EITHER.** A loan documentation fee is a **cost of obtaining
the loan**, which is capitalised and amortised over the loan term rather than deducted as interest.
⛔ **Stated from knowledge and NOT verified against the 2025 instructions.** ⓘ **At 395.00 over a 72-month
note it is immaterial to this return — about 5.49 a MONTH, so roughly 65.83 in a full year.** ⚠️ *(An
earlier version said "about 5.50 a year", which divided by the wrong thing.)* **So the honest handling is
to name it and not build a schedule for it.**

### ② 🛑 AND IT CORRECTS THIS PAPER: §3H ③'s *"395.00 doc fee financed"* IS WRONG

**§3H ③ records both AP notes as *"395.00 doc fee financed"*.** ⛔ **That reading came from a MANGLED
COLUMN HEADER.** The payment-schedule block's header row is

```
INSTALLMENTS   PAYMENT AMOUNT   FIRST PAYMENT DATE   INTEREST RATE   FEE   PURCHASED
72             $1,271.17        <merge field>        8.99 %          $395.00   COMMERCIAL
```

and the words `DOCUMENTATION FEE FINANCED` sit **above** it in the extraction, wrapped out of position.
🔑 **The 395.00 is the `FEE` column's value, and page 11 states how it is paid: with the first payment, in
cash.** ⛔ **"Financed" was the extraction's layout, not the contract's term.**

✅ **THE BANK AGREES WITH THE CORRECTED READING, WHICH IS WHY IT MATTERS:** if the fee had been financed
into the 70,540, no 395.00 would have left the account — and one did, in April *(§3AD ③)*.

🔴 **AND IT OPENS A NEW, FREE QUESTION ON THE OTHER NOTE — `160544`.** **The same wrong reading was applied
to it**, and §3AD ③'s caption sweep recorded its nine 2025 debits at **1,983.00 each with no variation** —
**no 395.00 anywhere.** ☑️ **Two readings, neither established:** the two notes were papered differently
*(one fee financed, one billed)*, **or April's `160544` debit does carry the fee and the sweep recorded the
regular amount.** ✅ **The check is free twice over:** `1839 2023 FRGHT Finance_Documents … 160544.pdf` is
on file *(§1 row 15)* **and has an invoice page of its own**, and April's bank debit can be re-read.
⚠️ **It moves no interest figure** — a fee is neither interest nor principal — **but it moves `160544`'s
payment total, which is one of the six components of the 102,834 tie in §3AG.**

### ③ ⛔ WHAT THE DOCUMENT DOES **NOT** SETTLE — the 262.85, and it names no fee of that size

**There is no second fee anywhere in the 14 pages.** `Title & Registration` reads **N/A**; the
documentation fee is the only one; the down payment is **0.00**; and the `Disbursement & Prefunding
Authorization` shows the **whole 70,540.00 going to the supplier, `Interstate 365 LLC`** — so nothing was
held back to be collected later.

☑️ **WHAT IT DOES SUPPLY IS THE MECHANISM, and it favours ONE reading.** §1 of the Note charges interest
*"from the date of disbursement until paid"*, and the Authorization lets the lender **pre-fund the supplier
before the equipment is delivered.** 🔑 **So odd-days interest between disbursement and the first payment is
exactly what this contract produces** — and the trailer went into service **2025-03-05** with the first
payment on **04/10** *(§3AD ③)*.

⛔ **BUT IT DOES NOT COMPUTE, AND THIS PAPER WILL NOT PRETEND IT DOES.** On 70,540.00 at 8.99%, 262.85 implies
**14.92 days** on an actual/360 count *(15 days would be 264.23)* and **15.13 days** on a 365 count.
**No whole-day count reproduces it, and the document never states the disbursement date.**

⚠️ **A SECOND CANDIDATE THE DOCUMENT ITSELF CREATES, and it is weaker:** page 14 authorises the lender to
**force-place insurance and add its cost to the contract balance at the contract rate.** ⛔ **That would
show up inside the balance, not as a separate debit — so it fits the evidence less well.**

🔑 **A 2025 YEAR-END PAYMENT HISTORY / PAYOFF STATEMENT FROM ALLEGIANT PARTNERS (AP Equipment Financing)
ON NOTE `160562`** — 2025 **interest paid** and the **12/31/2025 principal balance**, from the lender's own
books. ✅ **Three things at once:** it settles the 262.85, it puts a LENDER document behind a figure that

☑️ **And ask for `160544` on the same request** — one email, two notes, and it closes ② as well.

⚠️ **IT IS NOT A BLOCKER.** ✅ **63,651.77 and 4,552 are keyable today** *(decision 8: the client's ending
balance governs)*, and the exposure is **262.85 of interest at most** — ⛔ **which is why this goes on the

### ② ✅ THE MID-QUARTER CONVENTION DOES **NOT** APPLY — and this had never been tested

🛑 **Why it matters more than it sounds: if more than 40% of the year's additions are placed in service in the
LAST THREE MONTHS, the mid-quarter convention replaces half-year for EVERY addition of the year** — which
moves every figure on Form 4562, not just the late one.

| Additions by quarter | |
|---|---|
| Q1 — `1837` 63,195 · `1839` 105,550 · `Reitnouer` 70,540 | 239,285 |
| Q3 — `1840` 115,550 · `2022 Benson trailer` 43,650 | 159,200 |
| 🔑 **Q4 — `2018 FRGHT Cascadia 146`, 2025-11-01** | 🔑 **23,000** |
| **Total** | **421,485** |
| 🔑 **Q4 share** | ✅ **5.46%** — nowhere near 40% |

✅ **AND IT IS ROBUST IN BOTH BRANCHES OF THE `1839` QUESTION:** if that asset is NOT a 2025 addition,
additions fall to **315,935** and the Q4 share rises only to **7.28%**. ⇒ **Half-year applies either way, so
the convention does not wait on the client either.**

⚠️ **The RULE is stated from knowledge and the threshold must be read off the 2025 Form 4562 instructions
before keying** *(this file's standing rule)* — **but the CONCLUSION does not turn on the reading: 5% against
40% is not a close call.**

ⓘ **AND NO ADDITION IS "LISTED PROPERTY":** these are trucks and trailers above 6,000 lb GVW, so the §280F
caps that §3A-bis ④ flags on the 2024 **AUDI** do not reach them. **The 2025 Audi Q6 is a LEASE, so not an
addition at all, and the Porsche is the vehicle the client calls personal** *(decision 5, Julia's)*.

**§3E ⑤ already recorded a plain `DEPOSIT` of 19,500 on 21 August 2025 with no counterparty**, and noted that
the client's asset sheet says the 2019 Audi was *"traded in for $19,500"* — **the amounts match.**

⚠️ 🆕 **AND A SECOND FACT LINES UP WITH IT — ⛔ BUT IT IS NOT INDEPENDENT, corrected on review: it corroborates ONLY ON THE TRADE-IN READING, which limit 1 below concedes may be false. If the 19,500 was a CASH SALE to a third party, the replacement lease becomes an unrelated fact and the whole weight falls back on the amount match.** _(The fact, as found:)_ **which that subsection did not have: the REPLACEMENT Audi's lease
is dated 08/10/2025** *(§3U ⑥, the `CLOSED END MOTOR VEHICLE LEASE` itself)*, **and its first bank payment is
in September** *(§3U ②: 595.81 × 4, Sep–Dec)*. ⇒ **The old vehicle leaving and the new one arriving are the
same fortnight.**

☑️ **So the month is AUGUST 2025 on EITHER reading** *(trade-in → about 08/10; cash sale → 08/21)*, and ⛔ **it does not need to be asked.** ⌨️ 🆕 **AND THE `Date sold` BOX WANTS A DAY, NOT A MONTH** *(decision 13a ④, and 13a's own *"never invent one to fill the box"*)*: **the paper holds 21 August on the cash-sale branch and only "about 08/10" on the trade-in branch** — ☑️ **so key 08/21/2025 if the deposit IS the disposal, and otherwise leave the box for the client.** ⛔ **Do not split the difference.**

⚠️ **THREE HONEST LIMITS, because this is an inference and not a document:**

1. ⛔ **§3E ⑤'s own objection stands: a trade-in credit is normally applied against the new vehicle, not
   DEPOSITED into the bank.** **So the 19,500 may be a cash SALE rather than a trade-in** — ⓘ *which changes
   nothing about the month or the proceeds, only the word for the transaction.*
2. ⛔ **That the schedule's `AUDI` and his note's `2019 Audi A6` are the same vehicle is still unconfirmed**
   *(§3C, decision 33)* — **and THAT is what gates removing the row, not the month.**

   whatever the month**, and the asset was in service from **2/1/2021**, so the holding period is not in question.
   ⚠️ 🆕 **THE AUTHORITY FOR "half-year" HERE IS ⑤-bis, NOT §3AI ②** — an earlier version cited ②, which tests
   **2025's** additions and has no bearing on an asset placed in service in 2021. 🔑 **It fills the `Date sold` box and the record — which is exactly why it was worth
   finding for free rather than asking.**

### ⑤-bis 🛑 THE CONVENTION GROUND FOR THE DISPOSALS WAS WRONG ON FIRST WRITING — the right test is each asset's OWN placed-in-service year, and one prior year would have FAILED it

⛔ **The first version of §3AH ① row 1 discharged decision 13a ② with *"§3AI ② has now established HALF-YEAR."*
That is the wrong test.** 🔑 **§3AI ② tests 2025's ADDITIONS. The convention for an asset is fixed in the year
IT was placed in service and governs it for its whole recovery period — so 2025's 40% test has no bearing on a
truck bought in 2021.**

✅ **RUN CORRECTLY, ON §3A-bis ④'s OWN TABLE:**

| Placed-in-service year | Additions that year *(as the 2024 schedule shows them)* | In Q4 | Share | Convention |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **2020** | `HU4577` 46,000 | — | **0%** | ✅ half-year |
| **2021** | `AUDI` 50,020 · `#7107` 78,145 · `#1800` 100,245 = 228,410 | — | **0%** | ✅ half-year |
| 🔴 **2022** | `2019 Wabash` 50,000 *(5/3)* · `2023 Utility Trailer` 59,299 *(11/11)* = 109,299 | **59,299** | 🔴 **54.25%** | 🔴 **MID-QUARTER** |
| **2024** | `2022 Frght Cascadia` 85,500 · `2025 Benson` 67,050 = 152,550 *(both 9/25)* | — | **0%** | ✅ half-year |

✅ **THE CONCLUSION SURVIVES, and that is why this is a correction and not a retraction.** **Every DISPOSAL
candidate sits in a half-year year:** the three 2018 Freightliners map to `HU4577`, `#7107` and `#1800`
*(2020 and 2021)*, the **Audi** is 2/1/2021, and the **`2015 Utility TRL` is on no schedule at all**, so it has
no depreciation and no convention. ⇒ ⛔ **The month still does not move any disposal-year figure, and it stays
off the ask.**

🔴 **BUT THE CORRECTION SURFACED SOMETHING ELSE, AND IT IS NOT ABOUT DISPOSALS AT ALL: 2022 LOOKS LIKE A
MID-QUARTER YEAR, AND BOTH ITS ASSETS ARE STILL ON THE BOOKS.** **`2019 Wabash Trailer` and `2023 Utility
Trailer` carry into 2025**, so if 2022 really was mid-quarter, **their 2025 depreciation runs off the
mid-quarter tables, not half-year** — ⛔ **and this paper is REBUILDING line 14 from scratch** *(decision 3)*.

⚠️ **STATED AS A QUESTION, NOT A FINDING, AND HERE IS WHY IT CANNOT BE SETTLED FROM WHAT WE HOLD:**

1. ⛔ **The table above shows the 2022 additions THAT SURVIVED to the 2024 schedule** — **an asset bought in
   2022 and disposed of before 2024 would have been in the 40% test and is invisible here.** **The test needs
   the whole year's additions, which only the 2022 return's own Form 4562 carries.**
2. ⛔ **Nothing in this file records what the PRIOR PREPARER actually did** — per-asset depreciation was never
   extracted, only the **410,452** aggregate *(§7)*.
3. ⚠️ **The 40% rule, its exclusions and the mid-quarter tables are all cited from knowledge and unverified.**

☑️ **WHAT TO DO: it is a line-14 question for the rebuild, not a client ask** — **the 2022 and 2023 returns are
in Double's own file library** *(`ZAKOMINCORPORATED_2.pdf` and the 2023 statements, §3AJ)*, **so it is answerable
in-house.** ⛔ **Nothing is asked of the client for it.**

PROCEEDS ARE RECEIVED, which may fall in a different tax year from the loss.** **The client dates the casualty
2025-01-01 and states insurance of 57,500** *(§3C)*. ⚠️ 🆕 **AND THE FILE DISAGREES WITH ITSELF ON THAT DATE — flagged on review, not resolved here: §3C says 2025-01-01 while decision 18, §5A ③ and §3T all say 2025-06-01.** ⓘ **Three surfaces to one, and this subsection picked the minority reading without noticing.** ✅ **It changes nothing here — both are 2025, and the question is when the MONEY arrived, not when the truck was lost.**

574,516.65, USA Florida Freight 434,132.39, Robinhood 13,035, Huntington 15,000 and 13,216, and the 19,500
of 21 August. ⚠️ **THAT IS NOT EVIDENCE OF ABSENCE** *([`method.md`](../../pre-return-review/method.md) rule
1b — a negative belongs to the search that produced it)*: **dozens of unnamed credits sit inside an unitemised
remainder, and a 57,500 could be among them.** ⚠️ 🆕 **STATED CORRECTLY ON REVIEW — an earlier version said "87
FURTHER credits inside 328,505.11", which double-counts: the four items named above (Robinhood, the two
Huntington and the 19,500) are THEMSELVES inside that 87 and that 328,505.11.** ✅ **The honest residue is
about 83 credits and roughly 267,754.11** *(328,505.11 − 13,035 − 15,000 − 13,216 − 19,500)*, **which still
hides a 57,500 comfortably.**

☑️ **THE CHECK IS FREE AND IN-HOUSE, and it is the same shape as the three that reversed two findings on this
same day:** **re-read the twelve 2025 statements for a credit near 57,500, or near his split of 20,000 and
34,000, under an insurer's caption.** ⛔ **It has not been run.**

🛑 **AND THE STAKE IS LARGER THAN A DATE:** **if the proceeds were received in 2026, the GAIN is a 2026 item and
the casualty may not belong on this return's line 4.** ⚠️ 🆕 **THREE QUALIFIERS ADDED ON REVIEW, because the
sentence was true only in one branch:**

1. ⛔ **It holds for a GAIN, not for a LOSS.** **A casualty LOSS belongs to the year of the loss, so a 2025 loss
   would reach line 4 whatever year the money arrived.** ✅ **The gain branch is the live one here** — every
   candidate schedule row's 2024 adjusted basis is far below 57,500 *(§3A-bis ④ against the 410,452 aggregate)* —
   **but the claim is branch-dependent and now says so.**

   2025 realization. On 2026 proceeds it becomes 12/31/2028.** ⚠️ *§1033 cited from knowledge throughout.* ✅ **Either way the ANSWER is on the
insurance settlement letter** *(§3AH ① row 5)*, **which states when it paid** — ☑️ **so this does not add an

## 3AL · 🔑 WHY THOSE THREE AND NOT THE OTHER SIX — Lilian's question, and the honest answer INVERTS the emphasis

> **Lilian, 2026-09-27:** *"El cliente tiene muchos préstamos, pagos de intereses, etc., y no has mencionado la
> posibilidad de que creas que estos gastos puedan estar incluidos en esta cuenta… ¿cuál es la diferencia con
> el resto? ¿Por qué tanto énfasis en que pueden estar ahí incluidos nuevamente estos gastos?"*

### ① ✅ THE DIFFERENCE IS ONE THING: SIX ARE LOCATED AND THREE ARE NOT

**Of his TWELVE credit relationships** *(§3U ②)*, **NINE pay something in 2025 that could plausibly sit in an
equipment or vehicle caption.** ✅ 🆕 **CORRECTED IN REVIEW 2026-09-27 — the first version said simply "he has
nine", which is the six plus the three and therefore defines the remainder out of existence, when the
remainder IS her question.** 🔑 **The other three that pay in 2025 — the SBA EIDL, the Fifth Third line of
credit and the Amex — are excluded for a REASON, not by omission: their interest sits inside his `Interest`
caption, which this return REPLACES wholesale with the measured figure** *(§3AG ③: those three total
18,029.39 against his 17,401)*, ⛔ **and none of them carries equipment PRINCIPAL, so none can produce the
double-count the three residual streams can.** ✅ **SIX of them are ACCOUNTED FOR:** BMO's
three streams, both AP Equipment notes and Financial Pacific sum to **102,876.06**, against his own
`Heavy haul equipment` caption of **102,834** — **42.06 apart, and he writes whole dollars** *(§3AG ①)*.

⇒ **We know where those six are.** ⛔ **The other three — Mitsubishi, IPFS and the Audi — are NOT in that
bucket** — ✅ 🆕 **CORRECTED IN REVIEW 2026-09-27: adding Mitsubishi overshoots the caption by 4,759.76,
IPFS by 9,468.76 and the Audi by 2,425.30.** ⛔ **The first version quoted 9,426.70 and 2,383.24, which are
the PAYMENT TOTALS themselves, not the overshoot — two different quantities under one label.** ⚠️ **And the
overshoot test does not apply to IPFS at all: ③ below establishes it was never a candidate for an equipment
caption, because it finances no equipment.**

🔑 **SO IT IS NOT THAT I HAVE EVIDENCE THEY SIT IN `Automobile and truck expenses`. IT IS THAT THEY ARE THE
RESIDUAL.** ⚠️ **An earlier version of this paper named that caption as though it were a finding. It is a
CANDIDATE, and §3AG ③ was right to say the check was about which branch, not which caption.**

### ② 🔑 AND HIS OWN CONVENTION IS WHAT MAKES IT A REAL QUESTION RATHER THAN A WORRY

✅ **He keeps financed-equipment payments in ONE dedicated caption, and he has done it for years.** **His 2023
workbook has a separate `Financed equipment` table** *(§3AK ②)*; **his 2025 workbook has `Heavy haul equipment`
as an expense line.** ⇒ **If his convention had held for all nine, all nine would be inside the 102,834.**

⇒ 🔑 **Two branches, and only these two:** **ⓐ he broke his own convention for three**, or **ⓑ he never
expensed those three at all.** ⚠️ **ⓑ is the CORRECT treatment for a financed purchase, so it is not a
failure — it is the outcome we would want.**

### ③ 🛑 AND THE THREE ARE NOT ALIKE — which is the part the paper had been getting wrong by lumping them

| | Where it would naturally sit | How likely it is inside a caption we keep | What the double-count costs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **IPFS** — insurance premium finance | 🔑 **`Insurance` (79,963)** — **and this one is DIFFERENT IN KIND: the premium genuinely IS an insurance expense.** ⛔ **It was never a candidate for `Heavy haul equipment`, because it does not finance equipment at all** | 🔴 **HIGHEST.** The whole payment belongs in `Insurance` by any ordinary bookkeeping; the only question is whether the finance charge rode in with it | ⚠️ **SMALLEST in dollars — only the FINANCE CHARGE is at risk of being counted twice**, and four payments of a ~10-payment agreement is of the order of a few hundred |
| 🟠 **The Audi Q6** — a LEASE | **`Automobile and truck expenses` (54,032)** — a lease payment is an ordinary vehicle expense with no principal in it | 🟠 **MIDDLE.** Nothing else fits it | ⚠️ **2,383.24, AND IT RUNS THE OTHER WAY** — if it is NOT in there, we are under-deducting, not over |
| ⚠️ **MITSUBISHI** — financed equipment | **`Heavy haul equipment`, where it is NOT** | ✅ **LOWEST of the three.** **It is exactly the kind of payment his convention puts in the 102,834 bucket, and it is not there** — so the more likely reading is that he never expensed it, **which is correct** ⓘ *(bought August 2025, only five payments, and he DID capitalise the trailer at 43,650)* | 🔴 **LARGEST — 4,717.70:** 1,823.59 of interest deducted twice and 2,894.11 of principal deducted once |

🛑 **READ THE TABLE AS A WHOLE, BECAUSE IT INVERTS WHAT THIS PAPER HAS BEEN SAYING: the LIKELIHOODS RUN
OPPOSITE TO THE DOLLARS.** ⛔ **Mitsubishi was made the headline because its number is biggest — and it is the
one least likely to be there.** ✅ **IPFS is the one most likely to be double-counted and the one worth the
least.**

### ④ ☑️ SO THE QUESTION TO THE CLIENT IS ONE SENTENCE, BUT ASKED WITH A BASIS

✅ **Ask:** *"En tu reporte de gastos, ¿la línea `Heavy haul equipment` incluye TODOS los pagos de equipo
financiado? Y los pagos del trailer Benson 2022, los del financiamiento del seguro y los del leasing del Audi,
¿están dentro de alguna línea? ¿De cuál?"*

🔑 **WHY THAT WORDING:** **the first half tests his CONVENTION, which is the thing we actually rely on** — and
if he answers *"yes, all of them"*, **the second half is answered for free and all three were never expensed.**
⇒ ✅ **One question that can close all three at once.**

⚠️ **AND IT DOES NOT ASK HIM TO CLASSIFY ANYTHING** *(decision 59)*: **it asks him where a payment sits in his
own file, which only he knows.**

## 3AO · 🔴 HOW EACH ASSET IS CLASSIFIED — read off IRS Pub. 946 (2025), and it does NOT agree with the prior year

> **Lilian, 2026-09-27:** *"sé que necesito distinguir si la depreciación es a 3 años o a 5 años, el tipo de
> assets, todo eso tengo que definirlo en ATX. Necesito que leas las reglas y las instrucciones de la IRS y los
> datos del vehículo, y me ayudes a determinar cómo hacer esta clasificación."*

🔑 **Nobody in this firm's sessions had ever checked the class. The prior year's 5-year life was transcribed,
not derived — and it is wrong for the tractors.**

### ① ✅ THE SOURCE, QUOTED — Pub. 946 (2025 edition), Appendix B, Table B-1

📄 **Read 2026-09-27 from `irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p946.pdf`, the 2025 edition** *(`publication-946/2025`, page
proof dated 13-Mar-2026)*. 🛑 **Not from memory — the skill's own rule.**

| Asset class | What Table B-1 calls it | Class life | 🔑 **GDS recovery period** | ADS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **00.22** | `Automobiles, Taxis` | 3 | **5 years** | 5 |
| **00.241** | `Light General Purpose Trucks` — *"Includes trucks for use over the road (actual weight less than 13,000 pounds)"* | 4 | **5 years** | 5 |
| **00.242** | `Heavy General Purpose Trucks` — *"Includes heavy general purpose trucks, concrete ready mix-trucks, and ore trucks, for use over the road (actual unloaded weight 13,000 pounds or more)"* | 6 | **5 years** | 6 |
| 🔴 **00.26** | `Tractor Units for Use Over-the-Road` | 4 | 🔴 **3 YEARS** | 4 |
| **00.27** | `Trailers and Trailer-Mounted Containers` | 6 | **5 years** | 6 |

🛑 **THE WHOLE POINT IS THE LINE BETWEEN 00.26 AND 00.242.** ✅ **A TRACTOR UNIT — the thing with a fifth
wheel that pulls a semi-trailer and carries no cargo itself — is THREE-YEAR property.** ⛔ **A straight
truck, however heavy, is FIVE-year.** ⚠️ **The distinction is the VEHICLE'S CONFIGURATION, not its weight and
not its brand.**

### ② 🔴 WHAT THAT MEANS FOR THIS FLEET

| Asset | What it is | Class | 🔑 **GDS life** | On the 2024 return |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FRT'18 (HU4577)` · `2018 FREIGHTLINER #7107` · `#1800` | **Freightliner tractors** ⚠️ **pending body-type confirmation (③)** | 🔴 **00.26** | 🔴 **3 years** | ⛔ **5 years** |
| `2022 Frght Cascadia (1838)` | **Freightliner tractor** ⚠️ **pending body-type confirmation (③)** — 🔑 **the ONLY tractor that survives to 12/31/2025, and it has ONE filed return** *(⑤)* | 🔴 **00.26** | 🔴 **3 years** | ⛔ **5 years** |
| `2019 Wabash Trailer` · `2023 Utility Trailer` · `2025 Benson` | **Semi-trailers** | ✅ **00.27** | ✅ **5 years** | ✅ **5 years — CORRECT** |
| `AUDI` *(the 2021 car)* | **Automobile** | ✅ **00.22** | ✅ **5 years** | ✅ **5 years — but see ④** |
| 🆕 `1839` · `1837` · `1840` · `146` *(2025 additions)* | **Freightliner Cascadia tractors** ⚠️ **pending body-type confirmation (③) — the model name does NOT establish it** | 🔴 **00.26** | 🔴 **3 years** | — |
| 🆕 `2026 Reitnouer DropMiser` · `2022 Benson trailer` *(2025 additions)* | **Semi-trailers** | ✅ **00.27** | ✅ **5 years** | — |

⚠️ 🆕 **AND READ THE FIRST TWO ROWS TOGETHER WITH ⑤: three of those four existing tractors are DISPOSED of
in 2025, so only `1838` is still on the books at year end.** ⛔ **An earlier draft treated all four as a live
fleet and built the wrong question for Julia on it.**

⇒ 🔴 **FOUR of the six 2025 additions are tractors, carrying 307,295 of the 421,485.** ⚠️ **The two trailers,
114,190, are correctly 5-year either way.**

### ③ ⚠️ WHAT CONFIRMS "TRACTOR", BECAUSE THE MODEL NAME DOES NOT

🛑 **A Cascadia is built in both configurations.** ✅ **Any ONE of these settles it and all three are already
in the firm's reach or the client's glovebox:**

1. ✅ **The TITLE or REGISTRATION body-type field** — it prints `TRACTOR` or `TRUCK TRACTOR` for a
   fifth-wheel unit, `TRUCK` for a straight truck. **The strongest evidence, and a State document.**
2. ✅ **The purchase invoice or the lender's equipment description** — the firm HOLDS one for **`1839` and
   both trailers**. ⛔ 🆕 **CORRECTED IN ROUND-2 REVIEW: `1837` and `1840` are NOT among them.** **What the
   BMO letters give is interest and a year-end balance, and a unit number in a caption cannot settle
   tractor-versus-truck** *(§3AI ③)* — ⚠️ **which also means §3AN ③'s line *"no cost documents for `1837`
   or `146`"* is right and this row must not contradict it.**
3. ✅ **The operating fact:** this company **owns FIVE semi-trailers at 12/31/2025 and hauls with them**
   *(Wabash · the 2023 Utility · the 2025 Benson · the Reitnouer · the 2022 Benson — ⚠️ **corrected in
   round-2 review from "four", which had counted the disposed 2015 Utility TRL out but the two 2025
   additions in**)*. ⛔ **A straight truck cannot pull a semi-trailer.** ⚠️ **Strong, but inferential — use
   it to corroborate, not to decide.**

### ④ 🔴 AND ONE THING THE CLASS DOES **NOT** SETTLE — §280F, which is a separate gate

📄 **Pub. 946 (2025), verbatim:** *"A passenger automobile is any four-wheeled vehicle made primarily for use
on public streets, roads, and highways and rated at 6,000 pounds or less of unloaded gross vehicle weight
(6,000 pounds or less of gross vehicle weight for trucks and vans)."*

| | |
|---|---|
| **The tractors and trailers** | ✅ **Not passenger automobiles — nowhere near the threshold. §280F does not touch them, and neither does the SUV §179 cap** |
| **The 2021 `AUDI`** | 🔴 **IT IS ONE**, and the 2025 caps are **20,200 / 19,600 / 11,800 / 7,060** *(and **12,200** first-year where bonus is not taken)*. ⚠️ **This asset is being DISPOSED of in 2025, so whether the prior years applied the cap changes the ACCUMULATED DEPRECIATION and therefore the GAIN.** ☑️ **A question for Julia against the filed returns — not an assertion here** |
| **The Audi Q6 e-tron** | ✅ **LEASED, so no depreciation at all.** ⚠️ **What COULD apply is the lessee's §280F(c) INCLUSION AMOUNT — but only if it is a passenger automobile, i.e. GVW ≤ 6,000 lb.** 🔑 **The door-jamb sticker answers it in five seconds, and an electric mid-size SUV is usually ABOVE it, which would mean no inclusion amount at all** |

⛔ **The §179 SUV cap of 31,300 for 2025 is also irrelevant here** — it bites only sport-utility vehicles
between 6,000 and 14,000 lb GVWR. ✅ **And Lilian has ruled §179 at ZERO anyway** *(decision 3)*.

### ⑤ 🛑 THE PART THAT IS **NOT** OURS TO DECIDE — and it is the whole reason this section is not an instruction

⛔ **DO NOT re-life the EXISTING fleet on this return.** 🔑 **A recovery period used on TWO OR MORE
consecutive filed returns is a METHOD OF ACCOUNTING, and correcting one is a Form 3115 change with a §481(a)
adjustment, not a line edit.** ⚠️ **And it runs straight into Lilian's decision 3: *depreciation follows the
prior year exactly.***

🛑 **BUT THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS SUBSECTION GOT THE FACTS WRONG AND THE ROUND-2 REVIEW CAUGHT IT. It said
*"those four tractors have been depreciated at 5 years on two or more consecutive filed returns"*, and asked
Julia to authorise a 3115 on that basis.** ⛔ **THAT IS NOT THE POSITION.** ✅ **RECORDED, NOT TIDIED AWAY:**

1. 🔴 **THREE OF THE FOUR EXISTING TRACTORS LEAVE THE BOOKS IN 2025**, and this holds **however the unit-number
   map resolves**: there are exactly **four** Freightliners on the 2024 schedule, and **three** of the five
   2025 disposals are Freightliners *(his `1834`, `1835`, `1836`)*. ⇒ **Only ONE survives to 12/31/2025.**
2. 🔴 **THE SURVIVOR IS `1838`, AND IT HAS APPEARED ON EXACTLY ONE FILED RETURN** — in service **9/25/2024**,
   and §3I ①'s own table shows the 2023 schedule held six assets *without* it. ⇒ 🔑 **THE
   TWO-CONSECUTIVE-RETURNS TEST IS NOT MET, so no method of accounting is established for it** — which
   usually means the route is **amending the 2024 return**, not a 3115. ☑️ **Which route is Julia's to
   choose; the point here is only that the 3115 premise does not hold.**

☑️ **SO WHAT GOES TO JULIA IS THIS, AND IT IS A DIFFERENT SET OF QUESTIONS:**

| | The question | Why it cannot be answered here |
|---|---|---|
| **a** | 🔴 **Are the four NEW tractors keyed at 3 years or at 5?** ⚠️ **This is the only one that touches a figure on THIS return** | **Nothing protects a first-year asset**, so 3 is defensible and correct — ⚠️ **but the survivor `1838` sits at 5, so keying the new ones at 3 puts two lives on one fleet in one schedule, which a reviewer will ask about** |
| **b** | ⚠️ **For the THREE tractors being disposed of: which accumulated-depreciation figure feeds Form 4797?** ⛔ **Re-lifing an asset that will not exist at year end is not the question** — the question is what was allowed or allowable on the prior returns, and that is read off the filed returns | **It changes the GAIN on three disposals, and the answer lives in the prior returns rather than in any rule** |
| **c** | ⚠️ **For the survivor `1838`: correct it, and by which route?** 🔑 **One filed return means no established method, so this is probably an amended 2024 rather than a 3115** | **The choice of route, and whether to make it at all, is the signer's** |
| **d** | **Was §280F applied to the 2021 Audi in 2021–2024?** | **It changes the accumulated depreciation on an asset being SOLD this year, so it changes the gain** |

⚠️ **AND ONE HONEST CAVEAT ON DIRECTION:** 🔑 **3-year is not automatically "better".** ✅ **It deducts
faster** — ⛔ **but it also means MORE accumulated depreciation, which means MORE §1245 recapture as ordinary
income when a tractor is sold**, and this client sells them. **Julia decides with that in front of her.**

### ④ 🔴 SO MITSUBISHI REVERSES DIRECTION — the exposure is UNDER-deduction, not over

⛔ **THIS PAPER HAS SAID SINCE §3AG THAT MITSUBISHI'S 4,717.70 RISKED BEING DEDUCTED TWICE, AND MADE IT THE
HEADLINE OF THE CLIENT ASK. ON THE EVIDENCE NOW IN HAND THAT IS BACKWARDS.**

🔑 **His own financed-equipment table lists the 2022 Benson trailer as financed equipment, with its own 2025
interest and its own year-end payoff — exactly like the other seven. He TREATED it as financed equipment.**
⛔ **Yet its payments are not inside the 102,834.** ⇒ ✅ **The reading that fits both facts is an OMISSION
WHEN HE TOTALLED THE LINE, not a deliberate placement inside `Auto Repars & Maintenance`.**

⚠️ **It is a strong inference, not proof.** 🛑 **But the alternative now requires him to have put ONE of eight
financed contracts into a repairs caption while putting the other seven in the dedicated line AND listing all
eight in his financed-equipment table.** ⛔ **That is a strained reading, and it was never the likelier one.**

|---|---|---|
| Its **interest** 1,823.59 | ✅ **ADD it to line 13** — we are currently **UNDER**-deducting | ⛔ deducted twice ⇒ remove from line 20 |
| Its **principal** 2,894.11 | ✅ **Never deducted. Nothing to do** | 🔴 deducted once ⇒ remove from line 20 |
| **Net effect on line 22** | 🟢 **−1,823.59** *(more deduction)* | 🔴 **+4,717.70** *(less deduction — the whole payment)* |

### ⑤ ✅ IPFS HAS **NO** TAXABLE-INCOME EXPOSURE AT ALL — and this paper never said why

🛑 **THE POINT THAT WAS MISSED FOR TWO WEEKS: A PREMIUM FINANCE AGREEMENT'S "PRINCIPAL" IS THE INSURANCE
PREMIUM ITSELF, AND AN INSURANCE PREMIUM IS A DEDUCTIBLE EXPENSE.** ⛔ **It is not like equipment principal,
which buys an asset and is never deductible.**

⇒ **On the CASH basis this return uses, what he deducts is what he PAID. Of the 9,426.70 paid to IPFS in
2025:**

- ✅ **the premium portion is `Insurance`, line 20** — **deductible**;
- ✅ **the finance charge is `Interest`, line 13** — **also deductible**.

🔑 **BOTH LINES ARE DEDUCTIONS ON PAGE 1, SO MOVING AN AMOUNT BETWEEN THEM DOES NOT CHANGE LINE 22 BY ONE
DOLLAR.** ⇒ ✅ **The IPFS statement is a PRESENTATION document, not a figure that moves the tax** — ⚠️ **its
only substantive use is the §163(j) / Form 8990 interest base, which is Julia's open item.** ⚠️ **And if the
payments are NOT in his `Insurance` of 79,963, we are under-deducting, not over.**
☑️ **It stays on the list as a pending document, exactly as Lilian ruled — with its priority corrected.**

### ⑥ 🟠 THE AUDI LEASE RUNS THE SAME WAY

**2,383.24 of lease payments.** ✅ **A lease payment is deductible in full** *(subject only to a §280F
inclusion amount, and §3AO ④ says the weight rating probably takes it outside that)*. ⇒ **If it is inside
`Auto Repars & Maintenance` it is deducted once and correctly; if it is nowhere, we UNDER-deduct 2,383.24.**
⛔ **There is no over-deduction case.**

🔑 ⇒ **PUT ⓸ ⓹ AND ⓺ TOGETHER AND THE ANSWER TO HER QUESTION IS: NOT ONE OF THE THREE RESIDUAL PAYMENTS
CARRIES AN OVER-DEDUCTION RISK ON THE LIKELY READING. ALL THREE RUN TOWARDS UNDER-DEDUCTION OR ARE NEUTRAL.**

### ⑦ 🔴 A NEW FINDING, AND IT IS THE LARGEST THING IN `Auto Repars & Maintenance`: A 34,000 TOWING BILL

**His `Asset List 2025` says of the totalled truck, verbatim:**
*"Totaled, insurance covered $57500 ( truck -20K and towing service of 34K which I paid cash using business
line of credit)"*

🔑 **So he paid a towing bill of about 34,000 out of company funds, and the insurer reimbursed it.**
🔴 **34,000 against a `Auto Repars & Maintenance` caption of 54,032 is nearly two thirds of that line.**

⚠️ **AND IT CUTS BOTH WAYS, WHICH IS WHY IT IS A FINDING AND NOT AN ADJUSTMENT:**

- **If the 34,000 IS in the 54,032:** ✅ **the expense is deductible** — ⛔ **but the insurance reimbursement of
  it is INCOME, or it reduces the expense.** **Recognising one without the other overstates the deduction by
  34,000.**
- **If it is NOT in the 54,032:** **then neither side is on the return and they cancel** — ⚠️ **but 54,032 of
  genuine repairs on this fleet then needs no explanation, while 34,000 of paid cash needs one.**

🛑 **AND IT REFRAMES THE CASUALTY.** **His own split is `truck 20K` + `towing 34K` = 54,000, against insurance
of 57,500** ⇒ **3,500 unallocated.** 🔑 **The gain on Form 4684 turns on the part allocable to the TRUCK — on
his own words about 20,000, against an adjusted basis built on 78,145 — not on the whole 57,500.** ⇒ ⚠️ **The
paper has been carrying 57,500 as the proceeds. That is the INSURER'S total, not the truck's proceeds.**

### ② ✅ IT ANSWERS LILIAN'S QUESTION — AND HER OWN GUESS WAS RIGHT

> *"No estoy seguro de si el cliente dijo que ese Audi que rentó era para uso personal o si debería ir en los

named as the lessee**, and **the primary-use box is ticked `business`.**

nothing on Schedule L — no asset and no liability.** ✅ **The payments are a DEDUCTION.**

☑️ **AND ON THE CASH BASIS, 2025's DEDUCTION IS 2,383.24 — four payments, not five.** 🔑 **Because the
12,578.97 due at signing, which INCLUDED the first monthly payment, was settled entirely by rebates and
non-cash credits: the company paid nothing in August.** ⇒ **The bank's four debits of 595.81 (Sep–Dec) are
the whole of 2025's cash outlay** *(§3U ② row ➕)*.

⚠️ **THE CONFUSION LILIAN HALF-REMEMBERED IS REAL BUT IT IS A DIFFERENT VEHICLE.** ⛔ **The client never
called the Audi personal.** ✅ **What his own sheet calls personal is the PORSCHE MACAN:** *"July 27 2025
purchased another personal vehicle 2023 Porsche Macan"* — **financed by Tropical FCU** *(§3AP ⑨)*. 🔑 **And
Julia ruled in AUGUST 2025 that the 2021 Audi was 100% the company's** *(§5, client-intelligence §5)* ⚠️ **(corrected in review — an earlier version wrote *"in 2021"*, collapsing the vehicle's model year into the ruling date; and that ATTRIBUTION is cited only to the 2026-09-13 Gmail/Drive sweep with no message named — the RETURN is what is first-hand)**.
⇒ **Three vehicles, three positions, and only the Porsche is the personal one.**

### ③ 🔴 AND IT SETTLES SOMETHING NOBODY EXPECTED — THE 19,500 WAS A SALE, NOT A TRADE-IN

**§3E ⑤ recorded a plain `DEPOSIT` of 19,500 on 21 August with no counterparty, and flagged the problem: the
client wrote *"2019 Audi A6 traded in for \$19500 to Audi Dealer"*, but a trade-in credit is normally applied
against the new vehicle rather than DEPOSITED.** ⛔ **It could not be settled.**

🔑 **THE LEASE SETTLES IT: `Net trade-in allowance: N/A`.** ⇒ ✅ **There was NO trade-in on this deal, and the
11,000 capitalized cost reduction came from rebates and non-cash credits, not from a car.**
⇒ ✅ **So the dealer BOUGHT the old Audi and paid the company 19,500, which is the deposit.** **His word
*"traded in"* is loose usage.**

☑️ **WHICH SIMPLIFIES THE DISPOSITION: it is an ordinary SALE for 19,500 cash, not a trade-in** — ⛔ **and
there is no like-kind question to consider, since §1031 has not applied to vehicles since 2017 anyway.**

### ④ ⚠️ WHAT THE DOCUMENT DOES **NOT** SETTLE, and one of them got bigger

| | |
|---|---|
| 🔴 **The §280F(c) LEASE INCLUSION AMOUNT** | ⚠️ **It applies to a leased PASSENGER AUTOMOBILE above a value threshold, and the agreed-upon value is now known: 63,935.00 — far above it.** ⇒ 🔑 **So IF it is a passenger automobile the inclusion amount is material, where before the value was unknown.** ✅ **The escape is the weight: Pub. 946 puts the line at 6,000 lb GVW for a truck or van, and an electric mid-size SUV is usually above it** *(§3AO ④)*. ☑️ **The door-jamb sticker answers it in five seconds and nobody has looked** |
| ⚠️ **The BUSINESS-USE PERCENTAGE** | ⛔ **The `Primary Use: business` box is the LESSOR'S classification for consumer-leasing-disclosure purposes — it is why this lease carries no Reg M consumer disclosures. It is NOT a tax business-use percentage.** 🔑 **A company-leased car used partly personally still needs an allocation, and a shareholder's personal use is compensation.** ☑️ **Julia's** |
| ⚠️ **The 495.00 disposition fee and the 395.00 purchase-option fee** | **Neither is a 2025 item** — the disposition fee falls at the end of the term in 2028 if he does not buy |

🔑 **The answer is IN THE FILED RETURN, and it does not need asking. Compare every carried-forward asset's

| `2025 Benson` | **67,050** | **67,050** | ✅ **0** |

🛑 **READ THE SHAPE OF THE DIFFERENCES, NOT JUST THEIR SIZE.** ✅ **A client remembers a ROUND number —
78,000, 100,000, 56,000. A DOCUMENT produces 78,145, 100,245 and 59,299.** ⇒ 🔑 **THE PRIOR-YEAR PATTERN IS
DOCUMENT-FIRST: where the two differ, the return carries the odd figure, and 145 / 245 / 3,299 are
dealer-fee-shaped — sales tax, tag, title, doc fee.**

✅ **AND THE 3,299 IS ALMOST FULLY EXPLAINED BY THE CLIENT'S OWN NOTE**, which says of that same trailer:

purchase price PLUS the sales tax and the registration fees, which is correct**, and **could only have done
it from the bill of sale.**

⇒ ☑️ **SO THE ANSWER TO HER QUESTION: follow the document.** ⚠️ **AND THAT CUTS AGAINST THREE OF THIS
RETURN'S OWN WORKING DECISIONS**, which key his figures where nothing contradicts them *(decisions 13, 15,
60)*:

| 2025 addition | What we hold | What the prior-year pattern implies |
|---|---|---|

| `1840` — **115,550** | ✅ 🆕 **CORRECTED: a RETAIL PURCHASE AGREEMENT, stock `263894` — 115,000 price + 550 dealer prep = 115,550, matching his booked figure to the dollar** *(§3H ③)*. **An earlier version of this row said *"his figure only"*, contradicting THREE other surfaces of this paper** | ⛔ **NO — and it is EVIDENCE: his booked figure IS the document total, so where his figure is ODD he is probably already reading it off a document** |
| `146` — **23,000 cash** | ⛔ **His figure only, and no lender exists** | 🔴 **Ask — AND THIS IS THE LIKELIEST OF THE THREE TO BE SHORT: 23,000 is the only ROUND figure among the six additions, and round means memory** |
| `1839` — **105,550** | ✅ **A lender note stating a 95,550 LOAN** | 🔑 **NOT a contradiction — a loan is not a price.** ⛔ 🆕 **AND AN EARLIER VERSION OF THIS CELL ARGUED THE GAP WAS A DOWN PAYMENT *"because the Audi lease shows an 11,000 capitalized cost reduction"*. THAT IS FALSE AND §3AS ① IN THIS SAME SECTION DISPROVES IT: the whole 12,578.97 due at signing was settled by `Rebates and noncash credits`, and the 11,000 is a COMPONENT of that 12,578.97 — so it was neither his cash nor a trade-in.** ✅ **WHAT ACTUALLY SUPPORTS THE `1839`: Lilian's own instruction that a financed amount need not equal the asset's cost** *(§6)*. ⛔ **The 10,000 gap is UNEVIDENCED**, and the free test nobody has run is a bank search for a ≈10,000 dealer payment around February 2025 |

## 3AU · 🔴 THE CASUALTY — Lilian is right: only the VEHICLE'S share goes on the disposition

> **Lilian, 2026-09-27:** *"tal vez en ATX, en el asset disposition, se pone solo el monto correspondiente al
> vehículo y no se pone el monto correspondiente al pago de la grúa… También creo que pudiéramos buscar en los
> statements del banco si hay un monto parecido que entre a la cuenta."*

### ① ✅ THE TREATMENT IS EXACTLY WHAT SHE DESCRIBED

🔑 **Form 4684 Section B computes the gain or loss on the PROPERTY. Its reimbursement line takes the insurance
received FOR THAT PROPERTY — nothing else.** ⇒ ⛔ **A payment reimbursing a TOWING BILL is not proceeds for
the truck.** ✅ **It is a recovery of an expense: it either offsets the towing deduction or is income.**

⇒ ☑️ **SO IN THE ASSET DISPOSITION, ONLY THE VEHICLE'S SHARE IS ENTERED.** 🔑 **On his own words that is
about 20,000, not 57,500** — and **the paper has been carrying 57,500 as the proceeds since §3C and decision 18** ⚠️ *(an earlier version cited §3U, which is the LENDER CENSUS and carries no casualty figure)*. ⛔ **That
would overstate the casualty gain by the towing share.**

⚠️ **AND THE TWO SIDES MUST BE TREATED CONSISTENTLY OR THE RETURN DOUBLE-COUNTS:** **if the ~34,000 towing
was deducted (§3AQ ② — it may be inside his `Auto Repars & Maintenance` of 54,032), its reimbursement is
income. If it was never deducted, its reimbursement is not income either.** ⛔ **One side without the other
is wrong in whichever direction it is taken.**

### ② ✅ 🆕 **RUN 2026-09-28 — §3BD. THE 57,500 IS ON THE BANK: 9 April 2025, ONE credit, from `OWL LAND OF ILLI`, captioned `2 Checks for truck incident`.** 🔑 **Three of the four branches below are DEAD — the casualty IS a 2025 item, the bank did NOT split it, and Q1 survives.** ⛔ **And the search this subsection PRESCRIBED would have missed it: it says to look for an insurance caption, and the credit carries the freight customer's** *(decision 74)*. _(As written:)_ ☑️ AND THE FREE SEARCH SHE PROPOSES SHOULD RUN BEFORE ANY CLIENT ASK — IT HAS NOT BEEN RUN

> *"Debe estar en algún sitio porque ese dinero se le debe haber ingresado al negocio."*

✅ **She is right that it must be traceable, and the search is free and in-house.** ⛔ **BUT IT HAS NOT BEEN
RUN, AND THE PAPER MUST NOT BE READ AS SAYING IT WAS.** 🔑 **§3E ⑥ is explicit about the limit of its own
credit list: it is *"every movement matching the counterparty strings that were searched"* — NOT every
movement.** **The strings searched were lender and payroll captions. No insurance caption was among them.**

☑️ **WHAT TO LOOK FOR, and the shapes are different enough to distinguish:**

| If the credit looks like… | Then |
|---|---|
| **one credit of ≈57,500** | **the insurer settled everything in one payment** ⇒ **the split is his to allocate, and it is one question** |
| **≈20,000 and ≈34,000 separately** | 🔑 **THE BANK ITSELF SPLITS IT AND NO QUESTION IS NEEDED** — **the best possible outcome** |
| **≈20,000 only** | **the towing was paid direct to the tow company** ⇒ **the 34,000 never came in, so it is not income and probably was not his expense either** |
| **nothing in 2025** | 🔴 **the insurer paid in 2026** ⇒ **the gain is a 2026 item and the casualty may not belong on this return at all** |

⛔ **ONE CAUTION ON METHOD: the 2025 credits already inventoried include a 13,035 `Robinhood` transfer and two
`HUNTINGTON BP` credits of 15,000 and 13,216 whose nature is NOT established** *(§3E ⑤)*. ⚠️ **None is
insurance-shaped, but do not assume a credit is the settlement because its size is convenient** — 🔑 **match
it by caption AND date, the way §3P matched the lenders.**

## 3AV · 🔴 THE 2015 UTILITY TRAILER — never capitalized, and the answer is the same whichever way it happened

> **Lilian, 2026-09-27:** *"dime qué debemos hacer… pero asegúrate, por favor, de que no esté incluido dentro
> de la lista de assets y lo estemos pasando por alto dentro de ATX."*

### ① ✅ THE SAFETY CHECK SHE ASKED FOR, RUN — AND THERE IS A TRAP IN IT

✅ **CONFIRMED: the `2015 Utility TRL` is NOT among the eight assets on the filed 2024 Form 4562.** The eight
are `FRT'18 (HU4577)` · `AUDI` · `2018 FREIGHTLINER #7107` · `#1800` · `2019 Wabash Trailer` ·
`2023 Utility Trailer` · `2022 Frght Cascadia` · `2025 Benson` *(§3A-bis)*. ⇒ **It is not on the schedule and
nothing about it is being overlooked in ATX.**

🛑 **BUT THE TRAP IS REAL AND IT IS WHY THIS CHECK MATTERS: THERE ARE TWO UTILITY TRAILERS.**

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ **`2023 Utility Trailer`** | **IS on the schedule at 59,299**, financed by Financial Pacific, paid all year. 🔑 **IT STAYS. Do not dispose of it** |
| 🔴 **`2015 Utility TRL`** | **On NEITHER filed return**, no lender, sold 2025-06-01 for **4,000** |

⛔ **NEVER write *"the utility trailer"* unqualified on this client** — the same trap the two Benson trailers
already set.

### ② ✅ WHAT TO DO, AND IT IS THE SAME ANSWER ON EVERY PLAUSIBLE HISTORY

🔑 **The question looks open and is not, because a 2015 trailer on a five-year life is FULLY DEPRECIATED BY
2020 whether or not anyone recorded it — and §1245 recapture turns on depreciation *allowed OR ALLOWABLE*.**

| How it might have happened | Basis at the 2025 sale | The gain |
|---|---|---|
| **The company bought it and expensed the purchase** *(or §179'd it)* | **ZERO** | **4,000, ORDINARY under §1245** |
| **The company bought it and simply never recorded it** | **ZERO** — cost less allowable depreciation, and a 2015 five-year asset is fully written off by 2020 | **4,000, ORDINARY under §1245** |
| ⚠️ **The SHAREHOLDER owned it, not the company** | ⛔ **not the company's sale at all** | **the 4,000 is a CONTRIBUTION, equity side** |

⇒ ☑️ **KEY IT AS: proceeds 4,000, basis ZERO, ORDINARY income on Form 4797 Part II.** ✅ **Two of the three
histories give exactly that and neither needs a document.** ⚠️ **The third is the only alternative, it is
Julia's call and not the client's, and it moves 4,000 from income to equity — which is a real difference but
a small one.**

🔑 **AND THE POSITION IS DEFENSIBLE WITHOUT ANY DOCUMENT**, which is the point: ⛔ **we are not claiming a
basis we cannot prove; we are claiming ZERO, which is the least favourable assumption available.**
☑️ **A reviewer's objection to this row can only be that we were too conservative.**

⚠️ **WHAT JULIA SHOULD SEE, and it is why this section exists:** **the asset was sold by a company whose books
never carried it, and his own sheet assigns it a *"2023 value"* of 15,000 and a *"2024 value"* of 12,000.**
⛔ **Those are his ESTIMATES OF WORTH, not cost and not basis** — **they must never be keyed as basis, and a
reviewer glancing at his sheet could easily take the 12,000 for one.**

## 3AY · 🔴 THE DEPRECIATION DEFECTS, WITH THE AUTHORITY QUOTED — and the two disposals that never went on

### ① 🛑 §280F CANNOT REACH A TRACTOR OR A TRAILER — quoted, not remembered

📄 **Instructions for Form 4562 (2025)**, downloaded 2026-09-27 from `irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/i4562.pdf`, page
proof **30-Mar-2026**, verbatim:

> *"Passenger automobiles are 4-wheeled vehicles manufactured primarily for use on public roads that are rated
> at 6,000 pounds unloaded gross vehicle weight or less (for a truck or van, gross vehicle weight is
> substituted for unloaded gross vehicle weight)."*

⇒ ⛔ **A Freightliner Cascadia tractor and a Benson or Reitnouer semi-trailer are nowhere near 6,000 lb, and a
trailer is not a self-propelled road vehicle at all.** ✅ **§3AO ④ already recorded this** — *"the tractors and
trailers: not passenger automobiles. §280F does not touch them."* 🔴 **The return applies the caps to three of
them anyway, because all three were entered as LISTED PROPERTY.**

🔑 **THE CHAIN, AND IT IS WORTH SEEING BECAUSE ONE WRONG CHECKBOX PRODUCED ALL THREE:** **asset entered as
listed property (`V-7`) → ATX puts it in Form 4562 Part V → Part V applies the passenger-automobile limit →
the deduction stops at the table figure.** ⇒ ☑️ **The fix is one attribute per asset, not three separate
computations.**

| Asset | As keyed | 🔑 **Uncapped, on the life the return already uses** | 💵 **Understated by** |
|---|---|---|---|
| `2026 Reitnouer DropMiser Trailer` *(3/5/2025, 70,540)* | **bonus 20,200, MACRS 0** | **bonus 70,540** | 🔴 **50,340** |
| `2022 Frght Cascadia` *(9/25/2024, 85,500)* | **2025 charge 19,800** | **85,500 × 32% = 27,360** at 5-year 200DB HY year 2 | 🔴 **7,560** |
| `2025 Benson` *(9/25/2024, 67,050)* | **2025 charge 19,800** | **67,050 × 32% = 21,456** | 🔴 **1,656** |
| | | ⇒ **TOTAL** | 🔴 **59,556** |

⚠️ **AND THE 2024 RETURN DID THE SAME THING — this is INHERITED, not created this year.** 🔑 **12,400 is the
2024 first-year passenger-automobile limit where bonus is not taken, and BOTH 2024 additions carry exactly
12,400 of prior accumulated depreciation on costs of 85,500 and 67,050.** ⇒ ☑️ **So 2024 under-depreciated
those two as well, and correcting 2025 does not recover the 2024 shortfall.** 🛑 **Per §4C the label follows
the CORRECTNESS, not who chose it: this makes the return wrong, so it is raised as a DEFECT, naming where it
came from.** ⚖️ **Whether to reach back into 2024 is JULIA's — and §3AO ⑤ already frames that choice for the
`2022 Frght Cascadia` on the recovery-period question, which is the same asset.**

### ② 🔴 TWO OF THE FIVE 2025 DISPOSALS ARE NOT ON THE RETURN

| Disposal | Unit | On the return? |
|---|---|---|
| `1834` = `FRT'18(HU4577)`, 46,000 | May 2020 | ✅ **4797 Part III A — 6,000 proceeds, 6,000 ordinary** |
| `1836` = `#1800`, 100,245 | July 2021 | ✅ **§179 disposition report → K-1 box 17 code K, 4,500** |
| `AUDI`, 50,020 | Feb 2021 | ✅ **4797 Part III B — 19,500 proceeds, 11,736 ordinary** |
| 🔴 **`1835` = `#7107`, 78,145** | **June 2021 — THE TOTALLED TRUCK** | ⛔ **STILL ON THE SCHEDULE. No Form 4684. 4797 line 14 is blank** |
| 🔴 **`2015 Utility TRL`** | **sold 6/1/2025 for 4,000** | ⛔ **ABSENT — decision 67** |

🔑 **THE 78,145 IS NOT A GUESS: it is the exact difference between this return's closing 10a of 761,479 and
§3AQ ③'s 683,334, and 683,334 was built from the four disposals' cost of 274,410 before this return existed.**
⚠️ 🆕 **AND THAT IS A CONSISTENCY CHECK, NOT INDEPENDENT CORROBORATION — the review was right to narrow it.** **Both figures are `536,259 + 421,485` less a disposal set, so the difference IS `274,410 − 196,265` by construction.** ✅ **What it does prove is that NO OTHER discrepancy hides in the two builds** — and the missing truck is visible directly anyway, because it is still on the schedule.

🛠️ **AND REMOVING #7107 CLOSES §3AQ ③'s "ONE UNKNOWN":** **10a 761,479 − 78,145 = 683,334** ✅ **and
10b 638,227 − 78,145 = 560,082.** ⇒ 🔑 **`10b` IS NOW A NUMBER RATHER THAN A BLANK: 560,082 AS THE DRAFT STANDS.** ⚠️ **Not settled — §3AX rows 1, 2, 6 and 7 all move it, and row 6 alone takes it to 544,844** *(decision 69)*. ⚠️ 🆕 **AND THIS CROSS-FOOT CANNOT FAIL, so it is not evidence of anything:**
`5,788.50 + 100,000 + 683,334 − 560,082 = 229,040.50` **against the return's own 229,041 — but 10a and 10b were each reduced by the same 78,145, so the total was never going to move.** ✅ **It confirms the subtraction, nothing more.**
⇒ ⛔ **Removing the truck does NOT change total assets — 10a and 10b fall by the same amount.** ✅ **What it
changes is that a destroyed vehicle stops appearing as an owned asset, and the casualty gain gets reported.**

## 3AZ · ⚖️ THE BONUS ELECTION — the return reverses decision 3, and the swing is ≈245,900

🛑 **THIS IS THE LARGEST SINGLE FIGURE ON THE RETURN AND IT IS A DECISION, NOT AN ERROR.** ⛔ **It is raised
because it contradicts a decision this paper RECORDS, not because the keyed position is wrong in law.**

📄 **Instructions for Form 4562 (2025), verbatim:** *"P.L. 119-21, commonly known as the One Big Beautiful
Bill Act, reinstated the 100% special depreciation allowance for certain qualified property acquired and
placed in service after January 19, 2025… However, you can elect to take a 40% special depreciation allowance…
instead of taking the 100%."* ⇒ ✅ **Bonus is the DEFAULT. Claiming it needs no statement; ELECTING OUT does.**
☑️ **So the return as keyed is procedurally valid — nothing is missing from it.**

| | Line 14 | Page 1 line 22 |
|---|---|---|
| ⌨️ **As keyed — 100% bonus** | **416,276** | **−260,179** |
| ⚖️ **Decision 3 — elect out of bonus** | **≈170,400** *(`307,295 × 33.33% = 102,421` + `43,650 × 20% = 8,730` + `70,540 × 20% = 14,108` + the existing fleet's 45,131)* | **≈ −14,300** |
| 💵 **The swing** | 🔴 **≈245,900** | |

⚠️ 🆕 **READ THE SECOND ROW AS AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE, NOT A COMPUTATION — NARROWED IN REVIEW.** **It holds the
existing fleet's 45,131 constant, which is the §280F-CAPPED figure, while assuming §280F does NOT bite the
Reitnouer** — **two states of §3AX row 2 in one column.** ✅ **Fixing that moves it by a few thousand and does
not change the DIRECTION of her choice, which is what the row exists to show.**

🔑 **WHY IT MATTERS MORE THAN THE NUMBER:** **the shareholder can only use a 260,179 loss to the extent of his
STOCK AND DEBT BASIS** *(§8B — his 2024 Form 7203 line 15 is not in the firm's hands)*. ⛔ **A loss suspended
for lack of basis is not lost, but it is not deducted this year either** — **and the AAA opens at ZERO**
*(§3AE ①)*, **so nothing cushions it.** ☑️ **That is the whole reason 2024 elected out, and it is the case for
honouring decision 3.** ⚠️ **The case the other way is equally real: at 3-year lives the tractors write off
fast anyway, and electing out defers rather than saves.**

⛔ **NOT A SESSION'S CALL. Row 1 of §3AX puts it to her; it is her own decision to confirm or supersede.**
✅ **If she confirms the reversal, decision 3 is SUPERSEDED and this paper says so — it is not left to be
re-discovered as a contradiction.**

## 3BA · ✅ THE MEALS RATE IS **80%** — read off the 2025 publications, and the open item CLOSES

> ⚠️ **§3AQ ①'s line-20 row and decision 64 both said: PROVISIONAL until the rate is read off the current-year
> instructions on irs.gov.** ☑️ **It has now been read. 🔑 This is exactly the free in-house job the paper said
> to do, and it moves a figure on page 1.**

📄 **Publication 463 (2025)**, `irs.gov/pub/irs-pdf/p463.pdf`, page proof **27-Feb-2026**, chapter 2, verbatim:

> *"Individuals subject to 'hours of service' limits. You can deduct a higher percentage of your meal expenses
> while traveling away from your tax home if the meals take place during or incident to any period subject to
> the Department of Transportation's 'hours of service' limits. **The percentage is 80%.**"*

📄 **And the qualifying population, same page, verbatim:** *"Interstate truck operators and bus drivers who are
under Department of Transportation regulations."*

📄 **Instructions for Form 1120-S (2025), page 20, verbatim:** *"See section 274(n)(3) for a special rule that
applies to expenses for meals consumed by individuals subject to the hours of service limits of the Department
of Transportation."*

| **The meals are *"while traveling away from your tax home"*** | ✅ **Decision 64: *"todas las comidas son comidas trabajando. No hay nada con clientes"*** ⚠️ **which establishes there is no entertainment; it does not by itself establish away-from-home travel** — **but a 14,810 `Travel` caption sits beside the 13,714 of meals on the same statement** |

### ② 💵 WHAT IT MOVES, AND EVERY LINE IT TOUCHES

| Line | At 50% *(as keyed)* | At 80% | Movement |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Line 20 statement 1b** meals subject to limit | **13,714** | **13,714** | — |
| **1e** less disallowed | **6,857** | **2,743** | |
| **1f** total meals and entertainment | **6,857** | **10,971** | |

the OWNER-DRIVER's road meals, 80% is clean. If part of it is drivers' meals the company paid, that part
