## 3BB · 🔑 THE SCHEDULE L IMBALANCE DECOMPOSES EXACTLY — and the DISTRIBUTIONS fall out at 85,243

🛑 **THIS IS THE ROUTE BOTH PRINCIPALS DIRECTED, ARRIVING:** *"key the assets and the liabilities and let the
distributions fall out"* — ⛔ **never *"everything that left, less identified business uses"***, which on this
account would have swept most of a million dollars into the capital account *(decision 22, §3U ④)*.

### ① 🔑 THE ARITHMETIC, WITH NOTHING LEFT OVER

| | |
|---|---|
| **Total assets, line 15 (d)** | **229,041** |
| **− identified liabilities** *(36,921 + 469,568 + 130,500)* | **(636,989)** |
| ⇒ 🔑 **REQUIRED CLOSING EQUITY** | **(407,948)** |
| **Equity as keyed** *(0 capital stock + 0 paid-in + (262,536) retained)* | **(262,536)** |
| ⇒ 🔴 **THE GAP** | **(145,412)** |

**AND IT SPLITS THREE WAYS, TO THE DOLLAR:**

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| **+ Capital stock, omitted from the closing column** | **1,000** | 📄 **the 2024 filed return — §3AE ③** |
| **− Additional paid-in capital, omitted from the closing column** | **(61,169)** | 📄 **the 2024 filed return; nothing in 2025 removed it** |
| **− DISTRIBUTIONS, the residual** | 🔑 **(85,243)** | **⚠️ COMPUTED, NOT OBSERVED — ② below** |
| ⇒ **net movement** | **(145,412)** | ✅ **exactly the gap** |

⇒ ✅ **`1,000 − 61,169 − 262,536 − 85,243 = −407,948`.**

### ② 🛑 WHAT THIS 85,243 IS AND IS NOT — and it is NOT a plug

⛔ **§3AQ ③'s own words: the equity residual is *"PROVISIONAL — and NEVER a plug."*** 🔑 **What has changed is
that BOTH unknowns it named are now numbers:** ✅ **`10b` = 560,082** *(§3AY ②)* **and the closing balance
sheet is fully keyed.** ⇒ **So 85,243 is the arithmetic consequence of documented figures on both sides, not a
figure chosen to make the statement balance.**

🛑 **BUT IT MUST STILL BE VERIFIED AGAINST THE BANK BEFORE IT IS REPORTED AS DISTRIBUTIONS**, for one reason
that is specific to this client: ⚠️ **an owner withdrawal and an owner LOAN look identical in a residual**, and
**Schedule L line 19 *(loans from shareholders)* is blank in both columns.** ☑️ **And §3AQ ③ names an
18,000 inbound Porsche wire, so money moved BOTH ways** — 🔑 **which is exactly the case Julia's §5C-v netting
policy governs** *([`form-1120s-preparation.md`](../../sops/form-1120s-preparation.md) §5C-v)*: **where
contributions exceed distributions the two are netted and distributions reported at zero.** ⛔ **Here the
residual runs the other way, so netting does not apply** — **but the GROSS SPLIT is still mandatory as
analysis, because the policy's own test cannot be applied to a net figure.**

### ③ ⚠️ HOW SENSITIVE IT IS — and the rule is short enough to remember

| A change to… | Does the 85,243 move? |
|---|---|
| **The 2025 depreciation charge** *(§3AX rows 1, 2, 7)* | ⛔ **NO.** **10b rises by X and the book loss rises by X — both sides fall together** |
| **Removing `#7107`** *(row 3)* | ⛔ **NO.** **10a and 10b fall by the same 78,145** |
| 🔴 🆕 **THE MEALS RATE going 50% → 80%** | ⛔ **NO — AND AN EARLIER VERSION OF THIS TABLE HAD IT IN THE ROW BELOW, WITH THE DIRECTION WRONG.** 🔑 **80% changes the TAX disallowance, not book income: M-1 line 1 stays −262,536 either way, because line 3b falls from 6,857 to 2,743 by exactly the amount line 22 falls** *(`−264,293 + 4,500 − 2,743 = −262,536`, identical to `−260,179 + 4,500 − 6,857`)*. **Assets are unchanged too** ⇒ **the residual does not move at all** |
| **PRIOR-YEAR accumulated depreciation** *(row 6, the 15,238)* | ✅ **YES, one for one and opposite.** **Opening equity is FIXED by the filed 2024 return, so assets rising 15,238 pushes the residual DOWN to 70,005** |
| **REAL 2025 income** — the 4,000 trailer and the ≈20,000 casualty | ✅ **YES, one for one.** **The proceeds are already inside the documented closing bank balance, so extra income raises equity without raising assets** ⇒ **the residual RISES** |
| 🟡 🆕 **THE IPFS YEAR-END BALANCE** *(§3AQ ③'s remaining unknown, and this table omitted it)* | ✅ **YES, one for one.** **It adds to Schedule L line 20, so liabilities rise and the equity the balance sheet requires falls** ⇒ **the residual RISES** |

⇒ ☑️ **SO THE ORDER OF WORK IS FIXED: settle rows 1–7, THEN read the residual, THEN test it against the bank.**
⛔ **Keying 85,243 today would be keying a figure that THREE pending items will move** ⚠️ 🆕 *(an earlier version said four and counted the meals rate, which moves nothing — the three are row 6's 15,238, the real new income, and IPFS)*.

### ④ 🛠️ WHERE IT GOES ONCE IT IS SETTLED — and the 2024 return shows the shape

🔑 **§3AE ① prints the 2025 AAA opening at ZERO, and 2025 is a LOSS year, so the AAA carries NOTHING.**
⇒ **M-2 line 7 can only be 0** *(line 8 cannot go below zero from a distribution)*, **while
Schedule K line 16d and K-1 box 16d must report the FULL distribution anyway.** ✅ **That is exactly what the
2024 return did — K 16d 113,157 against M-2 line 7 of 51,988** *(§3AE ②)*.

🛑 **AND §3AE ④'s QUESTION FOR JULIA ARRIVES WITH IT, WITH LESS ROOM THAN LAST YEAR:** ⚠️ **driving additional
paid-in capital further negative is not the ordinary treatment** — **a distribution in excess of AAA and of
stock basis is normally a capital gain on the shareholder's own return.** ⛔ **2024 parked 61,169 there;
2025 would park another 85,243 on top.** ☑️ **Presentation is the signer's call, and both returns must carry
the same one** *(§8C)*.

Julia)*, **the home office** *(decision 2 — Julia)*, **the bonus election** *(§3AZ — Lilian's own decision 3)*,
**the `1838` recovery-period route** *(§3AO ⑤c — Julia)*, **the Mitsubishi 0.89 rule collision** *(Lilian)*,
**and the paid-in-capital presentation** *(§3AE ④ — Julia)*. 🔑 **Six decisions, none of them a calculation,
and §3AX row 5's residual waits on THREE of the calculations rather than on any of them.**

## 3BD · 🔴 THE INSURANCE-CREDIT SEARCH — RUN 2026-09-28, AND IT FOUND THE MONEY

> **Lilian, 2026-09-28:** *"corre la búsqueda del seguro y lee las fechas de adquisición."*
> **Her own proposal, 2026-09-27:** *"pudiéramos buscar en los statements del banco si hay un monto parecido
> que entre a la cuenta… debe estar en algún sitio porque ese dinero se le debe haber ingresado al negocio."*
> ✅ **She was right, and it was there.**

🛑 **§3AU ② HAD SAID IN TERMS THAT THIS HAD NOT BEEN RUN. It has now.** 📄 **All twelve 2025 Fifth Third
statements re-read 2026-09-28 through [`tools/redact-doc/`](../../../tools/redact-doc/), all twelve exit 0,
and the twelve extracts DELETED when the reading was done.**

### ① 🛑 THE METHOD — and why the search §3AU ② PRESCRIBED would have MISSED IT

⛔ **§3AU ② said to look for an INSURANCE-shaped credit. That search returns nothing.** 🔑 **The settlement
is captioned `OWL LAND OF ILLI ACH Pmt` — the company's own freight customer — so a caption filter on
insurer names finds it no better than the lender-and-payroll filter that missed it the first time.**

✅ **What worked is STRUCTURAL, not lexical: parse the `Deposits / Credits` block of every statement and
list EVERY credit, then read them.** **168 credit lines across the twelve months; 104 of them at 5,000 or
more.** ⇒ 🔑 **THE RULE THIS PAYS FOR, and it is method rule 1b's twin: when a caption search comes back
empty, the next step is to DROP THE CAPTION, not to widen the word list.**

### ② 🔴 THE SETTLEMENT — one credit, and every detail of it

| | |
|---|---|
| **Date** | **9 April 2025** |
| **Amount** | 🔑 **57,500.00 — ONE credit, not two** |
| **Payer, as the bank prints it** | 🔴 **`OWL LAND OF ILLI`** — the company's **customer**, not an insurer |
| **The bank's own caption, verbatim** | 🔑 **`2 Checks for truck incident`** |
| **Form** | `ACH Pmt`, the same instrument as every other OWL LAND freight payment |

### ③ ☑️ WHAT THAT SETTLES — three of §3AU ②'s four branches DIE

| §3AU ②'s branch | Verdict |
|---|---|
| **one credit of ≈57,500 ⇒ the split is his to allocate, and it is one question** | ✅ **THIS ONE. Q1 SURVIVES and must still be asked** |
| **≈20,000 and ≈34,000 separately ⇒ the bank splits it, NO question needed** | ⛔ **DEAD — it is a single credit.** *(the best outcome did not happen)* |
| **≈20,000 only ⇒ the towing never came in** | ⛔ **DEAD** |
| **nothing in 2025 ⇒ the casualty is a 2026 item and comes off this return** | ⛔ **DEAD.** ✅ **THE CASUALTY IS A 2025 ITEM AND BELONGS ON THIS RETURN** |

🔑 **AND ONE THING THE CAPTION ADDS THAT NOBODY HAD: `2 Checks`.** ⇒ **The 57,500 is the sum of TWO
instruments, which corroborates the client's own two-part story** *(≈20,000 for the vehicle, ≈34,000 for the
towing)* **and means the two amounts EXIST on paper.** ☑️ **So Q1 gets sharper and easier to answer:
⛔ not *"how does the 57,500 break down?"* but ✅ *"what were the two check amounts?"* — a question with a
document behind it.**

### ④ 🔴 AND THE THING NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR: IT DID NOT COME FROM AN INSURER

⛔ **Every surface of this paper calls it the insurance, the insurer settled, the insurance settlement
letter.** 🔑 **On the bank it is a payment from `OWL LAND OF ILLINOIS INC.` — the customer whose freight
payments make up most of the year's revenue.**

☑️ **Two readings, and NEITHER is established:**

| | |
|---|---|
| **OWL LAND's own insurer paid, and OWL LAND passed it through** | **Plausible — the truck was destroyed working their freight** |
| **OWL LAND paid it themselves**, as compensation | **Equally plausible on this evidence** |

⚠️ **IT MATTERS AND IT IS NOT COSMETIC.** **Compensation for destroyed property is an involuntary conversion
either way** — ⛔ **but a payment from a CUSTOMER is not an insurance recovery on the company's own policy,
and the question of whether any part of it is simply FREIGHT INCOME cannot be answered from a bank caption.**
🔑 **This is a question for the CLIENT, and it joins Q1 rather than replacing it.**

### ⑤ ✅ TWO FREE CORROBORATIONS THE SAME SWEEP HANDED OVER

1. 🔑 **THE AUDI'S 19,500 IS ON THE BANK: `08/21 19,500.00 DEPOSIT`.** ✅ **Decision 65's date is no longer
   bare — the DEPOSIT is documented to 21 August 2025.** ⚠️ **It still does not document the SALE date** — a
   deposit can lag a sale — **so the disposal date stays an assumption, now anchored to a documented deposit
   instead of to a recollection.**
2. ⛔ **AND ONE INFERENCE THAT LOOKED PERFECT AND IS WRONG — RECORDED SO NOBODY RE-DERIVES IT.**
   **`06/26 10,000.00 OWL LAND OF ILLI ACH Pmt … For Truck 1834 and Trailer 1011`.** 🔑 **`1834` is
   `FRT'18(HU4577)`, sold 6/1/2025 for 6,000, and the `2015 Utility TRL` sold the same day for 4,000 —
   and 6,000 + 4,000 = 10,000 EXACTLY**, so it reads as the proceeds of both.
   🛑 **IT IS NOT. `Trailer 1011` is the `2023 Utility Trailer`** — Financial Pacific contract
   `001-1733069-302`, **still running to 11/15/2027 with 37,433.27 outstanding** *(§3P, line 928,
   `trl_1011_FinPac.pdf`)* — **an asset the return correctly KEEPS** *(§3AV)*. ⇒ **The credit is an OWL LAND
   freight settlement captioned by the tractor and trailer that pulled the load, which is how a broker labels
   one.** ⚠️ **The arithmetic is a COINCIDENCE, and it is written down because it is a good one.**

### ⑥ 🟡 WHAT THE SWEEP DID **NOT** SETTLE — and the precise next read

⛔ **The 34,000 is NOT a new finding: §3AG ⑥ / §3T ⑤ already established that the bank debited 34,000.00 on
11 April 2025 as an `AMEX EPAYMENT`, one day after the card posted a 34,000 payment.**
🔑 **What IS new is the JUXTAPOSITION: 57,500 in on 9 April, 34,000 out to Amex on 11 April — two days
apart.** ⚠️ **And that contradicts what the client told us, which is that the towing was *"paid in cash from
the business line of credit"*** *(§3AQ ②)* — **the money moved from the operating account to a card.**

🛑 **BUT A CARD PAYMENT IS NOT A CHARGE, AND THE DATES DO NOT HAVE TO MATCH.** **A 34,000 payment in April
pays down a balance that may have been run up months earlier** — **and §3C dates the truck's loss to
2025-01-01** *(⚠️ a date §3AE-era review already flags as disputed within this file)*. ⇒ ⛔ **Do NOT conclude
the towing is on the April cycle.**

☑️ **THE NEXT IN-HOUSE READ, stated so it can be run without re-deriving any of this: the firm holds
THIRTEEN itemised Amex statements** *(§3T)*, **and §3S ④ read them PAYMENT by payment. Nobody has read them
CHARGE by charge.** 🔑 **Read the charge side of the cycles from the loss date through April 2025 for a
towing or recovery vendor at ≈34,000.** ✅ **If it is there, the towing is inside the expense base by
decision 57 and the recovery is income or an offset — §3AQ ②'s first branch, and the largest open figure on
this return closes without asking the client anything.**

## 3BE · 🔑 THE ACQUISITION DATES — read off the four documents the firm holds, and ONE OF THEM IS NOT WHAT THE PAPER SAYS IT IS

> 📄 **The 2025 Form 4562 instructions: 100% bonus needs property *"acquired AND placed in service after
> January 19, 2025"*; acquired before 20 January, the rate is 40%** *(§3AZ)*. ⇒ **The acquisition date is
> what decides the rate, and §3AX row 7 sent this job in-house.**

📄 **All four read 2026-09-28 through the redactor; the `1840` also RENDERED under §3R ⑥'s hand rule, because
its `DATE` field is printed on the form and its value did not extract. Both the extracts and the render were
DELETED when the reading was done.**

### ① ☑️ WHAT EACH DOCUMENT ACTUALLY IS, AND WHAT DATE IT CARRIES

|---|---|---|---|---|
| `2026 Reitnouer DropMiser` **70,540**, in service **3/5/2025** | AP Equipment Financing note **160562** | a **LOAN** note — 70,540.00 at 8.99%, 1,271.17/month | ✅ **February 25, 2025** | ✅ **100%** — and the loan **EQUALS the booked cost to the dollar**, so the whole cost was incurred on that date |
| `1839` **105,550**, in service **2/25/2025** | AP Equipment Financing note **160544** | a **LOAN** note — 95,550.00 at 8.99% | ✅ **February 25, 2025** | 🟡 **100%, but weaker — ② below** |
| `2022 Benson trailer` **43,650**, in service **8/1/2025** | Mitsubishi HC Capital **CFA `9827076-001`** | 🔑 **the CONTRACT itself** — *"dated as of July 01, 2025"*, guarantor signed **7/1/2025**, a *"PRIOR TO FUNDING"* certificate dated **7/01/2025** | ✅ **July 1, 2025** | ✅ **100% — the cleanest of the four** |
| `1840` **115,550**, in service **7/1/2025** | Southport Truck Group invoice, **stock 263894** | 🔴 **A BLANK, UNEXECUTED FORM — ③ below** | ⛔ **NONE — the form's `DATE` lines are empty** | ⛔ **NOT ESTABLISHED** |

### ② 🟡 WHY THE `1839` IS WEAKER THAN THE REITNOUER, ON THE SAME DATE

🔑 **Its note is for 95,550 against a booked cost of 105,550 — it funds 90.5% of the asset, not 100%.**
⇒ **10,000 was paid to the dealer some other way, and WHEN is not on this document.** ⚠️ **§3H ③bis already
recorded the other half of it: a note dated 2025-02-25 fits BOTH a 2025 purchase AND an earlier purchase
refinanced in 2025.** ☑️ **On balance it supports 100%** — the note is after 19 January and nothing points
the other way — **but it is an inference from a financing document, not a purchase contract.**
⛔ **And decision 60 does NOT close this: it ruled the `1839` is a 2025 ADDITION, which is the
IN-SERVICE question. The bonus rate turns on ACQUISITION, and those are different dates.**

🛑 **`1840 truck inv DE-13743.pdf` is a Southport Truck Group `Invoice` form carrying, printed:**
**`Stock#: 263894` · a VIN · `2023 Freightliner Cascadia 126` · `Price: $115,000.00` · `Dlr Prep Charge
$550.00` · `Total $115,550.00` · `Page 1 of 1`.**

⛔ **AND EVERYTHING ELSE ON IT IS EMPTY:**

|---|---|
| **Both party boxes at the top** *(seller and purchaser)* | **BLANK** |
| **The invoice-number lines, top right** | **BLANK** — four ruled lines, nothing on them |
| **`PURCHASER'S SIGNATURE`** · **`CO-PURCHASER'S SIGNATURE`** | **BLANK** |
| **`SALES REPRESENTATIVE`** · **`MANAGER'S SIGNATURE`** | **BLANK** |
| 🔴 **All four `DATE` lines** | 🔴 **BLANK** |

🔑 **AND THE FORM'S OWN TERMS SETTLE IT, verbatim:** *"THIS AGREEMENT SHALL NOT BECOME BINDING UNTIL SIGNED
AND ACCEPTED BY AN AUTHORIZED DEALERSHIP REPRESENTATIVE."*

⛔ **SO THREE CLAIMS IN THIS PAPER ARE TOO STRONG AND ARE CORRECTED HERE:**

1. ⛔ *"**the RETAIL PURCHASE AGREEMENT** for unit 1840"* *(§1 row 17)* — **it is an unexecuted invoice form.**
2. ⛔ *"**a seller's document** against the client's booked 115,550"* and *"**The acquisition document** §4
   decision 9 requires"* *(§3H ③)* — 🔑 **it names NO PURCHASER, so on its own it does not establish that

   CLIENT'S FILENAME — was right and does not go far enough.**
3. ⛔ **It cannot support a bonus rate**, because it carries no date of any kind.

✅ **WHAT SURVIVES, AND IT IS NOT NOTHING:** **the stock number, the VIN, the model and the
`115,000 + 550 = 115,550` are DEALER-PRINTED figures, and they match the client's booked cost exactly.**
⇒ ✅ **The COST is corroborated by a seller's document. The BUYER and the DATE are not.**

Withdrawn.)* **② The date CAN move one figure: depreciation in the year of disposal.** Under the **half-year convention** you take half a year regardless of the date, so it does not matter; under the **mid-quarter convention** *(which applies when more than 40% of the year's additions are placed in service in the fourth quarter — a live question here, given the size of 2025's additions)* the depreciation depends on **which QUARTER** the disposal falls in. ⛔ **③ An earlier draft said "the date of the loss starts the §1033 replacement clock". ## 8 · 🔗 What flows to ANOTHER return — the handoff

⛔ **Not prepared, and not to be prepared from this paper.** The shareholder's 1040 is a separate
request on a separate day *(the one-return-per-request rule)*.

### 8A · The tables to carry across
**Pending** — the K-1 cannot be built until §6's first four items close.

### 8B · 🔴 What this side CANNOT supply
- **The shareholder's opening stock basis** — his 2024 **Form 7203 line 15**, filed with his 1040.
  ⚠️ **The firm may not hold it**; if not, it must be **reconstructed as separate scoped work.**
- **Whether 2024's 113,157 distribution exceeded his basis** and produced a capital gain he has
  already reported, or not.

### 8C · What must MATCH on both returns
- The **distribution** figure, and its presentation.
- Any **officer compensation** — his W-2 if one is issued.
- **The home-office treatment**: rent to the shareholder is **income on his 1040**.

### 8D · Before the other return is TRANSMITTED
- This return **filed and accepted**, and every §6 item closed.
